# Divergence ledger

Every deliberate deviation from the Xash3D-FWGS tree is recorded here **the
same day it is written**. Without this, the third merge with upstream turns
into archaeology over why a given line differs, and the fork gets abandoned.

Fork base: `c7768c48` (2026-08-08).

## Format

One entry per divergence, with:

- **What**: the file(s) and function(s) touched.
- **Why**: the concrete reason, in one sentence.
- **Form**: `#ifdef` / new file / replacement / removal.
- **Merge risk**: high if it touches code upstream changes often.
- **Exit**: what it would take to remove the divergence (sometimes "none, it
  is permanent").

Preferred forms, from cheapest to most expensive to maintain:

1. **New file in one of our directories** — zero merge cost.
2. **New file inside an upstream directory** (e.g. a `filesystem/mpak.c` next
   to `pak.c`) — near-zero cost, just the `wscript`.
3. **`#ifdef XASH_XBOX` around upstream code** — low cost, but it pollutes
   diffs.
4. **Rewriting an upstream function** — high cost. Requires explicit
   justification here.
5. **Changing behaviour with no `#ifdef`** — forbidden unless it is a genuine
   upstream bug, in which case the patch goes upstream first.

## Active divergences

| # | What | Why | Form | Merge risk | Exit |
|---|------|-----|------|------------|------|
| 99 | `ref/nv2a` adds native alpha test for masked (`{`-prefixed - fences, grates, ladders, vents) world textures (X9-I), now that the baker's atlas carries real per-pixel alpha (PR #123 - the same fix's own residual-defect follow-up commits closed a real regression the specialist found, then a real defect codex-review round 2 refused to let stand as "documented, not fixed"). `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()` now pushes `NV097_SET_ALPHA_TEST_ENABLE`/`_FUNC`/`_REF` (`deps/nxdk/lib/pbkit/nv_regs.h:223,245-254` - the same registers D3D8's `D3DRS_ALPHATESTENABLE`/`_ALPHAFUNC`/`_ALPHAREF` map to on this hardware; NV2A's fp20 has no per-pixel conditional discard of its own - `ps_lit.inl` compiles to register-combiner STATE, not an instruction stream, confirmed by reading its actual compiled output) once per call, gated by a new `nv2a_r_noalphatest` convar. **A real, checked reference-fidelity divergence, not a straight port**: `ref/gl` and `ref/soft` disagree with each other on the mechanism, and this slice matches neither verbatim. `ref/gl` applies NO alpha test to a masked WORLD-brush surface outside `ENGINE_QUAKE_COMPATIBLE` (`gl_rsurf.c:1561`'s own gating, `gl_rmain.c:832`'s own "quake-specific feature" comment on the pass that condition guards) - retail HL's real masked world brushes only get cutout in `ref/gl` when tied to a separate entity with `rendermode kRenderTransAlpha` (`gl_rsurf.c:1739/1752`, `GL_GREATER 0.25`), a real GoldSrc mapping convention (func_wall + Render Mode "Solid"), not a gap in this fork. `ref/soft` instead does unconditional per-texel masking with no entity/rendermode gate: a hardcoded sentinel palette index (`TRANSPARENT_COLOR`, `r_local.h:588`) baked in at decode time for any `{`-prefixed texture and skipped during span rasterization (`r_scan.c`, six call sites), independent of `SURF_TRANSPARENT`, quake-compat, or entity ownership. X9 has no entity render list at all yet - only the static baked world model draws - so porting `ref/gl`'s entity/rendermode machinery just for this one feature would be a far larger change than the feature itself; this slice matches `ref/soft`'s simpler, unconditional, `SURF_TRANSPARENT`-gated-per-face mechanism instead (found in the same research pass as the reference disagreement, via `reference-renderer-specialist`, before writing any code). **A design decision made possible by verification this fork already did, not new to this slice**: NV2A alpha test is a GLOBAL per-draw-call toggle, not per-triangle, which would normally force bucketing masked-vs-opaque faces into contiguous index runs (mirroring X9-F's frustum-cull run-merging) to avoid risking ordinary opaque geometry. That bucketing turns out to be unnecessary: PR #123's own `blockNeedsTransparency` gate in the baker's DXT1 encoder only ever lets a BC1 block's transparent selector get assigned when that SPECIFIC block contains a real source-alpha<128 texel, and PR #123's own follow-up commits verified this exhaustively (not just by reading the code) by decoding and comparing every texel of every successfully-baked map in the full 125-map retail corpus against its pre-compression source (121 of 125 - the other 4 fail to bake for an unrelated, already-tracked reason - 0 opaque-source-but-transparent-decoded mismatches on any of the 121, `tools/verify-atlas-alpha.py`). So a single global alpha-test enable, applied to every face regardless of masked status, cannot punch a hole in ordinary opaque geometry on any of the retail content this baker has actually produced - an empirical result over known content (the encoder's own fix-or-no-op design, `TextureOpt.cpp`'s own comment: "A block that fails verification keeps whatever `compressToDXT` already produced" - not a structural proof that would hold for arbitrary future content unchecked by that same tool). The final combiner alpha the test stage compares against is the diffuse atlas's own real, filtered alpha alone (`ps_lit.ps.cg`, `main()`'s own return value). It was not that simple in the first version shipped for review: the shader computed color and alpha as one shared `diffuseMap * lightMap * 2.0f` expression, so alpha got the same `NV097_SET_COMBINER_ALPHA_OCW_OP_SHIFTLEFTBY1` doubling the `× 2` lightmap-brightness convention needs for color. `nv2a-xbox-specialist` review found two real comment problems in that version (fixed): the state-push comments overclaimed a plain alpha pass-through (missing the doubling entirely) and overclaimed the atlas invariant below as structurally "verified, not assumed" rather than an empirical corpus result. Then `tools/codex-review.sh` round 1 found the real bug those comments had been (wrongly) reasoning around: the diffuse atlas samples through genuine bilinear filtering (`NV097_SET_TEXTURE_FILTER`), so a fragment at a masked cutout's own edge gets a real, non-binary alpha by design - that's what makes a filtered alpha test antialias a cutout edge instead of aliasing it - and comparing that DOUBLED value against `NV097_SET_ALPHA_REF=0x80` silently moved the real pass/fail boundary to 25% opacity, not the intended 50%. Fixed at the actual source, not by picking a compensating reference constant: `ps_lit.ps.cg` no longer computes color and alpha as one expression - RGB keeps its own `×2`, alpha passes through the diffuse texture's own real value untouched - recompiled through the real `cgc -profile fp20`/`fp20compiler` toolchain (X9-C's own "compile, don't hand-derive" precedent) and confirmed in the actual compiled combiner output, not assumed (`out.a = unsigned(tex0.a)`, no `scale_by_two()` on that channel, replacing a doubled two-texture alpha product). `nv2a_draw_test_triangle()` (the map-not-ready/mid-transition fallback `R_EndFrame` genuinely exercises, `pb_reset()` being a push-buffer-pointer reset, not a GPU-register reset) never touched the alpha-test registers at all either, so it could inherit stale `ALPHA_TEST_ENABLE=1` state from a prior map-draw call once the convar default is ever flipped - inert today only because its own checkerboard texture is fully opaque, fixed (specialist round) with an explicit `ALPHA_TEST_ENABLE=0` push there too rather than relying on that coincidence. | XB's own atlas alpha fix (PR #123) removed the last blocker `fork-plan.md`'s own X9-I bullet list had named for this feature - masked textures previously baked with zero real transparency regardless of what the renderer did with them. This is the natural next slice XB unblocked, not a redesign | Form 1 (new code entirely inside `ref/nv2a/r_context.c` - a new convar, three new `pb_push1()` calls in `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()`'s existing texture-stage setup block, one new defensive push in `nv2a_draw_test_triangle()`; no upstream file touched, no baker/format change needed - the atlas alpha itself was already fixed in a prior PR) | Low - dead code unless a map's own `.mpak` has `MPAK_FLAG_NV2A_BLOCK` set and `r_refdll` is `"nv2a"`, and even then the convar ships disabled by default (see Exit). Every ordinary map and every other renderer is completely unaffected | **Booted live, 2026-09-22 - real, but codex-review correctly rejected calling this "A/B done."** Same real disc/boot infrastructure #98's own entry describes (first real live boot this session, one genuine previously-undocumented bug found and fixed, one suspected bug corrected/retracted - see `lessons.md`'s new entry). With `nv2a_r_noalphatest` temporarily flipped to `0` (enabled) in an uncommitted local rebuild and booted at the same real, live camera used across both boots (`GL_RenderFrame`'s own per-frame capture from `rvp->vieworigin`/`viewangles`, X9-E/#95 - NOT the fixed X9-B/D camera an earlier draft of this entry wrongly named, corrected per codex-review): **0 of 307,200 pixels differ, max channel delta 0**, against the already-shipped disabled-by-default baseline - no crash, no visible corruption, matching what the encoder-invariant reasoning above predicted - but, per codex-review's own real finding against this entry's first draft, a pixel-identical result CANNOT by itself distinguish "the alpha-test stage genuinely ran and correctly found nothing to cut" from "the feature never executed or never had anything to affect" - unlike #98's own backface-culling A/B (see that entry), this feature has no equivalent live counter this session could read to prove the code path did real work: `NV097_SET_ALPHA_TEST_ENABLE`/`_FUNC`/`_REF` are one-way GPU register writes with no software-side counter tracking how many fragments they actually discarded, and confirming a masked texture was genuinely IN FRAME would need either camera control this session doesn't have (no gamepad/input automation set up) or a new, temporary instrumentation pass not attempted here. So this boot is real (no crash, no visible corruption, the whole X9-B-through-I pipeline demonstrably running together) but does NOT constitute the A/B this convar's default flip would need - "ran without incident" and "confirmed correct cutout" are different claims, and only the first is made here. Default left at `"1"` (disabled) for the same reason #98's entry gives, and for the additional reason that even a strong A/B like #98's own hasn't been judged sufficient there to flip a shipped default within a docs-only change. The full static verification chain remains clean as previously recorded. Skinning and studio models, dynamic lighting, the frame-time exit criterion itself, and the full `ref_soft`-vs-`ref_nv2a` photometric comparison (#94) all carry forward unchanged. |
| 98 | `ref/nv2a` adds backface culling (X9-H) to its existing per-face frustum (X9-F, #96) and PVS (X9-G, #97) cull tests. `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()` now fetches the engine's own already-loaded world model (`gp_cl->models[1]`, guarded to `world == nv2a_map_owner`, NULL/fail-open otherwise - the exact pattern X9-G's `nv2a_mark_leaves()` already established for PVS) and, for each baked face, reads that SAME index into the engine's own `world->surfaces[i]` (reusing, not re-deriving, the "same face, same index" bridge X9-G already verified and bounds-checks - `i < world->numsurfaces` is checked again here, independently, since this reads `world` from a different call site than `nv2a_mark_leaves()` does) to get its real `plane`/`flags`. The test itself is the reference's own default: `dist = PlaneDiff(camera_eye, plane)`, negated if `SURF_PLANEBACK` is set, culled if `dist <= BACKFACE_EPSILON` - `ref/gl`'s own `R_CullSurface` (`gl_cull.c`), applied uniformly to every world surface because `R_SetupGL(true)` (`gl_rmain.c`, called from `R_RenderScene` - an earlier version of this entry named a nonexistent `GL_SetupGL`, corrected by `reference-renderer-specialist` review) sets `GL_Cull(GL_FRONT)` once per frame before the world draw runs and nothing in `gl_rsurf.c` overrides it for world surfaces (confirmed by grepping every `GL_Cull` call site, not assumed - sky surfaces included, since `R_RecursiveWorldNode`'s own `SURF_DRAWSKY` special-case runs AFTER `R_CullSurface`, not instead of it - and confirmed true of the REFERENCE ITSELF, not merely "doesn't matter for this port's own narrower scope yet": `R_CullSurface` is called identically for opaque, `SURF_DRAWTURB` and `SURF_TRANSPARENT` surfaces alike inside `R_RecursiveWorldNode`; the type-based split into separate wateralpha/alpha-surface draw passes happens strictly LATER, in `R_DrawTextureChains`, on whatever already survived this same cull test - there is no surface-type exemption from backface culling anywhere in the reference's own cull decision). One disclosed simplification, also found in review: the reference's real input is `tr.modelorg` (`RI.cullorigin`, set from `rvp.vieworigin` unless `r_lockfrustum` is active), not raw `vieworigin` directly - this port's `nv2a_eye_origin` is captured straight from `rvp->vieworigin`, which is EQUAL to `tr.modelorg` for this fork's own real single-player path (no mirror/portal pass exists here to reassign `cullorigin` differently, and `r_lockfrustum` defaults off) - an equivalence for the case that actually runs, not an identity, worth re-checking if a lockfrustum-equivalent or a mirror/portal pass is ever added to this module. A new camera-eye-position static, `nv2a_eye_origin`, is captured once per real frame in `GL_RenderFrame` (alongside the existing `nv2a_map_viewproj`/`nv2a_frustum`), since `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()` (called later the same frame, from `R_EndFrame`) has no `ref_viewpass_t` of its own to read `vieworigin` from - unlike PVS, which only needs recomputing on a leaf crossing, backface visibility depends on camera orientation and must be re-evaluated every single frame. `BACKFACE_EPSILON` and `PlaneDiff()` are used directly, already in scope via existing includes (no new mirroring needed for either); only `SURF_PLANEBACK`'s literal value (`common/bspfile.h`'s own `BIT(1)` = 0x0002) is mirrored locally as `NV2A_SURF_PLANEBACK`, matching the same "private, narrowly-scoped constant" precedent `NV2A_MAX_MAP_LEAFS`/`NV2A_CHECKVISBIT` already established in #97 for a module this isolated (`tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh`) that has no cheap way to pull in the engine-internal headers those real values live in. A new `nv2a_r_nocullback` convar (this module's own dev-aid, no reference analog by that exact name - `ref/gl`'s own backface culling is controlled by an internal `glState.faceCull` state variable, not a user-facing convar) matches the `nv2a_r_nocull`/`nv2a_r_novis` A/B-toggle pattern already established for the other two cull tests. | Frustum and PVS culling still submitted a face pointing directly away from the camera as long as it was inside the view volume and its own leaf was potentially visible - a wall's own back side, facing into the wall, away from a player standing right in front of it, was drawn every frame regardless. This is the cheap, self-contained item `fork-plan.md`'s own X9-H bullet list already carried forward since X9-F named it, and the natural next slice after PVS: no new baker work, no new format, reusing an already-verified index bridge | Form 1 (new code entirely inside `ref/nv2a/r_context.c` - a new local in `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()`, a new file-scope static (`nv2a_eye_origin`) and convar (`nv2a_r_nocullback`), one new mirrored constant, and a captured-eye-position write in `GL_RenderFrame`; no upstream file touched), same rung #96/#97 used | Low - dead code unless a map's own `.mpak` has `MPAK_FLAG_NV2A_BLOCK` set and `r_refdll` is `"nv2a"`; every ordinary map and every other renderer is completely unaffected. `world == NULL` (no map owner match) or `i >= world->numsurfaces` (a face-count mismatch, the same failure #97's own parity check already watches for) both fail open to "no backface test this call", never a black screen or missing geometry. Coupling surface: `msurface_t::plane`/`::flags` and `mplane_t`'s own fields (via `PlaneDiff`) - NOT new exposure, exactly what #97 already coupled this module to; this entry adds no struct/field this fork wasn't already reading | **Live-boot A/B done, 2026-09-22 - real evidence, still not enough to flip the default.** Built a real bootable `hl.iso` (real client+server+freevgui, `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh --xbe` + `tools/xbox-xiso.sh`), a real baked `crossfire.mpak` (`tools/asset-studio --target xbox --profile nv2a`), booted it live in xemu (`xemu.exe -s`, `tools/xemu-debug.py`'s GDB-remote bridge) - the first real live boot of a real disc this SESSION, closing a real, previously-undocumented bug that silently blocked it (see `lessons.md`'s new entry: an `.mpak` file must sit at the game directory's own root, not inside `maps/`, or filesystem archive discovery never enumerates it at all). Confirmed live: `nv2a_map_ready`/`nv2a_view_valid` both `1`, `debug_frame_count` genuinely advancing, and a real screenshot showing actual baked polygon geometry (dim, matching #94's own already-documented lighting finding for this camera) - `ref_nv2a`'s whole pipeline through X9-I is demonstrably running on real emulated hardware, not just linking cleanly. With `nv2a_r_nocullback` temporarily flipped to `0` (enabled) in an uncommitted local rebuild (same technique #96 used for its own `nv2a_r_nocull` A/B) and re-booted at the same real camera: **0 of 307,200 pixels differ, max channel delta 0**, against the already-shipped disabled-by-default baseline. Codex review, real finding: a pixel-identical result on its own cannot distinguish "the test ran and correctly agreed with the GPU's own always-on backface cull" from "the test never actually culled anything, a no-op" - so this was checked directly, not left as an assumption. The module's own existing per-frame stats globals (`nv2a_cull_faces_total`/`_visible`/`_backface_only`, X9-H's own instrumentation) were read live via `tools/xemu-debug.py mem`: `nv2a_cull_faces_total=4385` (exactly `crossfire.mpak`'s own real face count, matching #96's own independently-verified live figure), `nv2a_cull_faces_visible=299`, and **`nv2a_cull_faces_backface_only=68`** - a real, nonzero count of faces the backface test itself specifically rejected this frame, at this exact camera, proving the code path is genuinely active and doing real work, not dead weight the screenshot comparison happened to not exercise. Combined, the two results say something stronger than either alone: 68 real faces got backface-culled, and the resulting image was still pixel-identical to the uncculled baseline - direct evidence those 68 faces were already correctly invisible (behind other opaque geometry or otherwise not contributing), exactly the CPU/GPU cull-agreement property this convar's own cautious default was written to protect against being wrong about. Honest about what remains unconfirmed: **corrected from this entry's own first draft (codex-review, real finding)** - the camera both boots used is NOT a fixed one computed from the map's own vertices (that was X9-B/D's own camera, replaced in X9-E - divergence #95 - by a real, live camera `GL_RenderFrame` captures every frame from `rvp->vieworigin`/`viewangles`, unchanged since). Both boots used this same real, live camera at whatever position the engine's own boot sequence placed the player - most likely `crossfire`'s own real spawn point, since nothing in this session's own disc/config touched player position - but unlike X9-E's own entry, this session did not independently capture the exact `vieworigin`/`viewangles` values live to confirm that identity. Whatever real position it was, it was never confirmed to actually frame a back-facing wall, so a real face-appearing/disappearing case specifically was not observed either way - though the real, nonzero `_backface_only=68` count above means 68 real faces DID get the specific treatment that case would need (culled, then confirmed not to change the image), just not a face independently confirmed to be genuinely back-facing rather than already hidden some other way. This is meaningfully stronger evidence than a bare screenshot diff - real, exercised work with a real, matching result, on the same evidentiary bar #96's own `nv2a_r_nocull` A/B used to flip its default - but flipping THIS default is left to a following, smaller change specifically about that decision, not folded into this docs-only PR. Default left at `"1"` (disabled) here. Native alpha test (now live-boot-checked too, see #99), VP20 skinning and studio models, dynamic lighting, the frame-time exit criterion itself, and the full `ref_soft`-vs-`ref_nv2a` photometric comparison (#94) all carry forward unchanged. |
| 97 | `ref/nv2a` adds real PVS (potentially-visible-set) culling (X9-G), on top of X9-F's already-merged frustum culling. A new `nv2a_mark_leaves()` folds a port of `ref/gl/gl_rmain.c`'s `R_FindViewLeaf` plus `ref/gl/gl_rsurf.c`'s `R_MarkLeaves` into one function (this module has no separate per-frame setup pass to host the first half in): finds the real leaf the live camera stands in via `gEngfuncs.Mod_PointInLeaf`, including the reference's own crossing-line-contents merge for a camera sitting on a leaf boundary; fetches the real PVS row via `gEngfuncs.R_FatPVS`; and marks every face any PVS-visible leaf's own `firstmarksurface`/`nummarksurfaces` references into a new per-face `byte *nv2a_map_face_visible` table. The mechanism for that last step is NOT a literal port of `R_MarkLeaves` itself (which only stamps `node->visframe` on leaf ancestors for a later node-based descent to consume) - but it IS the same concrete step the reference's own normal, currently-active single-player path performs, just not inside `R_MarkLeaves`: `R_RecursiveWorldNode` (`gl_rsurf.c:3457-3496`), when its descent reaches an actual leaf, stamps `mark[i]->visframe = tr.framecount` for every one of that leaf's own `firstmarksurface` entries (`:3481-3489`) - literally "for a PVS-visible leaf, mark its own marksurfaces visible" - and its later node-based `firstsurface`/`numsurfaces` walk only keeps a surface whose visframe was stamped this way (`R_CullSurface`, `gl_cull.c:70`, rejecting anything else). The node walk is ref/gl's own draw-chain-building iteration order; the leaf-marksurface stamp is what actually decides per-surface visibility, and this module computes that same decision directly, with no node hierarchy needed on top of it (X9-F's `nv2a_map_faces` is a flat per-face AABB table, not a node tree, so there is no draw-order to replicate, only the visibility decision) - verified by reading `R_RecursiveWorldNode` and `R_CullSurface` in full, not assumed from a plausible-sounding shape (an earlier version of this entry wrongly credited this mechanism to the disused, overview-mode-only `R_DrawTopViewLeaf` instead - corrected here, Codex review). `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()`'s existing streaming cull-and-merge pass (X9-F, unchanged in shape) now skips a face if it fails EITHER the frustum test or the PVS test. Two new module-local convars mirror the reference's own toggles for this exact mechanism: `nv2a_r_novis` (mirrors `r_novis`, including porting the reference's `FCVAR_CHANGED` handling so toggling forces an immediate recompute) and `nv2a_r_lockpvs` (mirrors `r_lockpvs`, a dev aid freezing the table for inspection). Needed no baker or format changes at all: the v7 "classic core" lumps (`docs/r3d/mpak-loading-design.md`) already make a map archive a real, valid BSP30 file the engine loads through its own unmodified `Mod_LoadBmodelLumps` path, so this renderer reaches the engine's already-loaded world model (`gp_cl->models[1]`) the same way `ref/gl`/`ref/soft` already do - `nv2a_map_faces[i]` (X9-F's own table) and the engine's `world->surfaces[i]` are the same face at the same index because both are built walking the archive's one real `LUMP_FACES` in write order, verified directly against the real, current `Mod_LoadSurfaces` (`engine/common/mod_bmodel.c`), not assumed - so a marksurface pointer's offset from `world->surfaces` is directly a valid `nv2a_map_faces` index. That offset is bounds-checked against `world->surfaces`/`world->numsurfaces` before use (never trusted raw), and a one-time-per-map `world->numsurfaces != nv2a_map_face_count` parity check logs a warning if the two loaders ever disagree on face count - a real but unenforced cross-subsystem invariant, not something a compiler or linker can catch. | X9-F's own frustum culling still submitted every face the camera's field of view could geometrically contain, including faces behind a wall a few feet away - PVS is what #91/#95/#96 all named as the concrete next slice, and the baked archive already carried the real, populated GoldSrc visibility/node/leaf/marksurface lumps needed for it (#96's own exit paragraph), so no baker work stood between this slice and being written | Form 1 (new code entirely inside `ref/nv2a/r_context.c`, one wholly new function plus small additive edits to four already-established functions this module already owns - `nv2a_draw_map_geometry`, `nv2a_free_map_resources`, `nv2a_load_map_geometry`, `R_Init`, `GL_RenderFrame`; no upstream file touched), same rung #96 used | Low - dead code unless a map's own `.mpak` has `MPAK_FLAG_NV2A_BLOCK` set and `r_refdll` is `"nv2a"`; every ordinary map and every other renderer is completely unaffected. A NULL `nv2a_map_face_visible` table (allocation failure, or `nv2a_mark_leaves()` never matching `WORLDMODEL` to `nv2a_map_owner`) fails open to frustum-only, never a black screen. New coupling surface worth naming: the first X9-nv2a slice to reach into the engine's LIVE BSP node/leaf tree rather than only the baked mpak geometry - `model_t`'s `nodes`/`leafs`/`numleafs`/`surfaces`/`numsurfaces`/`visdata`, `mleaf_t`'s `contents`/`firstmarksurface`/`nummarksurfaces`, and `ref_api_t`'s `Mod_PointInLeaf`/`R_FatPVS`, all reached through unmodified, shared headers (`common/com_model.h`, `engine/ref_api.h`) the same way `ref/gl`/`ref/soft` already do, so a future upstream refactor of those layouts would break this module at the same moment it broke the other two, not uniquely | **Not yet live-boot confirmed - see below.** Backface culling, VP20 skinning and studio models, dynamic lighting, native alpha test, the frame-time exit criterion itself, and the full `ref_soft`-vs-`ref_nv2a` photometric comparison (#94) all carry forward unchanged. |
| 96 | `ref/nv2a` frustum-culls its own draw list (X9-F) instead of submitting the whole map's index buffer every frame regardless of the real camera X9-E gave it. `nv2a_compute_mpak_layout()` now captures the `FaceDrawCmds` lump (previously stepped over into an unused `skip` local, per #91's own comment - a lump this format bakes one entry per BSP face, contiguous and gap-free by construction, confirmed against the real `crossfire.mpak`) into a new `nv2a_map_face_t[]` table (`vec3_t mins,maxs` plus `dword_begin`/`dword_end`) built once at map load: the AABB comes from the already-remapped vertex positions in `nv2a_map_vb` (the baked format itself carries no bounding volume - `MpakFaceInfo` has only `planenum`/texinfo/lightmap fields), and the index range is snapped OUTWARD to a multiple of 6 logical indices (= 3 packed dwords, since `nv2a_map_index_dwords` packs two indices per dword) so a face's own range is always addressable without ever shifting a triangle boundary in the shared buffer - `lcm(2,3)=6` is exactly why 6, not 2 or 3 alone. The tiling is validated (running-cursor check, non-zero multiple-of-3 counts, final cursor equals `iCount`) before being trusted; any failure leaves `nv2a_map_faces` NULL and the draw path degrades to submitting the whole buffer uncculled - the same behaviour every X9 slice before this one had, never a black screen. `GL_RenderFrame()` now also builds a real 5-plane view frustum (LEFT/RIGHT/BOTTOM/TOP/FAR, `mplane_t nv2a_frustum[5]`) from the SAME `forward`/`right`/`up`/`vieworigin` its view matrix already uses, a direct port of `ref/gl/gl_frustum.c`'s own `GL_FrustumInitProj` (inward normals, no near plane, matching the reference's own `flZNear=0.0f` from `R_SetupFrustum`). `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()`'s own batching loop is unchanged in shape (`nv2a_draw_index_range()`, factored out verbatim) but is now fed by a new single streaming pass: walk `nv2a_map_faces` in bake order, cull each against the frustum with `nv2a_frustum_cull_box()` (a literal port of `GL_FrustumCullBox`'s own signbits switch, minus the BSP-node clipFlags early-out this module has no node hierarchy to propagate), and merge contiguous visible faces' own dword ranges into runs before handing each run to the unchanged batching loop - no run array, two locals. This session's own X9 testing (X9-A through the #92 investigation) never used that flag combination - an oversight, not a deliberate scope choice - so no graphical/menu boot attempt this entire session could ever have succeeded, with EITHER renderer, regardless of anything X9 itself did or didn't do right | Rebuilt X9-D's own `crossfire.mpak` test disc with the correct, already-proven combination: `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh hl --include-client --include-freevgui --xbe .sweep/game-hl/hl.xbe` - a real, valid XBE (0 duplicate, 0 undefined, 6,709,248 bytes, server+client+freevgui all real together). Booted with the disc's own `r_refdll "nv2a"`: **live memory reads confirm, for the first time in this project's entire history, that `ref/nv2a` is genuinely drawing real baked map geometry, sustained, not just statically wired.** `debug_marker == 5` (`R_Init` fully succeeded), `nv2a_ready == 1`, and - the new result - `nv2a_map_ready == 1` (a real `.mpak` was parsed and accepted) with `debug_frame_count` read twice, 10,572 then 16,124 across two live reads seconds apart - a real, advancing, sustained render loop, not a frozen one. Narrowed to delete only the `valve` leaf - the one, real, compile-time gamedir name (`XASH_GAMEDIR`) this fork's own Half-Life-based build ever uses - since a second nxdk project coincidentally ALSO naming its own save directory "valve" is vanishingly unlikely; `WIPE_ROOT` is still a fixed string literal in the tool's own source, never built from a variable, so there is no path-construction bug that could point it anywhere else. **A second codex-review finding, also fixed**: `GetFileAttributesExA` failing for ANY reason (not just "path doesn't exist") was treated as "already clean, nothing to wipe" - a real I/O or access error would have silently masqueraded as a successful, trustworthy wipe, exactly the false-clean signal this tool exists to prevent elsewhere. Fixed by checking `GetLastError()`: only `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`/`ERROR_PATH_NOT_FOUND` are treated as "absent," every other error now reports a distinct `WIPE_ROOT_CHECK_FAILED` status with the real error code. **Codex review, round 2 (the final allowed round for this branch), one real finding, fixed.** Even the narrowed, `valve`-only scope from round 1 still recursively deleted the WHOLE directory - real, legitimate Resonance3D user data (`config.cfg`'s own archived cvars/bindings, real game saves) lived in that same tree and had no business being touched by a tool built to fix one specific stale file. Rewritten to delete exactly the two real, known filenames `Host_FinalizeConfig`'s own atomic-write sequence (`engine/common/con_utils.c:1372-1384`) can ever leave behind for `video.cfg` - the file itself, and `video.cfg.new` (its own interrupted-write staging file) - by name, with no directory walk at all; the recursive `WipeDirectoryRecursive` helper and its own `WIN32_FIND_DATA` enumeration are gone entirely, replaced by two direct `DeleteFileA` calls. `video.cfg.bak` (the third file that same sequence produces) is deliberately left alone - it is never read by anything, only ever written, so a stale copy cannot shadow anything and deleting it would only cost a backup for no benefit. Re-verified live after the rewrite: booting the new build on an `E:` where the prior wipe had already removed `video.cfg` correctly reported `WIPE_DONE` with 0 files deleted, 0 errors - the same clean, honest "nothing to do" result the file-not-found path is supposed to produce, confirmed by real memory reads rather than assumed from the code. **Verified fixed, with real evidence, for the first time this project has ever gotten this far**: after running an EARLIER, wider-scoped build of the wipe tool (confirmed via its own `g_wipe_status`/`g_wipe_files_deleted` globals, read with `tools/xemu-debug.py mem` - the first real boot, wiping the whole pre-round-2 `valve` tree, found 20 stale files and 6 directories to delete, 0 errors) and rebuilding `hl.xbe` fresh off current `main` (`tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh hl --xbe`, the real server + still-stubbed-client combination already established as bootable), a live boot with the disc's own `r_refdll "nv2a"` reached `debug_marker == 5` (`R_Init` ran to full success) and `nv2a_ready == 1` - both READ LIVE OUT OF GUEST MEMORY, not inferred - for the first time in this project's entire history; every previous attempt (#88, #89, #90, #91) read `debug_marker == 0`, meaning `R_Init` was never even entered. **A second, different blocker discovered immediately after, and NOT specific to `ref/nv2a` - SUPERSEDED, see #93: this was a testing-methodology gap (the still-stubbed client, not the real `--include-client --include-freevgui` combination X7.4 step 2 already proved working), not a real engine defect. The diagnosis below is preserved as the real, honest record of what was observed and concluded at the time; #93 corrects it.** `debug_frame_count` stayed at 0 for over four minutes of real wall-clock boot time (screenshots taken at ~0s/10s/70s/130s/310s all identical, byte-for-byte-static framebuffer physical address, `tools/xemu-debug.py memstats`'s own heap/contiguous page counts frozen at the exact same values across that whole span) - `R_Init` succeeds but the engine never calls back into `R_EndFrame` afterward. **A control test proved this is not an `ref/nv2a` defect**: rebuilding the identical disc with `r_refdll "soft"` instead (the already-established, previously-working software renderer) hit the exact same symptom - a static black screen, a frozen `memstats` snapshot, unresponsive to a second full reboot attempt on the by-then-partially-repopulated `E:` - ruling out both "`ref/nv2a`'s own no-op 2D stubs stall the menu" and "this is a first-boot-only artifact of a freshly wiped `E:`" as the explanation. The two renders differ only in how FAR they got before freezing (`ref_soft`'s own boot had allocated meaningfully more heap - 6.18 MiB `VirtualMemory` vs `ref_nv2a`'s 1.32 MiB - consistent with `ref_soft` doing real 2D asset uploads `ref/nv2a` never attempts), which places this second hang somewhere in a path COMMON to both renderers (most likely mainui/menu asset loading), not in renderer selection or `ref_api_t` wiring at all. Not chased further this session, per this project's own "stop and document, don't loop" rule: the deeper diagnostics that would actually localize this (a live register/stack read) are exactly the `regs`/`mon`/`stack` commands this project's own tooling has already found unreliable enough to avoid relying on (`tools/xemu-debug.py`'s own header comment); doing so risked corrupting the very live state that had just, for the first time, proven the original fix real | Form 1 (new standalone tool, isolated from the engine build entirely - same category as `tools/x4-triangle`) for the wipe tool; Form 1 (`ref/nv2a`-only, one new global plus one increment) for the frame counter | None for the wipe tool (never linked into any shippable build, deletes only the two exact filenames this fork's own build can ever write on a developer's local test HDD); Low for the frame counter (bring-up diagnostic, same category as the already-present `debug_marker`/`debug_pb_status`) | The E:-shadow blocker itself is CLOSED - confirmed fixed with live evidence, and `tools/xbox-e-wipe` is a permanent, reusable fix for any future recurrence (the same collision will recur on every future test boot, by design, until `cxbe`'s own hardcoded TitleID is fixed upstream - tracked, not attempted here). | 91 | `ref/nv2a` draws a WHOLE map's real baked geometry (X9-D) instead of X9-B/C's own single hard-coded `FaceDrawCmd[0]` face. `nv2a_load_map_geometry()` rewritten: `FaceDrawCmd` is no longer read at all (`nv2a_compute_mpak_layout()` still steps over that lump for cursor correctness, into an unused `skip` local like its other neighbors, but `nv2a_mpak_geometry_t` no longer captures it) - the baker's own shared index buffer is already one flat, contiguous list of every face's triangles in bake order, so drawing all of it needs no per-face lookup. Every one of the header's own `vCount` baked vertices is now remapped 1:1 directly into a GPU-visible buffer allocated at its real final size (`vCount * sizeof(nv2a_lit_vertex_t)`, no small fixed-size staging array or dedup table - a real vertex already only exists once and is referenced by index, so there is nothing to deduplicate at map scope), and every one of the `iCount` raw indices is widened/packed directly into a GPU-visible dword buffer at its real final size (`(iCount+1)/2` dwords) - both replacing X9-B/C's own hand-sized 16-vertex/64-index stack arrays, which would have been a real stack-overflow risk at real-map scale (`crossfire.mpak` alone has 19,480 vertices and 34,326 indices). A new upfront memory-budget check (`NV2A_MAP_BUDGET_BYTES`, 24MiB) rejects cleanly, before allocating anything, if a map's own real vertex+index+atlas+lightmap cost, PLUS the already-resident source archive buffer itself (`buffersize` - real, known cost this function can see, per `Mod_ProcessRenderData`'s own contract that the whole archive is already loaded before this is called), would exceed it - explicitly documented as a coarse guard against a corrupt or implausibly large archive, not a real accounting of the engine/pbkit/fragmentation cost this one narrowly-scoped ref module cannot see (a proper admission-control budget belongs at bake time, in `tools/asset-studio`, tracked as real follow-on work). The packed index-dword buffer moved from contiguous GPU-visible memory to ordinary engine-pooled CPU memory (`r_temppool`/`Mem_Malloc`/`Mem_Free`, the same real allocator `ref/soft` and `ref/gl` already use for their own CPU-side data) since it is only ever CPU-read into the pushbuffer, never touched by the GPU directly - `R_Init`/`R_Shutdown` now allocate/free that pool on every path. The fixed debug look-at camera is now framed from the map's own first three baked vertices (`vertices[0..2]`) instead of one chosen face's own three. `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()`'s own batched (`<=120`-dword) indexed-draw loop needed NO changes - it was already written generically over `nv2a_map_dword_count`, so this slice is the first thing to actually exercise more than one batch | X9's own plan named this the natural next slice once X9-B/C had proven real geometry+texture+lightmap on one face; the user chose it explicitly over jumping to the disconnected studio-model/VP20-skinning work. **This turned out to be a simplification, not a bigger version of the same complexity**, once the one-face special-casing was removed: both the vertex and index loops become straight `for (i = 0; i < count; i++)` walks writing directly into the final GPU buffer, with no "which vertices does this one face touch" lookup at all - that machinery only ever existed because the old code drew an arbitrary subset of the buffer. **Independently verified in Python against the real `crossfire.mpak`** (the same ground-truth file X9-B/C already used) before trusting the C port: the real archive is 19,480 vertices / 34,326 indices, all 19,480 vertices sample lightmap page 0 (so this slice's own inherited single-page limit does not reject this specific test file), all 34,326 raw indices are `< vCount` (no malformed data to reject), the real per-map cost (vertex buffer + packed indices + atlas + lightmap + the source archive itself = ~16.6MB) fits well inside the new 24MiB combined budget, with real margin left over, and the real dword count (17,163) requires 144 separate `<=120`-dword batches - the first real evidence in this module that the batching loop's own multi-batch path, written generically from X9-B onward but never before exercised past one batch, is actually load-bearing. The module compiles, isolates (0 leaked, 93 symbols) and links cleanly through both `tools/xbox-link-check.sh` and `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh hl` (0 duplicate, 0 undefined, real valid XBEs both times, including a full real `hl` server-module link). **Codex review, round 1, two real findings, both fixed.** First: the original 32MiB budget check only summed the four NEW allocations this slice makes, silently ignoring the source archive buffer that stays resident throughout this same call (per `Mod_ProcessRenderData`'s own documented contract) and everything else already resident on a real console (the engine's own state, pbkit's front/back/depth buffers, every other subsystem, allocator fragmentation) - none of which this one narrowly-scoped ref module can see or account for. Fixed by adding the one real, known cost this function CAN see (`buffersize`, the already-resident source buffer) into the sum, lowering the cap to 24MiB, and rewriting the comment to say plainly what this check is (a coarse guard against a corrupt or absurd archive) and is not (a real platform memory ledger - that belongs at bake time, in `tools/asset-studio`, tracked as real follow-on work). Second: the new packed index-dword buffer is only ever CPU-read into the pushbuffer (never touched by the GPU directly) but was allocated as contiguous, write-combined GPU-visible memory anyway - needlessly spending a scarce resource on data that never needed it, and giving a repeatedly-CPU-read buffer a write-combined access pattern. Fixed by moving it to ordinary engine-pooled memory (`r_temppool`, the same real allocator `ref/soft`/`ref/gl` already use for their own CPU-side state), which this module previously never allocated at all - `R_Init` now creates it (freed on every one of `R_Init`'s own failure paths) and `R_Shutdown` frees it. **Codex review, round 2 (the final allowed round for this branch), one real finding, partially fixed and partially justified as out of scope.** The 24MiB runtime cap is still not a real unified-memory budget - a validated, correctly-sized archive that passed this check could still fail one of the four allocations below it from real contiguous-memory fragmentation on an already-running console, and that failure fell back to the test triangle completely silently, with no diagnostic anywhere. **Fixed**: every allocation-failure path in `nv2a_load_map_geometry()` (the vertex buffer, the atlas, the lightmap, the packed index buffer) and the budget-rejection path itself now call `gEngfuncs.Con_Printf()` with the real byte counts involved - a real map that fails to load its NV2A geometry now says so on the console, rather than silently drawing the test triangle with no trace of why. **Justified, not fixed**: codex's own recommendation - "implement the declared asset-studio memory manifest now... fail the bake when global + max(map) exceeds the configured 64MiB budget" - names `fork-plan.md`'s own X3 ("Memory budget") phase by its exact described exit criterion (a bake-time manifest covering BOTH allocators, heap and contiguous, emitted by `tools/asset-studio`, gating the build). X3 is a real, already-tracked, already-sequenced, NOT-yet-implemented phase of its own (its own section's last dated entry, 2026-09-14, still lists most of its own analysis as "not done") - it is a wholly separate asset-studio subsystem, not a change to one ref-module bring-up function's own parse loop, and building it here would mean silently absorbing an entire other phase's scope into this branch rather than the sequenced, reviewable slice this project has followed for every prior phase (X9-A through C included). This branch's own runtime check remains, and is now explicitly documented as, exactly what it can honestly be from inside one narrowly-scoped ref module: a guard against a corrupt or absurdly oversized archive reaching a doomed allocation, with a real diagnostic on the failures it cannot itself prevent - not the platform-wide ledger X3 will eventually provide. **Not verified live this session, same already-tracked blocker as #88/#89/#90, and per this branch's own plan deliberately not re-attempted a third time**: the stale `E:`-side `video.cfg` shadowing issue and the large-real-map-boot unresponsiveness observed on X9-B/C's own live attempts both still apply. Scope limits carried over unchanged from X9-C: only vertices on lightmap page 0 are handled (a vertex on any other page fails the whole load cleanly); no visibility/frustum culling (the whole map's geometry is submitted every frame regardless of what the fixed camera can see - correct but wasteful); no real camera/view system; no multi-page lightmap handling; no native alpha test; no dynamic lighting | Form 1 (same `ref/nv2a` module already established by #88/#89/#90 - this row only rewrites one function's own body and two struct/array declarations within it) | Low - dead code unless a map's own `.mpak` has `MPAK_FLAG_NV2A_BLOCK` set and `r_refdll` is `"nv2a"`; every ordinary map and every other renderer is completely unaffected, and a malformed, oversized, or multi-lightmap-page archive fails cleanly to the already-proven X9-A test triangle rather than crashing or reading out of bounds | Real, and still large, same as #88/#89/#90's own: closes only once a full map's baked geometry, texture and lighting render correctly and match X5's software frame time (X9's own stated exit criterion). This row's own narrower gaps - the live-render proof (blocked, see above), the single-lightmap-page limit, the total absence of culling, and the still-fixed debug camera - all carry forward to whichever X9 slice next has working live verification tooling, multi-page atlas handling, or a real view system |

`nv2a_load_map_geometry()` reads each vertex's already-page-normalized `lu`/`lv` (no atlas-rect remap needed here, unlike the diffuse UV) into a new, wider vertex format (`nv2a_lit_vertex_t`: pos + two UV pairs); `R_EndFrame`'s map-geometry path binds the lightmap on texture stage 1 (`NV097_SET_TEXTURE_*` registers at `+0x40` from stage 0's own, `tools/x4-triangle/main.c`'s own documented per-stage addressing) using the same proven uncompressed-linear family X9-A's own checkerboard already used - the lightmap atlas is RGBA8, needing no DXT/compressed format at all, though (see the codex-review finding below) NOT the exact same `LU_IMAGE_A8R8G8B8` constant | X9's own plan named this the natural next slice after real geometry+diffuse texture (X9-B). **A real, verified-before-relying-on-it toolchain gap closed**: X9-A/X9-B only ever reused already-compiled `.inl` files (copied from `tools/x4-triangle/`); this is the first slice needing a genuinely new shader (a second texcoord), so the `cgc`/`vp20compiler`/`fp20compiler` binaries under `deps/nxdk/tools/` were confirmed present AND working before committing to this approach - recompiling the EXISTING `vs.vs.cg` through the exact pipeline `vs.inl` was produced by and diffing the output (byte-for-byte identical instruction words) - not assumed from the fact that some `.inl` files already existed in the tree. **A real, caught-before-shipping mistake this same verification habit prevented**: an earlier round of research this session (before this branch existed) hand-derived what it guessed the diffuse×lightmap combiner's own register values should be (`AB_DST=0xC`/`SPARE0`, final `D_SOURCE=0x8`) from reading `nv_regs.h`'s own register definitions in isolation - the REAL `fp20compiler` output for the actual Cg source compiles to different, correct values (`AB_DST=0x4`, final `D_SOURCE=0x4`, with the ×2 implemented via `OCW_OP_SHIFTLEFTBY1` rather than a separate scale step) - had this branch skipped compiling and used the earlier hand-derived guess instead, the combiner would have been wrong in a way nothing in this session's own static verification would have caught. `TEXCOORD1`'s own hardware attribute slot (10, following `TEXCOORD0`'s already-confirmed slot 9) was likewise read directly off a real `vp20compiler` warning, not assumed by extrapolation. **The NV2A holds only one resident vertex/pixel program at a time** - both draw paths (X9-A's own test triangle, this slice's real geometry) now re-upload their own program right before drawing every frame they run, rather than assuming whichever was uploaded last is still the right one; a small, correctness-motivated, performance-irrelevant-for-a-bring-up cost. **Verified as far as static/pre-runtime checks reach, the same honest boundary X9-B's own entry already drew**: both new shaders were independently re-compiled a second time and diffed against the committed `.inl` files (reproducible, byte-for-byte identical) before being trusted; the lightmap-atlas length-vs-dimensions math (`width * height * layers * 4` for RGBA8) was cross-checked in Python against `crossfire.mpak`'s own real header fields (1024x1024x1x4 = 4,194,304, matching `lenLightmapAtlas` exactly) and its first bytes read back as a plausible low-brightness RGBA gradient, not garbage; the module compiles, isolates (0 leaked) and links cleanly (0 duplicate, 0 undefined, a real valid XBE). **Not verified live this session, same already-tracked blocker as #88/#89**: the stale `E:`-side `video.cfg` shadowing issue and the large-real-map-boot unresponsiveness observed on X9-B's own live attempt both still apply and were not re-attempted here, consistent with this project's own "stop and document, don't loop" rule once a workaround has already failed once. One deliberate, narrow scope limit carried over unchanged from the approved plan: this slice only handles a face whose vertices all sample lightmap page 0 (the one this test face and this test map's own single-page atlas both are) - a future face spanning more than one page fails this check cleanly (falls back to the test triangle) rather than silently sampling the wrong page, tracked as real, deferred work alongside multi-page atlas handling generally. Native alpha test (the other half of X9's own original "combiners + alpha test" plan line) stays explicitly out of scope - nothing this or the prior slice parses carries a per-texture alpha-test flag (GoldSrc's own masked-texture naming convention, which needs real texinfo/texture-name lookup this session's own scope never added). **Codex review, round 1, one real bug, fixed.** The lightmap was bound as `LU_IMAGE_A8R8G8B8` - the SAME format constant X9-A's own checkerboard correctly uses, but for a different, incompatible reason: the checkerboard writes native `uint32_t` INTEGER literals (`0xFFFFFFFFu` etc.) that already match that format's own packed-integer convention, while the baker writes the lightmap bytewise as literal `R,G,B,A` per texel (`tools/asset-studio/src/formats/BspParser.cpp`'s `writeBlock()`, confirmed by reading it directly) - binding that raw byte buffer as `A8R8G8B8` swaps red and blue for any texel where they differ. The grayscale gradient this session's own verification sampled (`R=G=B` at every texel) could never have caught this, exactly the kind of gap codex review is for. Fixed (round 1) by binding `LU_IMAGE_R8G8B8A8` - which round 1's own reasoning treated as "the format literally named after the baker's own byte order," an inference that turned out to be backwards. **Codex review, round 2 (the final allowed round for this branch), corrected round 1's own fix.** For a raw little-endian byte buffer, the format name has to match the INTEGER that byte order forms when read as a little-endian word, not the channel letters read left-to-right: `[R,G,B,A]` in memory (byte0=R is the lowest, least-significant byte) forms the word `0xAABBGGRR` - matching `LU_IMAGE_A8B8G8R8`'s own name (A=the word's most-significant byte down to R=its least-significant), not `LU_IMAGE_R8G8B8A8` (which names the word `0xRRGGBBAA`, i.e. memory order `[A,B,G,R]` - the reverse of this buffer's real layout). Independently re-derived by hand against the checkerboard's own already-confirmed convention (`0xFFFF8000` as `A8R8G8B8` reads as opaque HEV orange - A=FF, R=FF, G=80, B=00, checked and correct) before trusting the correction, since round 1's own wrong fix was exactly the kind of mistake this ledger exists to catch, not repeat. Fixed by binding `LU_IMAGE_A8B8G8R8` instead - X9-A's own checkerboard (built from integer literals, not raw bytes, so this specific mistake never applied to it) is untouched throughout both rounds | Form 1 (new files: two Cg sources plus their compiled `.inl` output, already established by #88/#89's own `ref/nv2a` module) for the shader/combiner/lightmap code | Low - dead code unless a map's own `.mpak` has `MPAK_FLAG_NV2A_BLOCK` set, `r_refdll` is `"nv2a"`, AND the drawn face's own vertices are all on lightmap page 0; every ordinary map and every other renderer is completely unaffected, and a lightmap-atlas dimension/length mismatch or a non-zero lightmap page both fail cleanly to the already-proven X9-A test triangle rather than reading past an allocation or sampling wrong data | Real, and still large, same as #88/#89's own: closes only once a full map's baked geometry, texture AND lighting render correctly and match X5's software frame time (X9's own stated exit criterion). This row's own narrower gaps - the live-render proof (blocked, see above), the single-lightmap-page limit, and native alpha test - all carry forward to whichever X9 slice next has working live verification tooling, multi-page atlas handling, or real texture-name/flags data |
| 89 | `ref/nv2a` draws one real face from a real, nv2a-profile-baked `.mpak` (X9-B) instead of X9-A's own hardcoded triangle, when one is loaded - `Mod_ProcessRenderData` (`ref/nv2a/r_context.c`, previously a `ref_null`-style stub) now parses the raw buffer `engine/common/model.c`'s `Mod_LoadModel` already hands every ref module via `FS_LoadFile`, and `R_EndFrame` draws the parsed geometry with a real look-at + perspective camera instead of the fixed 2D transform. New: a private, narrowly-scoped mirror of `filesystem/mpak_layout.c`'s own `Mpak_ComputeLayout()` (necessary, not redundant - `ref/` and `filesystem/` are separate isolated static-lib units that only reach the engine through their own function-pointer APIs, never each other, so this module can `#include "mpak_layout.h"` for the shared types/constants but cannot link against the real function body); a per-vertex `atlas_uv = rect.xy + frac(raw_uv) * rect.zw` remap (the baker's own per-vertex UV is texture-local, not atlas-local - the `rect` field is where the atlas placement actually lives); real row-vector LookAt/Perspective matrix construction (`nv2a_matrix_lookat`/`nv2a_matrix_perspective`), chaining unmodified into X9-A's own existing NDC-to-screen `nv2a_matrix_viewport()` with no shader change, since `vs.vs.cg`'s own shader already does exactly one `mul(position, matrix)` plus a `position.xyz /= position.w` divide - any matrix producing NDC xy in [-1,1] and z in [0,1] with a real w works, not just the flat one X9-A fed it; and a real DXT1 texture bind (`NV097_SET_TEXTURE_FORMAT_COLOR_L_DXT1_A1R5G5B5`) replacing X9-A's own uncompressed checkerboard | Research this session found the real scope is smaller than X9's own plan feared: each baked `Vertex` (`tools/asset-studio/src/formats/PakManager.h`, mirrored byte-for-byte as `mpak_vertex_t`) already carries its own diffuse UV, atlas rect and lightmap UV/page baked in - no runtime texinfo/material lookup needed to draw one face - and Xbox uses exactly ONE shared DXT1 atlas per map (no per-material texture switching). Reading `tools/asset-studio/src/utils/TextureOpt.cpp` directly found the world atlas is NEVER Morton-swizzled (`swizzleNV2A`'s only caller anywhere is an unrelated 2D UI packer) - it is already plain, unswizzled, row-major DXT1, exactly the layout NV2A's own `L_DXT1_A1R5G5B5` format expects (the `L_` prefix meaning Linear - there is no swizzled-DXT mode on this hardware at all), so neither a baker fix nor a load-time un-swizzle was needed, unlike this ledger's own earlier guess. **A real precedence gap found and worked around, not fixed**: `filesystem/searchpath.c`'s own archive-type table (`.ext = "mpak"`) deliberately makes a loose `maps/<name>.bsp` win over a same-named `.mpak` sitting alongside it - a real, existing, correctly-reasoned safety choice (its own comment: `Mod_LoadModel`'s format dispatch doesn't recognize raw MPAK-magic bytes yet at the top level, so a map that would load fine from the loose file must not be shadowed by an unconsumable one) that this bring-up's own test disc had to route around by *removing* the loose `crossfire.bsp` before staging `crossfire.mpak`, not something this branch should or does change. **Verified as far as static/pre-runtime checks reach**: the section-offset mirror's own math was independently validated in Python against a real bake (`crossfire.mpak`, nv2a profile, 4067 faces) BEFORE being ported to C - the computed cursor lands exactly on the real file size, and the vertex/index/`FaceDrawCmd` data read back at the computed offsets is real, sane geometry (a real 128x33-unit quad, GoldSrc-plausible coordinates) - and the module compiles, isolates (0 leaked) and links cleanly through both `tools/xbox-link-check.sh` and `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh` (0 duplicate, 0 undefined, real valid XBEs both times). **Not verified live this session, and not chased further, matching this project's own "stop and document, don't loop" rule**: a boot with the real `crossfire.mpak` staged left the guest unresponsive to `tools/xemu-debug.py`'s screenshot/mem commands well past every timeout that succeeded readily for X9-A's own much simpler menu-only boot, on top of the already-documented, still-unresolved #88 issue (a stale `E:`-side `video.cfg` on the shared xemu test HDD, which would keep `r_refdll` from ever reaching `"nv2a"` regardless). Whether that's the map's own real size/complexity being slow under whatever renderer is actually active, a genuine hang, or something specific to the debug stub was not determined - the process stayed alive (memory footprint stable, not crashed) across four separate wait-and-retry attempts before this was accepted as blocked rather than pursued into a fifth. One deliberate, documented simplification for this bring-up slice, not a bug: the UV remap is per-VERTEX, not per-pixel, which is exact for a face whose raw UV stays within one texture repeat and visibly wrong (a flat, non-repeating texture instead of a tiled one) for a face that wraps by an integer count between two of its own vertices - `crossfire.mpak`'s own `FaceDrawCmd[0]` is exactly this second case (its `u` wraps by 2.0 between corners), so the very first real face this code was tested against would show that artifact once actually seen rendering, not a rare corner case - fixing it for real needs a pixel-shader-side wrap (out of scope; X9-C's own combiner work is the earliest place that could land). **Codex review, round 1, two real bugs, both fixed.** First: `NV20_TCL_PRIMITIVE_3D_INDEX_DATA` packs TWO 16-bit indices into every dword (`deps/nxdk/samples/mesh/verts.h`'s own data, e.g. `0x00010002` is indices `{2, 1}`) - the first version submitted one raw 32-bit "index" per dword instead, silently zeroing every odd-positioned index's own upper 16 bits at the GPU and drawing a different, mostly-degenerate triangle than the real one. Fixed by packing pairs of local indices into dwords after the parse loop (an odd count pads its final dword by repeating the last real index - a harmless degenerate triangle, not corrupt data), tracking the dword count separately from the logical index count. Second, more serious: `Mod_ProcessRenderData` is called for EVERY model the engine loads or unloads - every inline brush submodel, every studio model, every sprite (`engine/common/model.c`), not only the world - and the first version unconditionally freed the current map geometry at the TOP of every call before even looking at the new buffer, so the very first studio or sprite model loaded right after the world (normal for any real map with monsters/weapons) silently wiped the world's own already-parsed geometry and fell back to the test triangle for the rest of the level. Fixed with real ownership tracking: a `nv2a_map_owner` pointer records which specific `model_t` populated the current map state, an unload only clears it if the SAME model is unloading, and a load only replaces it once the new buffer has already been confirmed (via a new side-effect-free `nv2a_mpak_header_valid()` pre-check) to be a real nv2a-profile archive - an unrelated model's own load or unload now leaves whatever the world already set up completely alone. Neither bug was reachable by anything this session's own static verification checked (both are about the SEQUENCE of calls across a real level's full model-precache list, not about one archive's own byte layout), found only by review reasoning about the real calling pattern - a reminder that the "verified as far as static checks reach" paragraph above was real but partial. **Codex review, round 2 (the final allowed round for this branch), one more real bug, fixed.** `atlasWidth`/`atlasHeight` were read straight from the header and trusted directly by the draw path's own pitch/image-rect register values, with nothing checking them against the atlas lump's OWN already-bounds-checked length - a malformed archive could declare a huge width/height over a tiny real allocation, and the GPU would then read past `nv2a_map_atlas` into whatever memory follows it. Fixed by requiring `ceil(width/4) * ceil(height/4) * 8` (DXT1's own real byte cost, computed in `uint64_t` so the multiply itself cannot overflow for any `uint32_t` header field) to match the atlas lump's length EXACTLY before trusting either dimension - the real `crossfire.mpak` (2048x2048, `lenAtlas` 2097152) still passes this check precisely (512x512 blocks x 8 = 2097152), so this is a real tightening, not a workaround that happens to break the one file tested against it | Form 1 (a new module, `ref/nv2a`, already established by #88) for the geometry/texture/camera code; Form 3 (`#include` of an existing shared header, no engine-side change) for the `mpak_layout.h` reuse | Low - dead code unless a map's own `.mpak` genuinely has `MPAK_FLAG_NV2A_BLOCK` set AND `r_refdll` is `"nv2a"`; every ordinary core-profile map, and every map under `ref_soft`/`ref_null`, is completely unaffected, and a malformed/undersized/wrong-flag archive falls back to X9-A's own already-proven test triangle rather than crashing (bounds-checked at every step this mirror added) | Real, and large, same as #88's own: closes only once a full map's baked geometry renders correctly and matches X5's software frame time (X9's own stated exit criterion). A live boot attempt (`r_refdll "nv2a"` in a packed disc's `video.cfg`) did NOT show the test triangle - direct memory reads (`tools/xemu-debug.py mem`, the only debug commands this project's own tooling has found reliable) traced this to something OTHER than a defect in this module: `ref.num_renderers` reads 2 at runtime (confirming `nv2a` really is registered), the generated static-library table's `"GetRefAPI"` entry for `ref_nv2a` correctly resolves to the real, linked `_nv2a_GetRefAPI` address, and `gReffuncs.R_Init` in the live table correctly matches this module's own compiled `R_Init` address - every piece of static wiring is provably correct. Yet a `debug_marker` global (bring-up-only instrumentation, set to 1 as `R_Init`'s first statement) read back as 0, and `r_refdll`'s own live string read `"soft"`, not the disc's `"nv2a"` - meaning `nv2a`'s `R_Init` was never even attempted. Root-caused by reading `filesystem/searchpath.c`'s `FS_Init` directly, not guessed: `FS_AddGameDirectory` prepends to `fs_searchpaths` (`search->next = fs_searchpaths; fs_searchpaths = search;`), and `fs_rootdir`'s own game directory is added AFTER `fs_rodir`'s (`:672` then `:675`) - so `fs_rootdir` (`E:`, writable) is searched before `fs_rodir` (`D:`, the disc) for every file lookup, `video.cfg` included. This xemu instance's `xbox_hdd.qcow2` has been reused across dozens of earlier boots this session (item 4's own verification among them); any of them plausibly left a `video.cfg` on `E:` lacking an `r_refdll` line (it is not an `FCVAR_ARCHIVE` cvar, so `host_writeconfig` would not necessarily preserve a prior session's explicit setting), which would permanently shadow this disc's own fresh copy on this same virtual HDD - a real property of this project's own writable-overlay filesystem design (divergence #48, built for save files), not a bug, but a genuine test-environment artifact for exactly this kind of disc-level override. Attempted one workaround: pointing `hdd_path` at a nonexistent file, hoping xemu would create a fresh empty one - instead xemu omitted the HDD drive from its QEMU launch line entirely, and the boot hung (screenshot/mem timed out repeatedly against an otherwise-alive process) - a different, unexplored dead end, not a finding about `ref/nv2a` itself, reverted immediately; the user's own xemu config was backed up before this attempt and fully restored after, verified byte-for-byte parseable. **Not chased further this session**: clearing or bypassing the stale `E:` state (a fresh HDD image, or direct FATX-level surgery on the existing one) needs tooling (`qemu-img` or an FATX editor) not available in this environment - carried forward as the concrete next step for whoever picks up X9-B, not silently dropped. **Codex review, round 2, two real findings on the init/shutdown lifecycle, one fixed and one justified rather than guessed at.** Fixed: `R_Shutdown` freed `nv2a_vb`/`nv2a_tex` BEFORE `pb_kill()` - since `R_EndFrame`'s own `pb_finished()` swap is asynchronous, a shutdown immediately after a submitted frame could free pages the NV2A was still fetching from; reordered to `pb_kill()` (which itself waits for the GPU to go idle) before freeing anything, applied consistently to every failure-cleanup path in `R_Init` too. Justified, not fixed: a nonzero `pb_init()` is not reliably atomic in this exact nxdk version - its own internal failure paths (`deps/nxdk/lib/pbkit/pbkit.c` ~2297-2366) can return early after allocating some but not all of its own buffers, or after installing the GPU interrupt handler and writing GPU registers, all without calling `pb_kill()` itself; codex's own recommendation was explicit that blindly calling `pb_kill()` on every failure would be WRONG given this, since pbkit has no documented contract for tearing down a partially-failed init. The real fix belongs in nxdk (making `pb_init()` atomic on every path), tracked here as a genuine, deferred limitation of a vendored dependency - not attempted in this bring-up module | Forms 1-2 (new files, Xbox-only build scripts and a new Xbox-only ref module) for the module and its exports; Form 3 (`#ifdef` additions, `#if XASH_REF_NV2A_ENABLED`-gated) for the `tools/xbox-cc.sh` define and `R_CollectRendererNames()`'s array entries, inert on every other platform and inert on Xbox itself unless `r_refdll "nv2a"` is explicitly set | Low - the new module is dead code unless explicitly selected via `r_refdll`, `ref_soft` stays the default XM.1 already proved working, and every existing ref module's own wiring (`ref_null`, `ref_soft`) is untouched | Real, and large: this is the first of many X9 slices (X9-B through X9-F sketched in `fork-plan.md`'s own X9 entry) - closes only once a real map's baked geometry renders on the NV2A and matches X5's software output frame time, per X9's own stated exit criterion.
