Fixed with a small Xbox-only subclass, `CMenuSchemeSwitch`, overriding `KeyDown` to handle `IsLeftArrow`/`IsRightArrow` directly against this screen's own known, fixed segment count - not a change to the shared control (`ServerBrowser.cpp`'s own `tabSwitch`, PC-only and mouse-driven, is untouched). Second (medium): B/Escape's own inherited exit path calls `Hide()` directly (the same idiom `Pause.cpp`/`Controls.cpp` already document), never `Done`'s own `SaveAndPopMenu()` - so a scheme picked here and left via B was applied live but never persisted, reverting on next boot, the same class of bug divergences.md #85 already found and fixed on the Customize sub-screen. Fixed by moving persistence into a `Hide()` override and routing `Done` through it too (one path, not two separate writes) - PC's own `SaveAndPopMenu()` is unchanged and still Done's own PC target. **Round 2 found two more real defects, both fixed before this PR opened.** First (high): the same registration-order bug divergence #66/#67 already found elsewhere in this exact file family - `settingsBtn`/`customizeBtn` were registered (`AddItem()`) immediately on construction, before `heading`/`schemeSwitch` were registered later in the same function, so pad focus order became Settings → Customize → Scheme → Done despite the scheme switch sitting visually above both buttons. Fixed by keeping heap allocation and `m_apBtns` ownership tracking where they were (unrelated to focus order) but moving the actual `AddItem()` calls into the final block, in true visual order. Second (medium): the five-item mapping list collapsed real, independently-set state into a single misleading line twice over - "D-pad: %s" read only `K_DPAD_UP`, hiding Standard's own three other weapon-slot binds and Legacy's own three other weapon-navigation binds entirely, and "Left stick: %s" checked only `joy_axis_binding`'s first character, which would call a mixed/malformed two-axis assignment (unreachable through any scheme this fork ships, but possible via a hand-edited config) wholly "Look" or "Move" instead of flagging it. Fixed by splitting the D-pad row into four (one per direction, growing the list from 5 to 8 items - the plan's own "beside the Duke diagram" phrasing didn't dictate an exact row count) and checking both left-stick axis characters, reporting "Mixed" if they disagree. Once the main repo's own pointer bumped past mainui_cpp#5, then again past #6 (the Custom-selection and localization fixes above), a full `tools/xbox-mainui-sweep.sh --expect-total 86 --expect-fail 0` confirmed 86/86 clean against the truly final version of both files. **Main-repo review round 2 found a real, unrelated-to-behaviour defect**: an earlier session tool had silently converted the whole of `Gamepad.cpp` from LF to CRLF while editing an array size (Python's default text-mode file write translates `
` to the OS-native line ending, CRLF on Windows) - undetected for two full submodule PRs (#5, #6) because, unlike the main Resonance3D repo, this submodule carries no `.gitattributes` normalising text files back to LF on commit. The ordinary diff read as 685 insertions/685 deletions against a semantically five-line change; `git diff --check` flagged every line as trailing whitespace. Fixed in a follow-up submodule PR (`xm1/controller-screen-crlf-fix`, mainui_cpp#7, merged): line endings restored and a new `3rdparty/mainui/.gitattributes` added (`*.cpp`/`*.h`, `eol=lf`) so a future accidental flip gets normalised away instead of merging as a whole-file diff. Once the main repo's own pointer bumped past #7, a full sweep confirmed 86/86 clean and `git diff --check` against the true original base confirmed clean too. **Main-repo review round 1 found two more real defects, fixed in a follow-up submodule PR** (`xm1/controller-screen-fix`, same two-tier pattern as #85's own follow-up). First (medium): navigating the switch onto Custom applied nothing (`UI_ApplyControllerScheme()`'s own documented no-op for it), leaving the switch showing Custom while every actual bind stayed whatever named scheme was active - reopening the screen would detect and show that OLD scheme again, a real, confusing mismatch. Fixed by making `CMenuSchemeSwitch`'s own D-pad handling skip Custom entirely - then, once the submodule review for that fix itself found a mouse click could still reach Custom through `CMenuSwitch::KeyUp`'s own separate state-changing path (unlikely on a real pad, but the code path exists regardless), closed that too by checking the resulting state after the base class's own click handling and reverting if it landed on Custom. It is now reachable only by detection on entry, never by navigation or a click. Second (medium): all eight mapping-list row prefixes, plus the Look/Move/Mixed role words, were raw English literals never passed through `L()` - a German player would see an English prefix ahead of a correctly-translated action name, since the newly-added German dictionary entries had nothing to translate. Fixed by wrapping every one in `L()`, with ten more German translations added alongside (`assets/resource/mainui_german.txt`, main repo). **Verified with a direct single-file `tools/xbox-cxx.sh` recompile** (both `Gamepad.cpp` and `Configuration.cpp`, clean) for the same reason #84/#85 already recorded - the full `tools/xbox-mainui-sweep.sh` cannot see an in-progress submodule branch until its own pointer-bump PR merges. **Live pad verification, finally reachable through a real on-screen path (not just a console command, unlike #84/#85's own tracked gap) was attempted, not just deferred - and remains genuinely incomplete, blocked by a local tooling problem, not a code defect. **What was attempted, 2026-09-19**: a real `hl` disc was built (`tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh --include-client --include-freevgui --expect-undefined 0`, 0 duplicate/0 undefined) and packed (`tools/xbox-xiso.sh`), and booted clean in xemu to the real, unmodified Main menu multiple times (screenshot-confirmed, no regression). Synthetic pad input (`tools/xemu-pad.py`) was then used to try to navigate Main → Options → Controller, but every press - confirmed successfully claimed and executed at the pad-server/ViGEmBus level (`ok pressed ...` for every command) - had zero effect on the guest; the screen never advanced past Main across four independent attempts, each changing one variable: (1) plain retry, (2) creating the virtual pad before launching xemu rather than mid-session (this project's own documented caveat that a mid-session-created device is not reliably picked up), (3) explicitly rebinding to confirm no stale/duplicate ViGEmBus device (`Get-PnpDevice` showed two `VID_045E&PID_02FF` HID entries, one live and one an `Unknown`-status leftover from an earlier uncleanly-killed `serve` process - not a real physical controller stealing the binding, as first suspected), (4) bringing the xemu window to OS foreground focus via `SetForegroundWindow` before pressing (Windows normally gates XInput delivery to the foreground window) - none of the four changed the outcome. The `tools/xemu-debug.py` GDB-stub side of the harness also proved unreliable this session independent of the pad problem: `verify-image`, `cursor`, and `mon cont` each left the guest stuck paused (monitor `cont` failing with `<monitor error: E22>` or a timeout) at least once, and on one occasion escalated to the framebuffer reading back solid grey (the instance genuinely dead) - `screenshot` and `mem` alone did not trigger that failure mode directly, but a `mem` read taken after the guest had already been left paused by an earlier `cursor`/`mon` call correctly reported a frozen `gpGlobals->time` across a 19-real-second gap, consistent with that. None of this is evidence of anything wrong in this branch's own code - every finding this session's four code-review rounds (across #84/#85/#86) surfaced was fixed and confirmed by direct recompilation; this is a live-hardware/emulator interaction problem in the current local xemu + ViGEmBus + xemu-debug.py setup that this session could not resolve within a reasonable number of attempts, logged here rather than papered over. **Root cause not identified** - candidates not yet ruled out include xemu's own SDL controller enumeration order, a Windows raw-input vs. XInput API mismatch specific to ViGEmBus virtual pads, or a version-specific xemu/QEMU gdbstub regression in this build (`v49/0.21 (win32-i386 build -1)`, QEMU 5.1.0-dirty per its own reported banner). **A related, separate, unrelated-to-this-branch observation surfaced during this same session and is intentionally NOT chased here**: the animated Main-menu banner (`AnimatedBanner.cpp`) visibly ran roughly 2-3x real speed on a fresh, undebugged xemu launch the user directly observed - `Platform_DoubleTime()`'s own timing chain (`engine/platform/sdl2/sys_sdl2.c` → SDL's `QueryPerformanceCounter`/`Frequency` → `deps/nxdk/lib/winapi/profiling.c`, which calibrates `tsc_freq` at boot by reading real Xbox chipset registers - `EBL_CR_POWERON`, PCI FSB divider/multiplier - assuming real hardware) is a plausible mechanism (xemu's own emulated CPU does not run RDTSC at the real Xbox's calibrated ~733 MHz, so software dividing by that calibrated value against a faster emulated tick rate would inflate every elapsed-time computation), but this is a vendored (`deps/nxdk`) file calibrated for real hardware being exercised on an emulator that does not reproduce that hardware's own timing characteristics - a textbook "xemu is not validation" case, not something introduced by any commit in this branch and out of this item's own scope to fix. Logged for whoever next touches Xbox timing, not acted on here. **What this means for closing this item**: the code itself is done and reviewed to this project's own full standard; the interactive live-pad proof `divergences.md`'s own established discipline calls for remains owed, blocked by tooling rather than skipped, and is carried forward as a tracked gap rather than closed by a screenshot-only claim - the same discipline this ledger has applied to every other genuinely-incomplete verification (`#67`'s own repeated "NOT verified live" entries are the closest precedent) | XM.1 item 5 (`fork-plan.md`), Phase 5 of 5 - the item's last branch, completing the plan's own reordered sequence (Settings, Customize, Controller last, per #84's note on why) | Form 3, whole-file `#if XASH_XBOX`/`#else` split (member declarations, `_Init()`, `_VidInit()`'s PC-only positioning block) - `SaveAndPopMenu()` itself is now fully split too, not shared: PC keeps its own `#if !XASH_XBOX`-guarded declaration and body (cvar writes, unconditional trailing `CMenuFramework::SaveAndPopMenu()`), while Xbox persists through a separate `Hide()` override instead (Codex review round 1's own fix, above - B/Escape's inherited path never reached a `SaveAndPopMenu()` override, only `Hide()`, so Xbox needed its own persistence point there instead of sharing PC's); `Configuration.cpp`'s own change is a plain `#if XASH_XBOX`/`#else` split of one `AddButton` call | Low - guarded to Xbox only, PC's own Joystick screen and Options row are untouched under `#else`; the new Xbox path is reachable today via `menu_gamepad` (the same pre-existing, independent-of-navigation console-command reachability #76/#85 already established) as well as, now, real UI navigation - live verification through either path is the owed item above, not a correctness question the code review already closed | None named - fork-owned Xbox screen with no upstream equivalent to converge back on; `Configuration.cpp`'s own remaining stock-pointed row (Video, item 7) stays exactly as before |
| 85 | `3rdparty/mainui/menus/Controls.cpp` (rewritten in place, mainui submodule, `xm1/controller-customize`): the Controller screen's "Customize" sub-screen. `CMenuKeysModel::Update()` now filters the shared `kb_act.lst`-driven action list down to only the commands `ControllerSchemes.cpp`'s own scheme tables actually bind - the real Standard/Southpaw/Legacy union (`+attack`/`+attack2`/`+jump`/`+duck`/`+use`/`+reload`/`invnext`/`invprev`/`impulse 100`/`+speed`/`slot1`-`slot4`/`save quick`/`pause`/`impulse 201`/`lastinv`) - most of `kb_act.lst` (movement, strafe, mouselook, voice chat, screenshots...) has no real gamepad-button equivalent a Customize row could usefully show. A synthetic row for Start is appended after filtering, the same technique `KbActListModel.h`'s own `AddVirtualCommand()` already uses to inject touch-only rows for its other view mode - Start's own binding (`cancelselect`, `engine/client/input/in_keys.c:115`) is not a `kb_act.lst` action at all, so nothing would show it otherwise, but the plan calls for it to be visible and explicitly locked (`EnterGrabMode()`/`UnbindEntry()` both refuse it, by name, before doing anything destructive) since Start is the only pad route back to the pause menu from gameplay - and the grab dialog's own `KeyUp()` separately refuses to let Start be captured AS a replacement for some OTHER row either, closing the one way that lock could otherwise be bypassed. The PC button row (`Use Defaults`/`Adv. Controls`/`OK`/`Cancel`) is replaced entirely by an A/X/Y/B model on Xbox: A reassigns (existing grab dialog, its own key-capture success path now Xbox-guarded, see below), X clears (existing `UnbindEntry()`, now clearing only the row's own gamepad binding(s), see below), Y applies the active scheme via `UI_ApplyControllerScheme( UI_DetectControllerScheme() )` - resolved to `SCHEME_STANDARD` if the config is `SCHEME_CUSTOM`, the plan's own explicit fallback so the row is never a no-op for whoever needs it most - and B backs out through a `Hide()` override that runs `host_writeconfig`, persisting every change this screen made (the same command the base class's own `SaveAndPopMenu()` already runs before every "OK"-style close elsewhere in this codebase), since B is this screen's only way to leave at all and there is no separate confirm action on Xbox. Four new literal strings (the locked row's own label, three legend verbs) translated into German in the same change, landing in the main repo (`assets/resource/mainui_german.txt`) rather than the submodule. **Codex review found and fixed four real defects across two rounds, all before this PR opened.** Round 1, first (high): the filtered action list at first only covered Standard's own 15 commands, not the real three-scheme union - a Legacy player saw their own Back/D-pad-up/D-pad-down rows ("pause"/"impulse 201"/"lastinv") hidden entirely while Standard-only rows ("save quick"/slot1-4) showed as unbound; fixed by adding the three missing Legacy-only commands (now reflected in the list above). Round 1, second (high): `UnbindCommand()`'s stock, verbatim-carried-over prefix match (`strncmp`) meant clearing "+attack" also cleared any key bound to "+attack2" (its own bound string starts with the same seven characters) - latent on PC (rarely reachable there) but made ROUTINE here, since every scheme binds both together by design and X (Clear) makes wiping one keystroke away; fixed with an exact, case-insensitive match (matching `LookupBoundKeys`'s own already-exact comparison), guarded `#if XASH_XBOX` per this ledger's own policy since it is a genuine upstream-quality fix not yet submitted upstream. Round 1, third (high): the `Hide()` override first matched the PC "Cancel" button's own revert-to-last-saved-config semantics (`exec keyboard`) instead of "OK"'s persist - with no separate confirm action on Xbox, every rebind/clear/scheme-reset was silently discarded the moment the player backed out; fixed to run `host_writeconfig` instead (now reflected above). Round 2 (high): `EnterGrabMode()` used to clear an already-full (two-key) command's bindings BEFORE the new key was confirmed, so cancelling the grab (Escape, or Start via the round-1 lock) still left the row unbound with nothing restored, and the now-persisting `Hide()` above then wrote that damage to disk on the very next exit - reachable on every Standard-scheme install, since Fire/Secondary Fire are always dual-bound by design. Fixed by moving the clear out of `EnterGrabMode()` and into the grab dialog's own `KeyUp()`, atomically with an actually-accepted replacement, guarded `#if XASH_XBOX` (PC's own long-standing, unreported version of this same latent bug is untouched). Main-repo round 1 (high): the shared `LookupBoundKeys()`/`UnbindCommand()` scan EVERY physical key number - keyboard and mouse included - so a row's displayed keys, its "already full" check, and Clear/Reassign could all see or erase a keyboard/mouse binding for the same command (a typical config already has `+attack` on CTRL/MOUSE1, stock defaults, in addition to whatever a scheme's own two trigger keys are) - reassigning or clearing Fire from this screen could silently wipe CTRL and MOUSE1 too, well outside anything this screen ever shows. Fixed with two new gamepad-scoped helpers, `LookupBoundGamepadKeys()`/`UnbindGamepadCommand()`, both restricted to the engine's own contiguous gamepad-key block (`K_JOY1`..`K_TOUCHPAD`, `keydefs.h`) - used for every row's displayed keys (recomputed after filtering, not just kept from the base class's own global lookup), the grab dialog's own already-full check before an accepted reassignment, and X (Clear). `UnbindCommand()` itself reverts to its exact original stock form (PC-only now, nothing on Xbox calls it any more) - the round-1 exact-match fix moves into `UnbindGamepadCommand()` instead, which already needed to be exact-match from the start. Main-repo round 2 (high): the grab dialog's own accept path still took ANY key except Start/Escape/console keys, including keyboard arrow keys a stick synthesizes while this dialog is open (`Joy_ProcessStick`'s own menu-navigation hat emulation, `engine/client/input/in_joy.c`) - accepting one would clear the row's real gamepad binding(s) (the pre-replacement check already ran) and bind a keyboard arrow key instead, which the row's own gamepad-only display then hid completely, leaving the action with no visible, clearable binding at all. Fixed by rejecting any captured key that fails `IsGamepadKey()`, alongside the existing Start rejection. **Corrected in main-repo review round 2, not just "not yet reachable"**: an earlier draft of this entry claimed nothing calls this screen yet, but `ADD_MENU(menu_controls, CMenuControls, UI_Controls_Menu)` (unchanged by this branch) registers `menu_controls` as its own console command regardless of any button (`BaseMenu.h:281-287`/`BaseMenu.cpp:1243-1247`), and divergence #76 already established the Xbox dev console itself is real and reachable - so this screen, including its live bind mutation and `host_writeconfig` persistence, is reachable TODAY by typing that command, not gated behind the still-unbuilt Controller screen. This is not new exposure this branch creates: `menu_controls` was already a live Xbox console command reaching whatever this file contained before this branch too (#76's own audit left `Controls.cpp` unhidden, by design - it is a real, reshaped-for-Xbox screen, not one of the hidden PC-only ones). **Live pad verification through that command is genuinely owed and NOT yet done** - matching this ledger's own repeated precedent (#67's multiple "NOT verified live" entries, #82's "owed before this model's own Controller/Customize screens ship") of merging real, already-reviewed correctness work with an explicitly-tracked live-verification gap rather than blocking on a full boot-and-interact cycle for an intermediate sub-screen; closing it belongs with the Controller screen itself (this item's next and last branch), once a real on-screen path reaches this same code the console command already does. No deadzone or rumble-duration rows, matching the spec's explicit "drop these" call. Also adds one `#if XASH_XBOX`-guarded forward declaration, `void UI_ControllerSettings_Menu( void );`, to the shared (upstream-maintained) `BaseMenu.h` - every `UI_*_Menu` function in this codebase is declared there, with no per-file alternative, so a caller cannot reference this screen by name without it. Added now rather than deferred to the branch that actually wires a button to it, because this session's own reordering (`fork-plan.md`) already made building this screen ahead of its caller the deliberate plan - the declaration is the same kind of forward reference as the screen itself. **Codex review found a real destructive bug**: stock Gamepad.cpp also exposed a separate 'Invert' checkbox for the horizontal (yaw) axis (its own `invYaw`), and this screen's first draft dropped that CONTROL per the design (one "Invert look" row, vertical only) but also silently dropped the STATE - `SaveAndPopMenu()` always wrote `joy_yaw` as a positive magnitude, so an existing player with yaw already inverted would lose it the moment they opened this screen and pressed Done with nothing else changed. Fixed by reading and remembering `joy_yaw`'s sign in `GetConfig()` (`m_bYawWasInverted`, no UI control) and re-applying it unchanged on save - the control is gone, the data is not. **Codex review round 2 found the same class of bug one layer deeper**: the round-1 fix preserved yaw's SIGN but not its MAGNITUDE - merging two independently-adjustable sensitivities into one slider is this item's own intended simplification, but with `joy_pitch=100`/`joy_yaw=40` (a real reachable state, e.g. a save from before this fork), merely opening this screen and pressing Done with nothing touched still overwrote yaw to 100. Fixed by remembering both original magnitudes and the slider's own initial position in `GetConfig()`, and only unifying pitch/yaw to the slider's current value in `SaveAndPopMenu()` if that slider actually moved - otherwise each cvar keeps its own original magnitude, with only the sign changing if "Invert look" was toggled. The merge is now something the player's own action causes, not a side effect of visiting the screen at all Six new literal strings (heading, two labels, three status lines) translated into German in the same change (`assets/resource/mainui_german.txt`, this fork's own established item-13 discipline), landing in the main repo rather than the submodule since that file lives here. **Not yet reachable from any menu** - the Controller screen that will open it (this item's Branch 3) is still unbuilt; this branch's own compile verification: a full `tools/xbox-mainui-sweep.sh` (86/86 clean, up from 85) against the pre-fix version of this file, then, after the yaw-invert fix below, a direct single-file recompile (`tools/xbox-cxx.sh` with the sweep's own include/define flags - the full sweep resets this submodule's checkout to whatever commit the MAIN repo currently pins, which does not include this branch until its own pointer-bump PR merges, so it cannot re-verify an in-progress submodule branch's own later commits). | 82 | `3rdparty/mainui/ControllerSchemes.h`/`.cpp` (new, mainui submodule, `xm1/controller-schemes`): the shared model XM.1 item 5's Controller/Customize screens will build on - four controller schemes (Standard/Southpaw/Legacy/Custom), each a complete bind-table-plus-`joy_axis_binding` profile, applied and detected through the existing cvar/bind systems, never a second store. `UI_ApplyControllerScheme()` issues one `bind` per table entry, sets `joy_axis_binding`, and re-pins Start to `cancelselect` unconditionally - against the real hardware's own USB HID driver, not guessed: `deps/nxdk/lib/sdl/SDL2/src/joystick/xbox/SDL_xboxjoystick.c:532-533` maps Black to `XINPUT_GAMEPAD_RIGHT_SHOULDER` (`R1_BUTTON`) and White to `XINPUT_GAMEPAD_LEFT_SHOULDER` (`L1_BUTTON`) - the exact ambiguity `fork-plan.md:4726-4730` itself flagged as unverified and warned "Standard silently binds Fire to a button the pad never sends" if guessed wrong. `+speed`/`+attack2` were also each stock-bound from three different physical inputs. **Legacy keeps the stock defaults verbatim** (including those same quirks - Black already meant Fire there, White duplicates Crouch), so an existing player's binds keep working under a name. **Southpaw is Standard with only `joy_axis_binding` changed** (`"sfpyrl"` → `"ypfsrl"`) - swapping which STICK does movement vs. look (fixed by SDL's own physical axis order, `engine/client/input/in_joy.c:311-335`), not the axes within a stick. **One judgment call this branch made, not the spec**: which of `slot1`-`slot10` (`deps/hlsdk/cl_dll/ammo.cpp:276-285`) each D-pad direction opens - `fork-plan.md` item 5 names "D-pad" for this row but not which slot goes where; this branch chose 1/2/3/4 clockwise from up, noted in-code as easy to revisit in review. **Codex review round 2 found a real bug live in this branch's own history**: `UI_ApplyControllerScheme()` changing `joy_axis_binding` (e.g. applying Southpaw while a stick is still displaced from navigating to that button) left the engine's own cached per-logical-axis analog value unreset - SDL only fires an axis-motion event when a physical axis's raw value actually changes, so releasing that stick AFTER the swap routes the "back to centre" event to its NEW logical slot, never to the old one, leaving the old slot's stale nonzero value driving movement/look every frame from then on. Fixed in the engine, not here, since `joyaxis[]` is `engine/client/input/in_joy.c`'s own static state, unreachable from mainui: `Joy_FinalizeMove`'s existing `FCVAR_CHANGED` block, which already re-parses `joy_axis_binding` into `joyaxesmap[]` whenever it changes. **A first version of this exact fix was a plain `memset(joyaxis, 0, sizeof(joyaxis))` - Codex review caught that this is itself wrong, not just incomplete**: `JOY_AXIS_RT`/`LT` derive a DIGITAL `Key_Event` from crossing a threshold (`Joy_ProcessTrigger`), and `JOY_AXIS_SIDE`/`FWD` derive a synthesized menu hat direction the same way (`Joy_ProcessStick`'s own `Joy_GetHatValueForAxis`/`Joy_HatMotionEvent` call, gated to `key_menu`/`key_console` - exactly the state every caller of `UI_ApplyControllerScheme()` is always in). Overwriting their cached state directly erases the history those two functions' own edge detection depends on, so a trigger or stick already held past its threshold at the moment of the remap has its FUTURE release silently swallowed by `Joy_AxisMotionEvent`'s own already-existing "value unchanged" early return - leaving that key's down state latched with nothing left to release it, a worse bug than the one being fixed (a permanently-stuck fire/use/duck action, not merely stale movement). **The actual fix routes every axis through its OWN existing process function with value 0** (`Joy_ProcessTrigger(JOY_AXIS_RT, 0)`, `..._LT, 0)`, `Joy_ProcessStick(JOY_AXIS_SIDE/FWD/PITCH/YAW, 0)`) instead of touching `joyaxis[]` directly - reusing each function's own already-correct edge detection so a currently-active trigger or hat correctly fires its release exactly as it would for a real physical release to zero, while PITCH/YAW (which derive no digital key from their value at all) are left cleanly zeroed either way. **Guarded `#if XASH_XBOX`, not shipped unguarded** - Codex review's own third finding: this project's own divergence policy (this ledger's "Format" section, item 5) forbids changing behaviour with no `#ifdef` unless the fix has already gone upstream, which this has not. The bug this closes is not conceptually Xbox-specific (any platform that changes `joy_axis_binding` at runtime could hit it), but no PC code path does that today either, so guarding costs nothing real yet and keeps this a proper, policy-compliant divergence instead of an unguarded universal behaviour change with no upstream submission behind it - worth upstreaming on its own merits (a real, narrow fix any Xash3D-FWGS fork with runtime axis remapping would want), but that submission is a separate, deliberate step, not something to fold into shipping this fork's own feature. **Verified by compiling only** (`tools/xbox-sweep.sh engine/client/input`, 6/6 clean) - not yet exercised live (hold a trigger or a hat direction, apply a scheme mid-hold, confirm the key actually releases): owed before this model's own Controller/Customize screens ship, not before this branch's own PR, since nothing calls `UI_ApplyControllerScheme()` yet either way | XM.1 item 5 (`fork-plan.md`), Phase 1 of its own implementation plan (the model every later Controller/Customize screen branch depends on) | Form 2 for the new file pair (Xbox-only, `#if XASH_XBOX` after every include, matching this fork's own established convention - divergences.md #72 already found the opposite order compiles silently to an empty translation unit); the `in_joy.c` fix is Form 3, `#if XASH_XBOX` around the fix itself inside an otherwise-shared function (corrected from an earlier, wrong "Form 4, no #ifdef" draft - Codex review's own third finding: this ledger's own Format section forbids an unguarded behaviour change without an upstream submission behind it, which this does not have) | Low for the new file pair - compiles to nothing on non-Xbox builds, and nothing calls either function yet (dead code from the linker's own perspective until a later branch wires it into a menu); Fixed by requiring `--base` explicitly whenever `--repo` is given (refusing to guess a submodule's own default branch name) rather than trying to auto-detect it, with a clear error naming the exact flag combination needed | Tooling only, no game or engine behaviour change | Form 1, one new option on an existing script, backward compatible (omitting `--repo` reproduces the exact prior behaviour) | None - a general tooling capability, not a divergence to converge back on |
| 80 | Phase 3 of #77's own plan, closing entry #76's still-open claim: `tools/verify-entry-76.py` (new) and a new `Scenario.cursor_and_window()` in `tools/xemu-pad-scenario.py`, built on #78/#79's harness, navigate Main → Options → Controls and Main → Options → Joystick with a real synthetic pad press, screenshotting each. **Three real navigation surprises, all found only by actually running the script, not by reading source**: (1) Main.cpp's own cursor sequence from a fresh boot is `0,1,3,4,5,7` (AdjustCursor skips indices 2 and 6 - always-hidden items consuming array slots), so Options (cursor 7) needs FIVE Down presses from Main, not three as a naive count of `AddButton` calls up to the Options button would suggest. (2) Options, Controls.cpp AND Gamepad.cpp all call `AddItem(banner)` - a decorative picture, `Configuration.cpp:66`, `Controls.cpp:246`, `Gamepad.cpp:234` - BEFORE their first real button; `AddItem` puts it in the exact same array `m_iCursor` indexes (`ItemsHolder.cpp:529`), so every button on these three screens sits ONE INDEX HIGHER than a naive count of `AddButton` calls suggests. The banner isn't skipped the way Main's truly-hidden items are - the cursor CAN rest on it, pressing A there just does nothing since a bare picture has no click handler - which is exactly what made this look like Main's own hidden-slot pattern at first. Confirmed mapping: Options 0=banner, 1=Controls, 2=Audio, 3=Video, 4=Joystick, 5=Game, 6=Done; Controls.cpp (Xbox) 0=banner, 1=GameUI_UseDefaults, 2=GameUI_OK, 3=GameUI_Cancel; Gamepad.cpp (Xbox) 0=banner, 1=Controls (button), 2=Done. (3) **A first live test of this script's own "activate and check where it lands" logic (Codex review, below) found a genuine bug this offset caused**: checking cursor 1 as "OK" and activating it instead popped a "Reset all actions to their default keys?" dialog - `GameUI_UseDefaults`'s own callback (`Controls.cpp:247`), proving the check had the wrong index (1, not 2) rather than OK doing anything unexpected. Re-verified live twice more after the index fix, once per screen, isolated from the rest of the script: activating Controls' cursor 2 and Joystick's cursor 2 each correctly closed back to Options, same window pointer, at the outer cursor each screen was entered from. **Codex review, MUST FIX**: the first draft only checked that the expected item's own cursor number matched, then declared PASS without ever activating it - a restored Adv. Controls/Gyroscope row at that same index would read the identical cursor number, so the check could not actually catch the regression it existed to catch. Fixed by activating the item and requiring the guest to land back on the SAME `CMenuBaseWindow*` Options itself is (compared by pointer via the new `cursor_and_window()`, not merely by cursor number) - OK/Done are terminal actions that close their screen and return to Options, while Adv. Controls/Gyroscope would open a third, different window instead. **#78's own "three steps for two presses" auto-repeat finding reproduced again during this work**, at the SAME 100 ms hold that had looked safe for isolated single presses; a longer **settle** between presses (400 ms, not the Scenario default 100 ms) is what actually held up under repeated live testing, not a shorter hold. **Codex review round 2 found a real, if narrower, gap in the round-1 fix**: confirming index 2 activates to OK/Done proves the forbidden row is not AT that index, but does not by itself rule out one inserted anywhere else on the screen (a hypothetical reordering that kept OK/Done at index 2 but moved Adv. Controls/Gyroscope after it would pass unnoticed). Fixed for Controls by also activating Cancel (index 3, `Controls.cpp:65-69` - `Cancel()` is also just `Hide()`, the same terminal action as OK), closing the gap for that screen entirely: nothing can sit between OK and Cancel, or after Cancel, without one of the two checks catching it. Justified rather than extended for Joystick: Gamepad.cpp continues past Done with six real focusable axis-binding buttons and several sliders that do not close to Options when activated, so the same proof does not apply there without trading a cheap, reliable check for an expensive, ambiguous one; Gyroscope's own guard has only ever existed BETWEEN Controls and Done in this tree, a shape the existing index-2 check already catches, and the screenshot remains the closing proof for the axis-bind region and for the hypothetical Codex named. The script's own final PASS message was reworded to say this explicitly rather than imply full automatic closure. **A live run of the Cancel addition then found a real, separate, already-known-class issue**: the full script failed once with Controls's own Cancel slot reading cursor 2, not 3, after three Downs from a re-entry - not a logic bug (re-entry resetting to 0 was independently confirmed live immediately after, isolated from the rest of the script) but a recurrence of the settle-timing flakiness #78 already documented, surfacing even at the 400ms settle that had looked reliable everywhere else it was tried. Added an explicit re-entry-cursor check so a future run tells this apart from a real reset failure instead of guessing. **Every individual check in the corrected script has since been independently confirmed live, in isolation, with a 100% pass rate across every attempt** - Main to Options, Options to Controls, Controls' OK and Cancel both closing to Options, Controls' own re-entry resetting to 0, and Joystick's Done closing to Options - even though full end-to-end runs of the whole 11-check script have occasionally hit either the settle-timing flake above or the GDB-stub desyncs below. **Verified live 2026-09-19, end to end, fully scripted, against a freshly-booted classic disc** (`.sweep/game-hl/xbe-build.exe`/`.map`, xemu v0.8.136): `python tools/verify-entry-76.py` exited 0, PASS - both screenshots (`.sweep/verify-entry-76/controls.png`, `.../joystick.png`) show exactly `Use Defaults`/`OK`/`Cancel` and `Controls`/`Done` respectively, no Adv. Controls or Gyroscope row on either, and every one of Controls' OK/Cancel and Joystick's Done independently confirmed (by window pointer) to actually close back to Options rather than open anything else. Several runs across both review rounds hung indefinitely on a `cursor` read, either after an unrelated exploratory script was force-killed mid-`GdbRemote` session, or after prolonged GDB-stub use over many minutes desynced the connection outright (`error: no qSupported reply within 2.0s`) - not a bug in this entry's own tooling; the fix each time was relaunching xemu, not touching the script | XM.1 tooling (`fork-plan.md`'s synthetic-pad-input section, Phase 3); closes #76's own "NOT verified" line | Form 1, one new dev-only Python script under `tools/` plus one new method on an existing one, and corrections to #76's ledger text - no engine, SDK or fork-plan-numbered-item source touched | None - nothing here ships to a disc or changes runtime behaviour for any platform | None - the verification is complete; #76 itself now carries the live result, and hardware confirmation (per AGENTS.md, xemu is not validation) remains the only thing still owed |
| 79 | Two more real bugs in Phase 2's own harness, both from Codex review round 2, fixed before merge - not a new tool, corrections to #78's own code. **A timed-out command's outcome was reported as a plain, uniform failure even when `serve` had already claimed it** (deleted its command file, exactly what a real dispatch does before it starts) and was simply slow to answer - a caller could not tell "never happened, safe to retry" apart from "already in flight or already landed, retrying could double-apply it," the second case being live ammunition for a false verdict. `_send()` now returns three exit codes instead of two: 0 ok, 1 never claimed (the command file is still there and removable when the client gives up), 2 claimed but never answered (the command file is already gone - outcome genuinely unknown). `tools/xemu-pad-scenario.py`'s `Scenario` raises a distinct `ScenarioStateUnknownError` (a `ScenarioError` subclass) for exit code 2, so a caller cannot accidentally treat "unknown" the same way it treats "definitely failed." **A dispatch exception between pressing a button and releasing it left the virtual pad stuck holding that button down** for the rest of the `serve` session, silently contaminating every later press and cursor read - `int(hold_ms)` accepted a negative value with nothing stopping it from reaching `time.sleep()`, which raises `ValueError` on one, after `press_button()`/`update()` had already fired and with the release call never reached. Fixed two ways: `hold_ms < 0` is rejected before the button is touched at all, and the sleep-then-release sequence is wrapped so release is attempted in a `finally` regardless of what raises in between. Both fixes have their own regression tests reproducing the exact failure shape review named (`tools/test_xemu_pad.py`: a command claimed-but-unanswered times out with exit code 2 specifically, not 1; a negative hold never touches the gamepad at all; a simulated exception during the hold still releases the button) - one of these tests itself had a real ordering bug caught while writing this entry (an assertion before its own background thread's `t.join()`, which on failure would have orphaned that thread into a later, unrelated test's already-deleted temp directory - fixed by joining before asserting, the same discipline the other thread-based tests in this file already followed) | Same as #78 - XM.1 tooling, Phase 2, `fork-plan.md`'s synthetic-pad-input section | Form 1, corrections within Phase 2's own new files, already covered by #78's own "no engine/SDK/disc" scope | None | Superseded by #78 once this entry's own fixes are folded in - kept separate here because they were a distinct, later review round on already-committed code, not part of the original Phase 2 diff |
| 78 | The synthetic-pad-input harness itself, Phase 2 of #77's own plan: three new dev-only tools, no engine or SDK changes. `tools/xemu-pad.py` owns ViGEmBus/`vgamepad` exclusively (kept out of `tools/xemu-debug.py`, whose own docstring states "Stdlib only... No pip" - a pip, Windows-only, native-DLL-backed dependency does not belong bolted onto a client anyone might want for read-only GDB-remote debugging). A `serve`/client split, not one-shot presses: the virtual pad exists only while its owning process is alive, and #77 already found losing and regaining that OS-level device mid-session left xemu unresponsive to it until relaunched - one persistent `serve` process holds the pad open for a whole test session, and `press`/`axis`/`quit` are thin clients writing one command file and reading one matching result file, the same file-based scheme #77's own spike already proved reliable, now under a per-command unique ID rather than one shared command/result file pair (Codex review round 1, MUST FIX: a shared, append-only result file let a command that outlived its own client's timeout have its eventual result claimed by a LATER, unrelated command as its own - a false verdict, exactly the failure class this measurement tooling exists to exclude - and let two concurrent clients overwrite each other's command outright; fixed by giving every command its own cmd_<id>.txt/result_<id>.txt pair, written via atomic os.replace(), so a client only ever reads the one result file its own ID names, and two clients can never collide on one file). `tools/xemu-debug.py` gains one new **read-only** command, `cursor` (`CMenuItemsHolder::m_iCursor`, the pad-focused item's own index on whatever mainui screen is up), reached by a pointer chase from `uiStatic` - `uiStatic.menu` (a `CWindowStack`, embedded) -> its `stack` (a `CUtlLinkedList<CMenuBaseWindow*>`, also embedded) -> the `active` node, validated the same way `CUtlLinkedList::IsValidIndex` does (`utllinkedlist.h:316-319`) -> the `CMenuBaseWindow*` it holds, which is also its `CMenuItemsHolder*` subobject (single, non-virtual inheritance, confirmed from the headers) -> `m_iCursor`. No GDB write packets added anywhere - input goes through `xemu-pad.py`'s entirely separate transport, so `GdbRemote`'s read-only design is untouched. A new `tools/xbox-offsetof-cxx.sh`, sibling to `tools/xbox-offsetof.sh`, makes the chase's own offsets possible at all: every field it reads is `protected`/`private` on a C++ class, and plain `offsetof()` on one is a hard compile error, not a silent success - the new script's probe `#define`s `private`/`protected` to `public` before `#include`-ing the target header (the real project headers are never touched, only this throwaway probe TU's own view of them), through `tools/xbox-cxx.sh` instead of `tools/xbox-cc.sh` so template instantiation and this project's real C++ flags apply. `tools/xemu-pad-scenario.py` composes the two into a `press, wait, verify` scenario class, deliberately thin - a handful of methods (`press`/`axis`/`wait`/`cursor`/`assert_cursor`/`screenshot`), not a framework - plus a `demo` CLI proving the whole chain end to end. Its own `screenshot()` method shells out to `xemu-debug.py`'s `screenshot` command rather than duplicating `cmd_fbdump`'s framebuffer-read logic (Codex review round 1, MUST FIX: `--host`/`--port` are top-level argparse args, added before `add_subparsers()`, and this method's first version placed them AFTER the `screenshot` subcommand name - argparse rejects that ordering outright, so every call failed regardless of host/port defaults; fixed, and the exact generated argv is now its own pure, tested function) | XM.1 tooling (`fork-plan.md`'s new synthetic-pad-input section, Phase 2). **The `#define private public` trick's safety, confirmed rather than assumed**: probed a genuinely public field (`CMenuBaseItem::iFlags`) both through the macro and through a plain, unmodified probe with no macro at all - both agree exactly (offset 200) - access specifiers carry no ABI/layout weight in this project's compiler (Clang, Itanium C++ ABI) for the non-virtual, non-multiply-inherited classes this chase touches. **Every offset in the chase is a real, live-probed number against this fork's own build**, not a guess: `CWindowStack.active` @+40, `.stack` @+0 (`sizeof`=40); `CUtlLinkedList<CMenuBaseWindow*>.m_Memory` @+0 (`sizeof`=16), `.m_TotalElements` @+32; `CUtlMemory<ListElem_t>.m_pMemory` @+4 (inherited from `CUtlMemoryBase`, itself preceded by `m_unSizeOfElements` - confirmed by probing the exact instantiated type directly, not assumed from the base class alone); `ListElem_t` `sizeof`=12, `.m_Element`@+0/`.m_Previous`@+4/`.m_Next`@+8; `CMenuItemsHolder.m_iCursor` @+416. `uiStatic`'s own map symbol is not the literal string "uiStatic" - it has ordinary C++ linkage (no `extern "C"` anywhere in `BaseMenu.h`), so lld-link's map carries its decorated name (`?uiStatic@@3UuiStatic_t@@A`, read directly out of a real build's own `.map` file) - `cmd_cursor` searches for a symbol ending in that suffix rather than assuming either name. **Verified live, end to end, against the classic disc's own real build** (`.sweep/game-hl/xbe-build.exe`/`.map`, the same pair the currently-merged-to-main build already produces): `serve` held a virtual pad open, `xemu-pad-scenario.py demo` read the Main menu's initial cursor (0, New Game), pressed Down once and confirmed it moved (to 1, Training Room) via a live `resolve_cursor` read - not a screenshot, an actual memory value - then pressed Down twice more and read a final value. **One real, unexplained finding, not chased further this pass**: that first single press moved the cursor by exactly 1, but the next two presses moved it from 1 to 4 - three steps for two presses, not two. Most likely UI auto-repeat firing during the (currently placeholder) 200 ms hold rather than a bug in the read itself - the single-press case behaved exactly as expected - but this is recorded rather than quietly averaged away, and needs calibrating (probably a shorter default hold, or reading the actual auto-repeat threshold from source) before Phase 3 can assert exact step counts rather than just "it moved". `mainui`/engine sweeps do not apply (no C++/engine files changed, only new/changed `tools/*.py` and one new `tools/*.sh`); 15 new offline unit tests cover this PR's own logic without a live emulator: `tools/test_xemu_debug.py`'s new `Cursor` class (6 - a valid single-node list, a later node in a longer list, an empty stack, an out-of-range active index, a node failing `IsValidIndex`, a null window pointer, all raising rather than returning a plausible-looking wrong number); `tools/test_xemu_pad.py` (button/axis name validation, `_dispatch`'s own per-command logic against a fake gamepad, and - directly reproducing both Codex round-1 findings as regression tests - a slow response arriving after its own client's timeout proven never claimable by a later command, and two commands in flight at once proven never to share a command file); `tools/test_xemu_pad_scenario.py` (the `screenshot()` argv's exact ordering) | Form 1, three new/changed dev-only Python/shell tools under `tools/` - no engine, SDK or fork-plan-numbered-item source touched | None - nothing here ships to a disc or changes runtime behaviour for any platform | Phase 3 (`tools/verify-entry-76`) is done - see #80 - and confirmed the auto-repeat finding above needed a longer settle between presses, not a shorter hold, to actually go away |
| 77 | A feasibility spike, not shipped tooling: synthetic gamepad input can reach a running xemu guest (tested against v0.8.136, commit `fc24584`, built 2026-06-08 per its own `xemu.log`; the bundled SDL2's own exact version is not printed anywhere and stays unknown - this result is not yet confirmed to hold on any other xemu build) from an external Python script, via ViGEmBus (a Windows kernel-mode virtual-XInput driver, archived/EOL upstream since 2023 but still the practical current standard - `vgamepad`, its still-maintained Python wrapper, pushed as recently as this year) creating a virtual Xbox 360 controller that Windows enumerates identically to real hardware (`VID_045E&PID_028E`, confirmed via Device Manager) | To close the "NOT verified live... needs a pad" gap named repeatedly across items 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12 and 13 - every one of those screens has only ever been boot-screenshotted, never actually pad-navigated. Two blockers found and closed, both the kind no amount of reading xemu's docs would have surfaced - only live experimentation did: (1) **xemu silently drops all controller input whenever its own window lacks OS focus**, unless `[input] background_input_capture = true` is set in `xemu.toml` - the actual root cause of three consecutive, byte-for-byte-identical (real pixel diff, not eyeballing) failed round-trip attempts before this was found, not a device or binding problem at all. (2) **The virtual pad's SDL GUID, at least across these two specific SDL builds, did not agree** - an external `pysdl2` probe (a separate SDL2 2.32.10 build) enumerated the same device as `0300b9695e0400008e02000000007200`, but xemu's own bundled SDL (an unknown, different version/build) accepted only a GUID its own "press a button to bind" capture UI produced (`0300fa675e0400008e02000014017801`), which does not match the probe's value past the shared vendor/product bytes. This is one confirmed mismatch between two specific builds, not proof the GUID can never be computed externally - it is not yet known whether a probe built against xemu's own exact SDL version (or reading its GUID-generation logic directly) would agree instead. Until that is tested, capture through xemu's own UI is this fork's working fallback, not a claimed general limitation of SDL GUIDs. **What worked, concretely**: `vgamepad.VX360Gamepad()` creates a device with a GUID stable across recreation in the same session (tested twice); driving it via a small file-polled command loop in a long-lived Python process (one `press <button> <hold_ms>` command per line, written by a separate controller script) reached the guest reliably once both blockers above were fixed - confirmed live, D-pad Down x3 followed by A moved Main's focus and it was observably different from where it started. **What is NOT yet confirmed**: cross-reboot GUID stability (only tested within one Windows session); precise end-to-end latency (not measured, only "fast enough to watch"); whether the file-poll command loop is fast/robust enough for a scripted assertion-based harness rather than a human watching the window - all three are Phase 2's job, not this spike's | Form 1 - no project files changed by the spike itself; the two blockers found are pure xemu/OS configuration facts, not fork code | None - nothing shipped yet. `background_input_capture = true` and the captured GUID live only in the local, gitignored `xemu.toml`, not tracked anywhere in this repo | Converges into `tools/pad-input-harness` (Phase 2): a purpose-built input tool (`tools/xemu-pad.py`) and a read-only cursor-position command added to `tools/xemu-debug.py`, per `fork-plan.md`'s new synthetic-pad-input section, this spike's own DONE note |

| 75 | `menus/LoadGame.cpp`'s `CMenuSavesListModel::Update()`: the loop that strips trailing whitespace from a save's title used `size_t len` with a `len >= 0` exit condition - always true for an unsigned type, so an empty title (`strlen(title)-1` underflowing to `SIZE_MAX`) made the loop read `title[]` backwards from far out of bounds instead of doing nothing. Changed `len` to `int`, cast `title[len]` to `unsigned char` before `isspace()` (the same defensive cast the rest of this codebase already uses for that function) | Not an XM.1 item - a pre-existing bug (`git blame`: upstream, 2024-06-19, commit `54a9ff2`, present in the vendored clone too) found while investigating a user report of Load Game and Save Game freezing open in xemu on a build with real save data. `title` is empty whenever a save's own comment (`SV_GetSaveComment`) is an empty string with no leading `[` - not yet confirmed as the specific save/comment shape behind that report, but the loop is provably wrong on its own terms regardless of that: an unsigned loop counter can never satisfy `>= 0` as an exit condition, and `strlen()-1` on an empty string is a classic underflow. **Verified the mechanism, not the live symptom**: a standalone repro of the exact loop body (MSYS2 clang, `-O0`, an empty local buffer) confirmed `len >= 0` never stops an unsigned counter and the underflowed read lands one byte before the array - in that isolated test, on stack content that happened to read as non-whitespace, so it broke after one out-of-bounds read rather than continuing; the real `comment[256]` buffer's neighbouring stack content is unpredictable and untested, and could as easily keep walking. Two new tracked assets: `assets/gfx/shell/duke.tga` (280x208, opaque) and `assets/resource/XboxDuke.png` (1120x830, the master, 173 KiB) | XM item 0's *Animated title* bullet and its new step 7 (`fork-plan.md`). The title half exists because `tools/asset-studio`'s own `UiPacker.cpp` comment above `resizeBilinear` flags exactly this gap and leaves it: the background it already bakes is opaque, so a straight RGBA resize is correct there, while the title's eleven layers carry real non-uniform alpha, where colour under a fully transparent texel is undefined (retail's layers carry black) and averaging raw RGB across a soft edge rims every glow with a dark halo. Both tools resize in premultiplied space and convert back. **The title arithmetic the plan estimated is now confirmed against the real HL25 install: 5.24 MiB retail → 2.05 MiB baked** by `ref_soft`'s own cost formula (`r_image.c:235-255`), landing on the plan's own "~2.1 MiB if the mips cannot be dropped after all" branch - which settles that item's requirement (b), the `ref_soft` no-mips change, as a further ~0.3 MiB optimisation rather than a prerequisite. The layer list is fixed rather than globbed because `CMenuAnimatedBanner::TryLoad` is all-or-nothing (`AnimatedBanner.cpp:49-84`): a partial bake turns the title off entirely instead of dropping one element. Output row order was read out of `img_tga.c:137-148` (descriptor bit 5 set means the loader fills forward and the file's first row is the image's top; clear means it starts at the last row and steps back) and checked against retail's own headers rather than assumed from the TGA spec, so a baked layer is interchangeable with the file it replaces on the same `PIC_Load` path. The Duke half exists because the Controller screen needs one picture with no retail ancestor, making it the first menu asset whose source this fork owns and the first that could become the one file nobody can regenerate - hence the generator and its byte-for-byte `--check`. **Format and resolution decided by measurement, not preference**: the master arrived as an 8600x4800 TGA, 165 MB, of which 4582x3394 is content and the rest is the drawing program's canvas padding - 954 times the bytes of the same picture, for a file no build step reads - so a master is tracked as content-cropped PNG RGBA at four times the baked width, and game art stays uncompressed TGA like every other asset on these discs. PNG would work on the console too (`img_utils.c:99-101` registers the decoder, `img_png.c` is in the Xbox build) at roughly a third the disc bytes, but inflate wants the compressed and decompressed buffers live at once, and on 64 MB that peak outweighs 140 KiB of DVD. 280x208 was chosen by baking 200, 240 and 280 and comparing at 1:1, the way a 640x480 CRT shows them: at 200 the face-button letters and the XBOX wordmark stop resolving. Flattened to opaque rather than keeping alpha, since the HL25 tiles behind it are solid black (measured, item 0 step 3) and the opaque one skips the second full-resolution alpha plane `r_image.c:254-255` allocates - 228 KiB on disc, 151 KiB resident. `legend_button.tga` is checked by shape (square, pure white wherever visible, one radially symmetric alpha island that never grows outward again - what `Legend.cpp:157` relies on when it tints one image into six glyphs) rather than by bytes, because it was made by a different tool and shipped on a verified disc, so a mismatch would mean "different tool", not "wrong file"; its edge is also softer than it looks (alpha solid to r=12.5, zero only at r=17.2, measured off the file) and `--regen-legend` reproduces that falloff so the option cannot quietly restyle something that already shipped. **What is NOT done, and is not claimed**: the title-bake tool is not wired into `tools/xbox-xiso.sh` yet, and no disc has been built with its output, so that memory number stays the uploader's formula applied to real baked dimensions, not a `memstats` heap delta. `duke.tga` IS now staged onto every Xbox disc build (`tools/xbox-xiso.sh`, divergence #83, verified with a real packed ISO and a byte-for-byte `cmp` against the staged copy) - but it still has no consumer, since the Controller screen (XM.1 item 5) that would load it is unported, so staging alone closes nothing yet. The title bake also cannot fix its own blocker: `CMenuAnimatedBanner` reads each layer's true size from its TGA header (`UI_GetTargaImageDimensions`, `:30-47`) and multiplies by `ScreenWidth/1024` when drawing, so baked layers - already at scale - would render at 0.39 of intended size until the fork skips that multiply. The factor is therefore recorded twice, for two readers, a distinction found by checking rather than assuming: `title-bake.json` beside the art is this tool's own manifest, for `--check` and for a human, while `resource/title_bake.txt` inside the overlay is a `TITLE_BAKE_SCALE` key and value - **`mainui` has no JSON parser at all**, it reads token streams through `EngFuncs::COM_ParseFile` exactly as `colors.lst` is read (`BaseMenu.cpp:1003-1020`), so the text marker is the only one the fork side can consume, and `--check` refuses a bake whose two copies disagree. **Run end to end, 2026-09-18**: both bakes into one overlay (`tools/asset-studio --bake-menu-art` for the background, this tool for the title), 20 files and 3.0 MB, replayed against `xbox-xiso.sh`'s own `validate_menu_bake` rules - the `resolution 640 480` line, every referenced tile present, each one exactly `18 + w*h*4` bytes - and all six background tiles and eleven title layers pass. Not packed onto a disc | Form 1, dev-tooling and fork-owned assets only - no engine, SDK or vendored source touched. `tools/asset-studio`'s existing `--bake-menu-art` and its MPAKv6 modes are untouched | Low - the title-bake script is not part of any build and nothing invokes it yet. `duke.tga` is staged onto every Xbox disc build as of divergence #83, but starts costing runtime memory only when XM.1 item 5 ships and a menu actually loads it | Shrinks if `tools/asset-studio` ever grows a premultiplied resize path, at which point the title bake folds into `--bake-menu-art` as a second mode rather than staying a separate tool; the Duke generator is permanent, since it is the only source the diagram has |
| 73 | `3rdparty/mainui/controls/Legend.{h,cpp}` (new, owned fork): a shared `CMenuLegend` control every Xbox screen draws its own list of (glyph, verb) pairs from, always at the same real 640x480 position - A/B/X/Y as a filled disc (one new tintable asset, `assets/gfx/shell/legend_button.tga`) in the canvas's own pad colours with a white letter on top, the D-pad as a hollow cross outline and START as a dark-filled pill inside a grey border, with no letter or word inside it, both drawn from `UI_FillRect`/`UI_DrawRectangleExt` directly with no second asset (both shapes corrected 2026-09-18, see the end of this entry). Per-screen lists follow the canvas's own explicit ones where it gives one (LoadGame: A Load (Save) / X Delete / B Back; Pause: A Select / B Resume / START Resume) and its generic baseline elsewhere (A Select / B Back), with the D-pad glyph drawn first, not last, on the three screens that use it: "D-pad Move - Adjust" on Audio/Display (the D-pad moves the row selection too, not just the value) and "D-pad Move / A Toggle" on Game, whose three rows are checkboxes rather than buttons. **Real pixel coordinates, not this codebase's usual virtual 1024x768 layout space - found only by reading Main.cpp's own Xbox layout closely.** Every other Xbox screen's `SetCoord`/`SetRect` calls take plain literals in that virtual space with no conversion, relying on `uiStatic.scaleX/Y` to scale them back down at draw time; `Main.cpp`'s own Xbox `VidInit(bool)` is the one exception, explicitly computing `(404/480.0)*768.0` to anchor Options at a real target row, because a plain `404` there would land higher up the real screen than its face value suggests. The legend's own canvas-given position ("at 1x", y=438) is a real-pixel measurement in exactly that same sense, and has to land there regardless of which convention the hosting screen happens to use - so `SetRealCoord()` does that same conversion once, inside `CMenuLegend::VidInit()`, recomputed fresh from stored real-target fields on every call (never mutating `pos.x/y` in place, since `VidInit()` can run again on a later `Show()`). An earlier version of this wrongly assumed a real-pixel collision between the legend and Display's own "Video output" row and Game's own Done button, and moved both to make room - reverted once the real/virtual distinction above was understood, since those two elements' own uncorrected literal coordinates put them far higher up the real screen than a face-value reading suggests, nowhere near the legend's own real y=438. Codex review round 1 found two more real defects, both fixed before this PR opened. First (medium severity): LoadGame's own legend advertised "A Load (Save)" and "X Delete" unconditionally, but input goes to whichever control has focus first (`ItemsHolder.cpp`), and X-delete exists only inside `CMenuTable::KeyDown` (`Table.cpp`) - so A could just as easily cancel or delete depending on focus, and X did nothing at all unless the saves table specifically was focused. Fixed by changing A's verb to "Select" (the same focus-agnostic verb every other screen's legend already uses) and adding a `CMenuLoadGame::Think()` override that toggles the X entry's own visibility against `ItemAtCursor() == &savesList` once per frame - a new `CMenuLegend::SetVisible()` method, replacing the now-unused `SetVerb()` this fix made dead code. Second (medium severity, confidence 1): the new file and its `uiColorLegend`/`LEGEND_COLOR` additions had no `#if XASH_XBOX` guard at all, so both build systems (which glob every `controls/*.cpp` file unconditionally) compiled the whole class - inertly, but really - on every platform, contradicting this very entry's own "Xbox only" claim. Fixed by wrapping `Legend.h`/`.cpp` the same way `Pause.cpp` wraps itself (guard after the `#include`s, for the same transitively-reached-`XASH_XBOX` reason), and guarding `uiColorLegend`'s declaration, definition and `colors.lst` parsing branch - unlike `uiColorHeading`, which stays unconditional on its own established precedent, since nothing on Xbox reads `uiColorLegend` except this file. Codex review round 2 found two more real defects, both fixed before this PR opened. First: the round-1 fix above still wasn't enough - `CMenuTable::KeyDown` (`Table.cpp`) buzzes instead of deleting on an empty saves list, and in save mode the synthetic "new save" row (index 0) is deliberately not deletable either, so focus on the table alone still advertised Delete in cases where pressing X does nothing. Fixed by also checking the Delete button's own grayed state in `Think()` (`!(remove.iFlags & QMF_GRAYED)`) - reusing `UpdateGame()`'s own already-computed verdict for exactly this condition rather than duplicating its predicate. Second: the new literal legend keys ("Select", "Back", "Delete", "Adjust", "Resume") had no entries in `assets/resource/mainui_german.txt`, the one shipped translation file - `L()` falls back to English on a miss, so every German screen would have shown an English legend, a real regression against item 13's own already-proven localization. Fixed by adding all five keys with real German translations (Auswählen/Zurück/Löschen/Anpassen/Fortsetzen). Verified live: booted the classic disc to Main and read the rendered legend off a screenshot - a green "A Select" and a red "B Back" both draw exactly where `SetRealCoord` targets them, at the real bottom of the screen. `mainui` sweep clean (84/84); classic disc links clean (0 duplicate, 0 undefined) with the new control and asset staged and packed; the full engine sweep was not re-run, since nothing under `engine/` changed for this item. **Corrected against the design canvas itself, not just re-verified against notes about it**: asked whether the button glyphs could come from the design's own assets rather than generated primitives - the canvas has no bitmap button assets either (rendered it headless via Edge and inspected its own bundle: 5 PNGs total in the whole file, all wordmark art), so this control's own technique (plain colour discs) already matches. But reading the rendered canvas pixel by pixel found four real defects: Y's colour was believed transcribed wrong in an earlier note (`#f0a91c`), and a headless render sampled `#f0c21b` instead - **that finding was itself wrong and is reversed at the end of this entry**; Audio/Display's own D-pad glyph was drawn last with verb "Adjust" alone, the canvas puts it first with "Move - Adjust" (the D-pad moves the row selection too); Game had no D-pad entry at all and used "Select" for A, the canvas shows "D-pad Move / A Toggle / B Back" since its rows are checkboxes; the START glyph drew the word "START" inside the pill, the canvas draws an empty pill with the verb ("Resume") outside it like every other glyph. The D-pad shape itself stays an approximation (a filled plus, not the canvas's own slightly notched icon) - rect-only primitives were not chased further to close that specific gap. **All three of this paragraph's own loose ends closed 2026-09-18, after the user reported the D-pad reading on screen as "two crossed lines instead of the hollow cross", and an audit of every legend in the tree against every artboard found two more.** (1) The D-pad is now the hollow outline the canvas draws, built from the twelve edges of the plus polygon (the canvas's icon is a 24-unit box with corners at 4, 9, 15 and 20), each edge lengthened by the line thickness so the corners meet without a notch - rect-only primitives do close the gap after all; filling the plus and punching a hole in it never could, since what would show through is the menu background, an image this control knows nothing about. Verified by simulating the twelve calls at glyphSize 20 and 40 and looking at the result, a closed outline at both. (2) START gains the canvas's own fill and border (`.pb.st`: background `#222`, border 2px `#777`) instead of a bare outline in the legend's text colour - nearly invisible on the black menu background, but it is what keeps the pill reading as a button over the dimmed scene behind the pause screens. (3) **Y goes back to `#f0a91c`, and how it was wrong is the lesson.** This entry's own earlier correction above reached `#f0c21b` by rendering the canvas headless and reading a pixel; the artboards' own source declares `background: #f0a91c` in plain text in both of the two that draw a Y at all, and `#f0c21b` appears in no source anywhere in the canvas or this tree - **`fork-plan.md`'s own item 12 specified `#f0a91c` from the start, so the implementation had drifted from its own plan and this ledger then recorded the drift as a fix**. Corroborated independently against the master controller illustration this fork now bakes its Duke diagram from: its Y button's most common texel is `#f7a318`, six levels from `#f0a91c` and thirty-one from `#f0c21b` in green alone. A, B and X were re-read from the artboard source the same way and are unchanged. A rendered pixel is the design after a compositor has touched it; the declaration is the design. The same audit compared all eight screens' verb lists against their artboards and found them matching, with one deliberate exception already explained above (LoadGame's A says "Select", not the canvas's "Load", because focus decides what A does), and confirmed every verb string is present in the German translation. `mainui` sweep clean after the change (84/84). NOT verified: the rendered result on a disc, which needs a pad and a boot. Codex review (on the correction itself) found one more real defect: "Move - Adjust"'s own German translation ("Bewegen - Anpassen", noticeably longer) would have clipped against the fixed per-entry slot width `Draw()` used at the time, since `UI_DrawString` ellipsizes text that overflows a fixed box. Fixed by measuring each entry's own verb width live (`g_FontMgr->GetTextWideScaled()`, the same call `Action.cpp`'s own `VidInit` already uses to size a label from its text) and advancing a running x cursor by it, rather than assuming a fixed width fits every language - correct regardless of how long a future translation runs, not just the two checked here. NOT verified: the D-pad/START glyphs specifically and whether each screen's verb list matches its real pad behaviour, both needing a pad. **A genuine, unrelated tooling bug found and fixed along the way**: `tools/xbox-link-check.sh`'s manifest-drift check assumes `sweep-manifest.txt` never repeats a source line - false when an earlier `tools/xbox-sweep.sh` invocation is killed mid-run while its own truncate-and-append file handle is still open, interleaving late writes from the dead run into the next clean run's manifest; `comm -13`'s multiset comparison then reports each duplicate's second occurrence as a source "no longer exists," failing the baseline gate deterministically (confirmed with `sort \| uniq -d` on the manifest's own source column). No code change needed - a clean, uncontended sweep is the fix - but cost real time to trace, so recorded here for the next session that interrupts a sweep mid-run | Form 3, `#if XASH_XBOX` around one new file and small additions to eight already-Xbox-only screens, plus one new asset and one new `colors.lst` key staged only for Xbox discs | Low - guarded to Xbox only; every other platform's build never compiles the new file or stages the new asset/key | None named - a deliberate shared design element the canvas calls for, not something to converge back on |
| 72 | `3rdparty/mainui/menus/Pause.cpp` (new, owned fork, divergence #57): a single-player in-game pause screen, entirely `#if XASH_XBOX` since there is no stock in-game pause menu to diverge from - Resume Game / Save Game / Load Game / Options / Quit to Main Menu (through the existing `CMenuYesNoMessageBox`), a chapter title and difficulty annotation, `ui_renderworld` set to 1 on show and reversed in an overridden `Hide()` covering every exit path, `pause` toggled on show/hide only when this screen itself is the one that needs to change it (see Codex round 1, below), and `K_START_BUTTON` closing the menu the same way Resume does. `BaseMenu.cpp`'s `UI_UpdateMenu` (the one place that decides what opens when the menu system activates with nothing already showing) now opens Pause instead of Main whenever `CL_IsActive()` is true AND the session is really singleplayer (`host_serverstate` and `maxplayers == 1`, the same test Main.cpp's own `QuitDialogCb` already uses - see Codex round 2, below), and a per-frame poll (`UI_Pause_CheckClosed`) finishes this screen's own cleanup if it disappears some way other than its own `Hide()` | XM.1 item 10 (`fork-plan.md`). Both of the plan's own "measure before writing" prerequisites turned out to be already-complete GoldSrc machinery, not new engine work: `SV_Pause_f` (`sv_client.c:1435-1446,1630-1655`) toggles `sv.paused` and broadcasts `svc_setpause` to every client including a listen server's own local one, `sv_pausable` already defaults to `"1"`, and `CL_EmitEntities`'s own early-out on `cl.paused` (`cl_frame.c:1289`) freezes simulation while `V_RenderView` keeps drawing the scene regardless - the exact frozen-but-visible effect this screen needs. The plan's OWN premise for the dim was simply wrong, not merely undone: `DrawInGameBackground` already fills at `uiColorBlack` = `0x80000000`, 50% alpha, not the opaque black the plan assumed, so no change was needed there at all; the real gate is `ui_renderworld` (default `"0"`), the single flag `V_RenderView` checks to skip the 3D draw while a menu is up - already flipped by `AnimatedBanner.cpp`/`MovieBanner.cpp` for their own reasons, not new territory. Both facts were checked by reading the real render/pause path directly - a pause screen has no `memstats`-style number to measure, so this is verified by source reading alone, named as such rather than dressed up as a boot test. Chapter title is real, not dropped: an earlier version of this claimed classic Half-Life's SDK has no chapter-title concept at all, checked only against `deps/hlsdk/cl_dll/` (the client DLL, which never sees it) - Codex review round 1 corrected this. Worldspawn's own `chaptertitle` key (`deps/hlsdk/dlls/world.cpp:663-674`) fires a one-time client message at map start, not something to read back later, but `engine/server/sv_save.c`'s own `gTitleComments[]` - a complete mapname-prefix-to-title-token table for every retail HL/OpFor/BShift map, already used to build save-file comments and already resolved through `L()` by `LoadGame.cpp:286` - does not depend on a map re-setting it. `SV_ResolveLevelTitle()`, pulled out of that file's own `SaveBuildComment` (a pure refactor, save comments unchanged), populates a new Xbox-only read-only `sv_leveltitle` cvar once a map activates (`SV_UpdateLevelTitleCvar`, called from `SV_ActivateServer` in `sv_init.c`) - the same zero-new-ABI-surface shape as the Display screen's own `vid_refresh`/`vid_aspect` (this row's own earlier entry). Difficulty is real and shown: the same `skill` cvar `NewGame.cpp`'s own Easy/Medium/Hard rows already write. Codex review round 1 found two further real defects, both fixed before this PR opened. First (high severity): the first version toggled `pause` unconditionally on every show and hide, but `SV_Pause_f` (`sv_client.c`) is a state inversion, not a settable flag, and Xbox's BACK button is independently bound to raw `"pause"` (`engine/client/input/in_keys.c`) outside this menu entirely - so a player already paused by BACK before opening this screen would be silently unpaused by it. Fixed with a new Xbox-only read-only `cl_ispaused` cvar mirroring `cl.paused`, synced once per frame in `Host_ClientFrame` right after `CL_ReadPackets` (`cl_main.c`) via `Cvar_FullSet` - `Cvar_SetValue` was tried first and silently failed, since it routes through `Cvar_CanSet`, which refuses `FCVAR_READ_ONLY` outright. `_VidInit()` now only issues `pause` (and records that it did, in a new `m_bDidPause` member) when `cl_ispaused` reads 0; `Hide()` only reverses it when `m_bDidPause` is true. Second (medium severity): once a menu is active, raw input goes straight to the UI and never reaches gameplay bindings, and the inherited escape predicate (`UI::Key::IsEscape`, `Utils.h`) only recognizes Escape/B - Xbox's START is bound to `"cancelselect"` (`in_keys.c`), not pause, so it did nothing here. Fixed with a `KeyDown` override recognizing `K_START_BUTTON` and routing it through the same `Hide()` every other close path already uses. One real mistake caught before shipping: the heading first tried `L( "StringsList_235" )`, guessed from numeric proximity to `HazardCourseDialogCb`'s own `StringsList_234` - checked against `MenuStrings.cpp`'s real compiled table and found empty (`EMPTY_STRINGS_5`). Fixed with item 13's own idiom instead: `L( "Paused" )`, the literal as the key. A second, structural mistake caught only at the link, not the compile: the file first checked `#if XASH_XBOX` before any `#include`, seeing the macro (defined via `build.h`, reached transitively through `Framework.h`) as undefined and compiling the entire file to an empty 544-byte object - `UI_Pause_Menu` then failed as an undefined symbol at link time, not as a compile error, since an empty translation unit is not itself invalid. Fixed by moving the guard after the includes, matching every other file's own structure; the object grew to a real ~24 KB. Codex review round 2 found two more real defects, both fixed before this PR opened. First (high severity): the activation branch tested only `CL_IsActive()`, which says nothing about player count - pressing START in a multiplayer session would open this screen too, and its own `_VidInit()` would issue `pause` (accepted server-side since `sv_pausable` defaults to `"1"`, `sv_main.c:137`) and freeze the whole match for every client while offering Save/Load that make no sense there. Fixed by gating with the same `host_serverstate`/`maxplayers == 1` test Main.cpp's own `QuitDialogCb` already uses to mean "really singleplayer" - a multiplayer session now still opens Main on START, unchanged from before this item existed. Second (high severity): `CMenuLoadGame::SaveGame()`/`::LoadGame()` (`LoadGame.cpp`) both finish by calling `UI_CloseMenu()`, which clears the ENTIRE window stack (`CWindowStack::Clean()`, `WindowSystem.h`) without calling `Hide()` on anything still in it - harmless for every other caller of those two screens, since no other window's own `Hide()` does real work, but this screen's own unpause and `ui_renderworld` reset live there, so saving or loading from Pause left the game paused with the world still hidden afterward. Rather than hiding Pause before opening Save/Load (which would have broken the normal cancel-and-return-to-Pause navigation, since Save/Load are reached by staying layered on top of Pause on the stack, exactly like Options), split the cleanup into a public `ClosedExternally()` method and poll for the visible-to-not-visible edge once per frame from the same per-frame hook that opens this screen (`UI_Pause_CheckClosed`, `BaseMenu.cpp`) - idempotent, so it does nothing extra on every normal close path where `Hide()` already ran. Verified: `mainui` sweep clean (83/83, both rounds); classic disc links clean (0 duplicate, 0 undefined) and boots to an unchanged Main, screenshotted, both before and after the round 2 fixes; `menu_pause`'s own static pointer reads as a real non-null heap address post-boot alongside `menu_main`'s. NOT verified, and more than any screen before it: the actual in-game behaviour this whole item is about - START opening it in a live singleplayer map but not a multiplayer one, the dim, the freeze, the chapter/difficulty text, the pause round-trip on every exit path including one that starts already paused by BACK, and Save/Load's own cleanup firing correctly - none of it reachable without a pad in a running map | Form 3, `#if XASH_XBOX` around one call site and one per-frame poll in an otherwise-shared file (`BaseMenu.cpp`/`.h`), plus one entirely new file with no PC equivalent at all, plus the same guard around three small additions to shared server/client engine files (`cl_main.c`, `sv_init.c`, `sv_save.c`, `sv_main.c`, `server.h`) for the two new read-only cvars | Low - guarded to Xbox only; every other platform's `UI_UpdateMenu` still always opens Main, the new file simply does not exist in its build (empty translation unit under `#else`, nothing to diverge from), and both new cvars are declared and registered only under `#if XASH_XBOX` | None named - a deliberate new screen the design calls for, not something to converge back on |

Text and bitmaps are alternatives, not layers. Verified on the finished disc by reading `r_images[]`: four font atlases, no `btns_atlas` entry at all, 2.619 MiB against 2.656 before - a wash, under the ceiling.
