Mapped `"Tahoma"` to the already-staged `FiraSansCondensed-Regular.ttf` under `#if XASH_XBOX` instead, reusing the file rather than adding a second one since this face is tall 11, the smallest text on screen. Re-verified on the same German disc: help lines render "früher"/"ändern" correctly. **Measured with `memstats`, not assumed - Codex review round 2 caught the first draft over-claiming what this proves.** This one face costs `+162 pages = +648 KiB` of kernel `VirtualMemory`, corroborating the `r_images[]`-based prediction of 610,304 B; end to end from the last pre-item-13 build (strip resident, no font work) to this one, `+231 pages = +0.902 MiB`. That number is complete for what it claims - all five font atlases are eager, created once in menu-wide `UI_VidInit()` (`BaseMenu.cpp:1049,1090`) before any screen's own `_VidInit`, unlike the lazily-loaded `head_*.bmp` banners item 0's own deferred acceptance measurement exists to catch, and today's four shipped screens carry zero banner cost by construction - but it is **not** the whole-profile ceiling verdict, which stays deferred exactly as item 0 already recorded, until `Controller`/`Display` exist and a real pad traversal is run. `--ui-language` writes `vfs.cfg` specifically because that is exec'd during filesystem init (`filesystem_engine.c:115-116`), whereas `userconfig.d` and `config.cfg` both run too late for `Localize_Init` to see - the same ordering trap item 0 hit with `ui_xbox_menu_art`. Options" buttons, reading "Adv. Options" rather than "Game" - a real visual mismatch, caught re-verifying this row rather than by either Codex round above (both scoped to the Done button). | 65 | `3rdparty/mainui/sdk_includes/public/build.h` (owned fork, divergence #57): adds the identical Xbox sub-platform branch the engine's own `patches/library_suffix-xbox-subplatform.patch` already applies to `3rdparty/library_suffix/include/build.h` - under `#if defined _WIN32`, `#if defined NXDK` now also `#define XASH_XBOX 1` (plus the matching `#undef XASH_XBOX` in this file's undef block) | Found live, blocking divergence #66 below: every `#if XASH_XBOX` written into `Main.cpp` compiled clean but silently took the wrong (non-Xbox) branch. Root cause, found by preprocessing the file directly rather than guessing: `tools/xbox-mainui-sweep.sh`'s include path (`-I3rdparty/mainui/sdk_includes/...`) never reaches `3rdparty/library_suffix/include/build.h` - `mainui` builds against its OWN, entirely separate `sdk_includes/public/build.h` copy of the same platform-detection matrix, which had no Xbox sub-platform branch at all, so the macro was silently undefined (0 in `#if`, not a compiler error) for every file in this fork's own menu module, for as long as the module has existed. This is exactly why the earlier `ui_xbox_menu_art`/`uiStatic.lowmemory` gates in `BackgroundBitmap.cpp`/`Btns.cpp` were built as RUNTIME checks instead of `#if XASH_XBOX` - a choice made correctly at the time, for a reason this row is the first to write down explicitly. | 64 | `3rdparty/mainui/controls/BackgroundBitmap.cpp`/`.h` (owned fork, divergence #57): `LoadBackground()` now frees whichever background `UpdatePreference()` did not select right after deciding, via two new helpers `FreeWONBackground()`/`FreeSteamBackground()`; the runtime flip path (`Draw()`'s `ui_prefer_won_background` handler) now (re)loads the newly-preferred side if it isn't resident and frees the losing side, instead of only repointing `s_state`; `bimage_t` gains a `name[64]` field so each Steam tile's load-time filename survives for `EngFuncs::PIC_Free(name)`, which frees by name | XM item 0 step 3 (`fork-plan.md`): `LoadBackground()` loaded BOTH the Steam-style and WON-style background unconditionally whenever a disc's content carried both - `UpdatePreference()` only ever decided which one to DRAW, never freeing the loser, so a mixed-content disc kept two full background image sets resident for the rest of the session when only one is ever shown. `EngFuncs::PIC_Free(name)` → `menu_int.h`'s `pfnPIC_Free` → `GL_FreeImage` → `GL_FindTexture`/`GL_FreeTexture` is a real, already-used-elsewhere free (`ColorPicker.cpp`, `LoadGame.cpp`, `BaseFontBackend.cpp`) - the design constraint that blocked freeing banners elsewhere in this same item (`CImage` has no unloading destructor by design, `Image.h:40-44`) does not apply here, since `CMenuBackgroundBitmap` uses raw `HIMAGE` handles directly, not `CImage`. No disc anywhere actually carries both content sets, so verification built a synthetic one: `tools/asset-studio --bake-menu-art`'s existing Steam-background bake, overlaid via `tools/xbox-xiso.sh --menu-bake` onto a real classic WON install. Read live off the guest (the `.map`'s RVA+Base addresses for the class's static members, the pattern used throughout this item): `s_state=DRAW_STEAM` (correct - `ui_prefer_won_background` defaults `"0"`), `s_WONBackground.hImage=0` (freed), `s_SteamBackground` fully intact (6 valid entries, handles/coordinates/sizes matching the bake) - the exclusivity mechanism is confirmed correct at the data level. The on-screen result was solid black under otherwise-correct menu text; traced one level further (dumping the loaded tiles' own pixel data) to the CURRENT Steam install's real `resource/background/800_*.tga` files themselves reading back near-zero alpha at the sampled points - confirmed NOT this fix's doing by packaging the identical synthetic disc against the pre-fix binary and getting byte-identical solid-black output. Left open, not this row's: whether that is expected content (the 25th-anniversary build's tiles are documented solid black by divergence #60) or a real bake/alpha bug. No regression on either single-profile disc (classic, Steam), rebooted with the fixed binary. **Codex review round 1** found two real failure modes in the runtime flip path, both fixed: `s_bGameHasSteamBackground`/`s_bGameHasWONBackground` only turned true on a gamedir-specific load (harmless before this row, since both sides always stayed loaded and `UpdatePreference()`'s own secondary "what's actually resident" check caught a base-only background regardless) - with the loser now freed, a base-only background would read as permanently unavailable on a flip, so both the gamedir and base-fallback branches now set the flag. The flip handler also froze the old background unconditionally after attempting to load the new one, discarding a working background when the reload failed (`PIC_Load` returns 0 under memory pressure or a missing file) - it now only frees the losing side once the replacement actually loaded, restoring the previous state otherwise. `LoadSteamBackground()` also left a partial tile list behind on a mid-layout parse failure, which a later `Count() > 0` check could mistake for a complete background - it now rolls back exactly what the failed attempt appended. Re-verified live after the fix: same `s_state=DRAW_STEAM`/`s_WONBackground.hImage=0` result. **Codex review round 2** found the live handle check wasn't proof of actual freed memory: `UI_Precache()` (`BaseMenu.cpp`) unconditionally preloads the same splash art under the key `"gfx/shell/splash"` (no extension), independent of this file's own `ART_BACKGROUND` (`"gfx/shell/splash.bmp"`) - `GL_LoadTexture` caches by the exact caller-supplied string (`r_image.c:587`), before imagelib's own extension resolution runs, so the two keys created two separate resident copies of the identical decoded image; freeing `s_WONBackground`'s copy left `UI_Precache()`'s untouched. Fixed with a new `ART_BACKGROUND_PIC` constant matching `UI_Precache()`'s own key exactly for `PIC_Load`/`PIC_Free`/the stored `img.name` (`FileExists()` keeps `ART_BACKGROUND`'s real extension, since it does no probing of its own) - re-verified live: `s_WONBackground.name` now reads `"gfx/shell/splash"`, and `VirtualMemory` on the same synthetic disc dropped from 3501 to 3350 pages (~0.59 MiB), real freed memory this time. Second finding, same round: `LoadSteamBackground()` called `PIC_Load` for a tile before parsing its remaining fields, so any of those three later parses failing left the texture loaded but never appended to `s_SteamBackground`, unreachable to the round-1 rollback - restructured so `PIC_Load` is the last fallible step, immediately before `AddToTail`. Two review rounds, no third per the standing cap - both round-2 findings fixed | Form 1, entirely within this project's own `mainui_cpp` fork (divergence #57) - not `#if XASH_XBOX`-guarded, since a mixed-content disc wastes the same memory on any platform and freeing the undrawn side changes nothing a player can see | Low - the freed side is, by construction, the one `UpdatePreference()` already decided not to draw; a disc carrying only one content set (the overwhelming majority, checked live on both) takes the exact same load path as before, since the other side's free call is a no-op on an already-empty `hImage`/vector | None named - this is a straightforward correctness fix within an owned fork, not a divergence from an upstream FWGS behaviour to converge back on |
| 63 | `engine/client/console.c`: `con_oldfont` cvar's compiled-in default, under `#if XASH_XBOX`/`#else`, changed from `"0"` to `"1"` | Follow-up to #62, same investigation (XM item 0 step 5): fixing the console-font colour-noise left the glyphs legible but wrong - `"PQ RST"`, `"YZ WX"`, consecutive-alphabet slices. With `con_oldfont` at its stock default (`"0"`), `Con_LoadConsoleFont` (`:562-594`) only ever reaches `Con_LoadFixedWidthFont("gfx/conchars", ..., TF_KEEP_SOURCE)` (`:592`) on Xbox: the "modern" branch it tries first needs `fonts.wad` (CRC-gated) or `fonts/font%i` from xash-extras, which no retail disc carries and no Xbox packaging ships, and the only thing that ever sets `con_oldfont` is the `-oldfont` command-line switch (`Con_VidInit`), which an Xbox boot has none of. `Con_LoadFixedWidthFont` assumes a uniform 16x16-pixel glyph grid with no regard for the font's own metrics; dumping the real WON `CONCHARS` lump's `qfont_t` header and `charinfo[256]` table directly from `gfx.wad` confirmed it is a genuine, valid **variable**-width font (glyphs 3-9px wide, packed edge to edge, 12px row height, not 16) - so every 16px cell the fixed loader draws straddles two or three real glyphs. Steam content's own `CONCHARS` happens to use the modern qfont layout where `Image_LoadFNT` computes `width = font.width * 16` - a real 16px grid that coincides with the fixed loader's assumption by luck, not correctness - which is why this only ever showed up on classic WON content. Setting `con_oldfont` to `"1"` on Xbox routes both vintages through `Con_LoadVariableWidthFont("gfx/conchars.fnt", ...)` instead, which reads the file's real per-glyph metrics; the WAD virtual filesystem already resolves a `QFONT`-type lump under the `.fnt` extension (`filesystem/wad.c:83`, `{ "fnt", TYP_QFONT }`), so no new asset or code path was needed, only the default that selects it. Verified live: classic WON disc now shows the correct menu description text and version line (previously gibberish); Steam-content disc re-checked side by side on the same fixed binary, unchanged, no regression | Form 3, `#if XASH_XBOX` around one upstream cvar registration - every other platform keeps the stock `"0"` default and its own existing `-oldfont` opt-in behaviour unchanged | Low - a compiled-in default only, on one platform; no code path, struct or format changed, and the `-oldfont` command-line override (unreachable on Xbox) still works identically everywhere it can be reached | Shrinks to "delete the guard" if this platform ever gains its own `fonts.wad`/`fonts/font%i` xash-extras equivalent (then the modern branch would succeed and `con_oldfont` wouldn't need forcing) or if upstream ever teaches `Con_LoadFixedWidthFont` to read a font's real `charinfo` table instead of assuming a 16px grid |
| 62 | `ref/soft/r_image.c`: `GL_UploadTexture`, under `#if XASH_XBOX`, now expands an indexed (`PF_INDEXED_24`/`PF_INDEXED_32`) `pic->buffer` to RGBA before the mip loop and `GL_ResampleTexture` touch it - via `pic->palette`, strided correctly per type (3 bytes/entry, alpha forced 255, for `PF_INDEXED_24`'s 768-byte palette; 4 bytes/entry, real alpha, for `PF_INDEXED_32`'s 1024-byte one - `com_image.h:37-38`), `(R,G,B,[A])` order either way since `Image_GetPaletteBMP`/`Image_GetPaletteLMP` both funnel through `Image_SetPalette`'s `HostFourCC` into the same `d_currentpal` and `Image_ConvertPalTo24bit` only ever drops the alpha byte (`img_utils.c:284-346`) | Found live tracing the WON console-font corruption (`gfx.wad`'s `CONCHARS` lump, XM item 0 step 5): `GL_ProcessImage` computes `img_flags \| IMAGE_FORCE_RGBA` for an indexed `pic->type` but never applies it - `img_flags` is a dead local, upstream's own gap, present on every platform this software renderer runs on. `Con_LoadFixedWidthFont("gfx/conchars", ..., TF_KEEP_SOURCE)` (`console.c:592`) is the trigger reached live: `TF_KEEP_SOURCE` becomes `IL_KEEP_8BIT` in `GL_LoadTexture`, and `Image_AddIndexedImageToPack` then stores raw palette-index bytes (`pic->size=width*height`, one byte/pixel - legitimate, meant to keep `pic->original` for later re-tinting) instead of expanding them - but nothing downstream in `ref_soft` ever performed that expansion, so `GL_UploadTexture` read the index bytes four-at-a-time as if already RGBA: real (if meaningless) data for the first quarter of the image, `Mem_Calloc`'s own zero for the rest up to the small allocation's true end, then unmapped memory beyond it. Measured precisely before fixing: `pic->size=32768` for a real 256x128 conchars texture (expected `131072`), live data through row 31 of 256, zero rows 32-91, unreadable past that. Both the console font (this bug) AND the WON button-strip atlas (BMP-sourced, unaffected - `Image_LoadBMP` expands indexed BMPs to RGBA itself, inline, before `GL_UploadTexture` ever sees them) were independently proven correct at the DECODE stage first, via host-side reproductions of each loader's exact byte layout against the real retail files (`btns_main.bmp` from `pak0.pak`, `CONCHARS` from `gfx.wad`) rendering perfectly legible output - the corruption was conclusively downstream of both loaders before this row's fix. Verified live, same disc, same binary: the on-screen colour-noise grid and yellow/black band are gone, replaced by real console-font glyphs - legible, but not yet the intended text: they are consecutive-alphabet slices (`"PQ RST"`, `"YZ WX"`), a second, separate, pre-existing bug analysed the same day and recorded in `fork-plan.md` XM item 0 step 5 (the console font loads through the fixed-width 16x16-grid fallback, `console.c:592`, because nothing on Xbox sets `con_oldfont`, while the oldstyle `CONCHARS` lump is a variable-width qfont with a valid `charinfo` table the fixed path never reads) - not this row's. **Codex review round 1** caught two things in the first draft: the palette was always strided at 4 bytes/entry regardless of `pic->type` - wrong, and out-of-bounds, for `PF_INDEXED_24`'s 768-byte palette, reachable live via `engine/client/cl_remap.c`'s alias-skin remap (also submits with `TF_KEEP_SOURCE`) - fixed with the per-type stride above; and the fix was filed as Form 1, when an unguarded upstream behaviour change is Form 5 ("forbidden unless it is a genuine upstream bug, in which case the patch goes upstream first") - upstream submission was not attempted this session, so guarded to `#if XASH_XBOX` instead (Form 3), the cheaper, honest option the review itself named | Form 3, `#if XASH_XBOX` around a real upstream `ref_soft` gap - guarded rather than sent upstream, since this session has no path to submit a patch to FWGS; 'unbindall' from config.cfg will be reset it"` (`in_keys.c:577` post-patch; `:562` on `main`). Retail Half-Life's own `config.cfg` (auto-generated by the PC game, present verbatim on this disc) opens with `unbindall` then re-lists every keyboard/mouse bind it cares about - having no concept of a gamepad, it names none of the ~30 gamepad keys, and `Key_Unbindall_f` (the actual C handler `unbindall` invokes) wipes them along with everything else. Round 1 found that broke a real, reachable path: the in-game Controls menu lets a player explicitly clear a gamepad bind (`3rdparty/mainui/menus/Controls.cpp`, `UnbindEntry`/`UnbindCommand`), and `Key_WriteBindings` skips empty bindings, so a cleared gamepad key would have come back on every restart; a `Key_WriteBindings` change (explicit `unbind` lines for empty gamepad keys) was added. Round 2 then made the stronger point that undid both: `unbindall` is registered as "removes all commands from all keys" and `resetkeys` (`in_keys.c`, `Key_Reset_f`) is the separate, existing restore-all-defaults path - so the ESC/START lines are a "can always leave a menu" safety rail, not a defaults-restore precedent, and making `unbindall` install `+attack`/`+use`/weapon binds would change shared semantics for any PC user or mod script that runs `unbindall` and deliberately binds only a subset (a plugged-in controller would start firing actions). Final shape follows that recommendation exactly: `unbindall`/`Key_WriteBindings` are left as upstream has them; the seeding is a separate, one-shot, post-`config.cfg` step that only fills gamepad keys that are EMPTY, gated by an archived marker cvar (`joy_binds_seeded`) that the very next config save persists - so a legacy keyboard-only `config.cfg` gets the defaults once, a config that already carries explicit gamepad state (any config saved by this engine after the first seed writes every non-empty gamepad bind, and the marker) is respected as-is including a player's deliberate unbind, and a mod/user script calling `unbindall` later still gets an empty set. The same wipe also fires from the Controls menu's own "reset to defaults" button (`Controls.cpp:112-149`, `ResetKeysList`: `unbindall` then `gfx/shell/kb_def.lst`, retail's keyboard-only list) - after which `resetkeys`-style recovery on a console is exactly `Key_Reset_f`'s job, not this row's. | 52 | `engine/platform/sdl2/vid_sdl2.c`: (a) `VID_SaveWindowSize()`: an `XASH_XBOX` arm that, for an integer `vid_scale` > 1, sets the render target to `window / vid_scale` (so `vid_scale 2.0` is a 320x240 render on the 640x480 window) before `R_SaveVideoMode()`, regardless of `VID_SetDisplayTransform()` having declined; (b) `SW_UnlockBuffer()`: an `XASH_XBOX` arm that, when the render target is an integer fraction of the 4-byte window surface, expands it to the full surface in place (nearest, one expanded row per group, `memcpy` for the rest) before `SDL_UpdateWindowSurface` - so the 320x240 frame fills the screen instead of its top-left quadrant | X5's exit line needs frame times "at 640x480 and 320x240", and neither of the two ordinary ways to get 320x240 exists on this target: `ref_soft`'s `R_SetDisplayTransform` refuses any scale ("scale transform not supported", `r_glblit.c:36` - it has no upscaling blit, its own comment says "maybe implement 2x2?"), so `vid_common.c`'s `VID_SetDisplayTransform` leaves the render size at the window size and `vid_scale` is a no-op; and nxdk's video mode table (`hal/video.c:78-137`) has only 640x480, 720x480 and the HD modes, no 320x240 to set with `XVideoSetMode`. fork-plan.md's X5 already named the one real lever - "`R_SaveVideoMode(640,480,320,240,…)`, not `vid_scale`" - and this arm is that lever, driven by the same cvar the rest of the engine uses for the same meaning (a pixel scale: render = window / scale). On the SDL-surface path `ref_soft` then draws 320x240 into the top-left quadrant of the 640x480 window surface (`SW_LockBuffer` only requires the surface to be at least the render size), unscaled - which halves the rasterizer's pixels and its surface cache (`r_surf.c:778`'s formula is in render pixels), i.e. exactly the cost a 320x240 frame-time number has to measure, while the blit's own cost (the 640x480 `SDL_ConvertPixels`) is the same as at 640x480 and is `vid_xbox.c`'s to delete. Integer scales only, and only above 1. The present step (b) is the interim full-screen presentation of that quadrant - nearest 2x2, because a CPU bilinear filter (four reads and a blend per output pixel) would spend a good part of what the smaller render saved, while the NV2A does that filter for free on a textured quad, which is `vid_xbox.c`'s planned present path. Its cost was measured twice (X5 plan entry): a divide-per-pixel first version, then the `memcpy`-based one kept - slower under xemu (TCG makes the guest's `div` one native instruction and emulates `memcpy` step by step) and the right one for the target (a `div` per output pixel is ~16 ms a frame on a 733 MHz Pentium III; `rep movsd` streams the 1.2 MB in ~2 ms). Numbers measured with both arms are in the X5 plan entry. Three review findings shaped the tooling around it: the map `xemu-debug.py` binds against is now the one the XBE's OWN link writes (`tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh --xbe` passes `-map:` and copies it beside the XBE) - section extents alone were rightly rejected as identity; `fps` computes `host_parm_t`'s field offsets at run time with `tools/xbox-offsetof.sh` rather than carrying constants; and, round 2, the FIRST binding scheme (the link timestamp lld writes into both the map header and the PE's COFF header) was itself rejected - the value is wall-clock SECONDS, so two links finishing in the same second collide, and combined with equal section extents (an ordinary equal-size substitution) a stale map would still have bound. Replaced with a real identity: `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh --xbe` now writes a SHA-256 sidecar (`xbe-build.exe.identity`, the exact hash of that exe and of its own map) next to `xbe-build.exe`, and `pools`/`fps` require both hashes to match the files on disk before reading a byte - verified live, including that a one-byte-tampered sidecar is refused with the mismatched hashes printed, not silently trusted. The same round also found `GdbRemote.close()`'s resume-on-exit could not tell "the guest resumed" from "the connection just ended with no reply" (both read as an empty string from `monitor()`) - `monitor()` now raises on that specific case, so `close()`'s warning and nonzero exit fire for real, not only when the stub sends an explicit `E` error. | Form 3 (`#if XASH_XBOX` arm in a shared SDL2 file, after the common transform call; inert everywhere else, and inert on Xbox at `vid_scale 1.0`) | Low - a few lines behind the existing Xbox guard in a function that already carries this fork's Xbox arms (divergence #50); reads the cvar through `Cvar_VariableValue` because `vid_common.c` keeps it `static` | Shrinks to nothing with `vid_xbox.c`, whose own mode/blit code owns render size and presentation together; until then this is how 320x240 is measured |

Fixed with the guard (f), a genuine upstream latent bug (relying on an SDL API tolerating NULL only in release builds) - the one Form-5 change here, behaviour-identical on every platform, worth sending upstream; and (g), because this is the SECOND silent SDL-assert failure this port has paid a live session for (divergence #49's hung instead of aborting): every future one now prints its condition/file/line through the same `Sys_Error` every other fatal boot problem takes, still fatal, no longer a mystery. **The picture, real**: with `verify-image` passing first, a breakpoint on the return of `SDL_XBOX_UpdateWindowFramebuffer`'s second call and the framebuffer read physically from PCRTC START while halted (`.sweep/x5-shots/halted-frame2.png`, 95% of sampled pixels non-black) shows Half-Life's own loading screen - Gordon, the lambda, "LOADING", the engine's version string - drawn by `ref_soft` at 640x480. The first frame this fork has ever put on the Xbox's own scan-out. **Then the map runs the console out of memory**: `Sys_Error( "_Mem_Alloc: out of memory ... engine/client/dll_int/cl_game.c:1002" )` (`CL_InitEdicts`'s `cls.packet_entities` reallocation) at 8.6 s with the kernel at 249 free pages (0.97 MiB): `VirtualMemory` 7,876 pages (30.8 MiB, still climbing), `Image` 6,936 (27.1 MiB - up from 19.1 MiB with `ref_null`: `ref_soft`'s own statics are `vid` 2.5 MiB of rasterizer tables, `r_images[MAX_TEXTURES]` 2.4 MiB, `g_studio` 1.8 MiB, `r_leafkeys` 0.5 MiB, `gDecalPool` 0.2 MiB), `Stack` 3.0 MiB, `Contiguous` 2.0 MiB. X7.4 step 4's `ref_null` load cost +15.0 MiB and left 17.4 MiB; `ref_soft` costs ~7.6 MiB more image and ~8.5 MiB more heap on the same map - over the line by a few MiB, exactly the ceiling X3 and X8 own (`cl` 3.4 MiB, `sv` 2.4, `net` 1.6, `mod_known` 1.5 are the engine-side statics already named there). Not hidden behind a smaller map: X5's exit line is `c1a3`/`crossfire` at 640x480 and 320x240, and that needs the memory work first. **Codex review, round 1, two real findings, both fixed**: (1) removing #46's `R_ChangeDisplaySettings` arm re-exposed every LATER mode change - a cvar restart, or `vid_setmode W H` (`VID_Mode_f`, `vid_common.c`), which never passes `R_Init_Video`'s cvar pinning - to a driver with exactly one framebuffer whose present path asserts the surface is no larger than it (`SDL_xbframebuffer.c:90-91`); so that arm is back, as a CLAMP rather than a bypass: every request becomes 640x480 windowed with a `S_WARN` line, and the cvars are re-pinned. Its flag is verified to propagate - a manual link of its own response file with the flag reads `SizeOfStackReserve=0x100000` - but a full dedicated boot could not be re-verified here: that target's link currently fails on an unrelated, pre-existing `undefined symbol: _lib_ref_null_exports` from `engine_platform_misc_lib_static.obj`'s `_libs` table, identically with or without the flag, i.e. its library table references `ref_null`'s export-table object that the dedicated sweep never builds - a separate, real gap for that target's own next bring-up pass, not this row's) | Root cause of the stack overflow divergence #46 left open, found live rather than guessed: on the very first `Host_Frame`, `NET_QueuePacket` (`engine/common/net_ws.c`) puts `byte buf[NET_MAX_FRAGMENT]` (65535 bytes, a 0x10100-byte frame) on the stack, and nxdk's `__chkstk` → `_xlibc_check_stack` (`deps/nxdk/lib/xboxrt/c_runtime/check_stack.c`) raises `EXCEPTION_STACK_OVERFLOW` as `EXCEPTION_NONCONTINUABLE`, which the kernel turns into exactly the observed `KeBugCheckEx(0x1E, 0xC00000FD)` with the exception address inside `RaiseException`. The decisive clue was the bugcheck's own ESP sitting only ~2 KiB below `Host_Frame`'s entry ESP - not deep recursion, a single frame that could never fit. Why it cannot fit: the XBE header's `SizeOfStack` comes from `nxdk-link`'s hardcoded `-stack:65536`, and nxdk's `CreateThread(..., 0, ...)` resolves a zero stack size to `CURRENT_XBE_HEADER->SizeOfStack` (`deps/nxdk/lib/winapi/thread.c`) - including the thread crt0's own `thrd_create()` runs `main()` on (`deps/nxdk/lib/pdclib/platform/xbox/crt0.c`), so the whole engine ran on 64 KiB. Upstream assumes far more and already raises it explicitly on ports with small native defaults (DOS: `stack=512k`, Emscripten: 16 MiB - `scripts/waifulib/compiler_optimizations.py`, `engine/wscript`); Windows, the platform `XASH_XBOX` is a sub-platform of (divergence #7), gives 1 MiB. Chose 1 MiB to match Windows: it also covers the larger frames already known further in (`MAX_INIT_MSG` = 196608-byte buffers in `CL_ProcessFile`/`CL_BatchResourceRequest`/`CL_SendResourceList`, `Cmd_TokenizeString`'s 32 KiB frame probed on every console command). Verified end to end, not assumed: the linked PE's `SizeOfStackReserve` reads 0x100000 and the XBE header's `SizeOfStackCommit` (offset 0x130) reads 0x100000; live, the boot then sustains 300 consecutive counted `Host_Frame` calls with no failure breakpoint firing, and a 75-second free run with nothing armed ends in the kernel's between-frames idle loop (`HLT=0`, EIP `0x8001b02f`) rather than the bugcheck (`HLT=1`, EIP `0x800151ef`). Caught with a real technique worth keeping: arm `__xlibc_check_stack` only after the milestone breakpoint fires (during init it probes 32 KiB for every console command, hundreds of times), record each probe's requested size and requester (readable off `stack_ptr - 4`), and take the last one before `RaiseException`. **Guarded, not just set (Codex review, round 2)**: the flag relies on lld taking the LAST `-stack:` given, and nothing would have noticed if that ordering, cxbe's propagation into the XBE header, or the option itself ever stopped overriding nxdk-link's own `-stack:65536` - the binary would still link and pass every other gate while restoring the overflow. So `tools/xbox-check-stack.sh` (read-only, `od`-based, no toolchain) now reads the real field - PE `SizeOfStackReserve`, or XBE `SizeOfStackCommit` at offset 0x130 - and every link tool asserts it equals 1048576 on every PE it links and every XBE it converts, failing loudly on a mismatch (exit 1, real value printed) or an unreadable/truncated file (exit 2). Exercised on real outputs: five assertion sites across the baseline and game paths all read 1048576. | Build flag plus a measured assertion, both in this fork's own tools; no engine or vendored source touched | None for upstream; real cost on the target: every thread created with stack size 0 now gets 1 MiB (the main thread, `net_ws.c`'s DNS resolver thread, anything else created with 0) - a handful of MiB of the 64 MiB total, to be re-weighed at the memory-ceiling milestone, where a per-thread size for the secondary threads is the obvious refinement | Real: revisit at the memory-ceiling milestone (per-thread sizes for secondary threads, or a smaller main stack once the largest frames are known to be bounded); the flag itself stays as long as upstream keeps 64 KiB-hostile frames like `NET_QueuePacket`'s |

Left standing, `Host_Frame`'s own real per-frame `Host_ClientFrame`→`VID_CheckChanges` call would see it still true and call `VID_SetMode`→`VID_CreateWindow`→`SDL_CreateWindow`, reaching the exact same broken driver on literally the next real frame - a real, if not (checked live, see below) the ONLY, way back into the crash this fix exists to avoid. Fixed as recommended, with defense in depth: the early return now clears `host.renderinfo_changed` itself, AND `VID_SetMode()` gained its own matching `XASH_XBOX` no-op guard, so no future legitimate cvar change can reach the broken driver either, not just this one call site. **Re-tested live after this additional fix: the stack overflow still happens, at the same point (one real `Host_Frame` call, confirmed via a direct race between `Host_Frame` and `__xbox_assert` breakpoints), with the identical bugcheck signature.** This rules out Codex's finding as THE cause of the stack overflow specifically (it was a real bug regardless, and is now fixed), and confirms the stack overflow is a genuinely separate, still-unresolved problem somewhere later in `Host_Frame`'s real per-frame call graph, past the ~400 already-traced, entirely normal early instructions (`Platform_DoubleTime`/`Con_DPrintf`/`Host_InputFrame`/`IN_CheckMouseState`). **Codex review, round 2, found two more real gaps, both fixed**: (1) the independently registered `vid_setmode` console command (`VID_Mode_f`, `vid_common.c`) calls `R_ChangeDisplaySettings()` directly, bypassing `VID_SetMode()`'s own guard entirely and reaching the same broken driver a third way; (2) `refState.width`/`height` - set only by `R_SaveVideoMode()`, which every Xbox arm was skipping - stayed at zero, and real client code (crosshair scaling, overview setup, `SCR_VidInit`) reads them unconditionally once active. Source read confirmed the real cause: `UTIL_IsValveGameDirectory()` exists to refuse running a mod out of a retail Valve game's own directory (a real, deliberate safety check, matching one in `SV_InitServer`, `dlls/game.cpp:468` too) - and our disc's real retail Half-Life assets sit in a directory literally named `valve`, matching upstream HL's own canonical gamedir, since Resonance3D's actual goal is porting the stock game itself, not shipping a third-party mod alongside a separate retail install. Sibling project Ferrum56 already hit this identical tension for its own static Rust-FFI linking and already carries a bypass (`_STATIC_ENGINE_LINK`) - but that macro also gates unrelated Ferrum56-Rust-FFI-specific code throughout the SDK (`animation.cpp`, `client.cpp`, `enginecallback.h`), so defining it for Resonance3D's Xbox build would have been wrong, pulling in behavior that doesn't apply here. Fixed with a new `#elif defined(NXDK)` arm (leaving `_STATIC_ENGINE_LINK`'s own path completely untouched), added as a new, separate branch rather than folding into that macro's condition - `NXDK` is already this fork's own established, proven Xbox-only guard for exactly this kind of platform-specific SDK divergence (precedent: the prior `dlls: guard the RTTI-free downcast fix to NXDK only` commit, same submodule). Verified live, not just "it compiles": read the actual compiled machine code at the resolved runtime address in a live, running boot for the first version of this fix (an unconditional `return false;`, byte-identical for both the client- and server-isolated copies), re-tested the full boot, and confirmed the `quit`-at-~2.5s was gone, with the boot demonstrably advancing further, now reaching `R_Init()`'s real renderer-selection code (a new, later, well-understood, separate gap - `video.cfg`'s `r_refdll` needs to be `"null"`, not empty, for this milestone's headless `ref_null` to be selected; real retail `video.cfg` never needed to set it). **Codex review, round 1, caught a real gap in that first version**: it returned `false` unconditionally for the WHOLE `NXDK` branch, silently accepting every Valve-prefixed gamedir (`ricochet`/`dmc`/`cstrike`/`czero`/`dod`/`tfc`), not only the ones this fork actually ports - a disc/XBE mismatch (this SDK's own compiled client/server running against a mod gamedir it was never built for) would then proceed with the wrong statically-linked game logic instead of failing loudly. **Round 2 caught two more, in the fix for round 1**: `gearbox`/`bshift` do not belong in THIS submodule's own allowlist at all - each Resonance3D title has its own separate SDK checkout (`deps/hlsdk` for Half-Life, `deps/hlsdk-bshift`, `deps/hlsdk-opfor`, per `docs/r3d/fork-plan.md`'s "Per-title XBEs"), so this one should only ever accept its own title's gamedir; and the check itself reused the original `strnicmp`+`strlen(prefix)` substring match, which would also have silently accepted `valve_custom` or anything else merely starting with `valve`. Final shape: `stricmp(modDirectoryName.c_str(), "valve") == 0` - full case-insensitive equality against exactly this checkout's own title, `stricmp` already available via `Platform.h`'s existing cross-platform macro (the same header `strnicmp` below already comes from) - falling through to the SAME original prefix-check loop (and therefore the same correct refusal) for everything else, including `gearbox`/`bshift` themselves on THIS checkout. Blue Shift and Op4 need this identical fix in their own SDK checkouts, exempting their own gamedir, when their own bring-up starts. Re-verified after each round: compiles cleanly, re-links 0 duplicate/0 undefined. | Form 4 (owned fork, direct commit - `deps/hlsdk` is `github.com/mathiasdbo/halflife-updated-xash3d`, not a patch against pristine upstream) | Low - the new `NXDK` arm only changes behavior on the Xbox target, only for exactly this checkout's own title (`valve`); every other platform's `_STATIC_ENGINE_LINK`-driven behavior is untouched, and every other gamedir - including `gearbox`/`bshift`, and any prefix-matched variant like `valve_custom` - is still correctly refused on NXDK too, verified by diff | Closed for this exact check, this checkout. `FileSystem_LoadFileSystem()` (the OTHER early-return in `CL_InitClient()`/`SV_InitServer()`, checked before this one) was directly verified NOT to be the active failure this session (a live breakpoint on it never fired once the `quit` was gone) - if it ever does fail on a future build, it needs its own, separate investigation |

Divergence #37 had already dismissed implementing real MSVC RTTI, for the server SDK's own `dynamic_cast` needs, as "real, separate, low-level ABI work, not warranted" - that dismissal is reconsidered here and found to have rested on an unverified assumption. Checked directly: `llvm-nm` on real compiled `3rdparty/freevgui` objects shows clang already emits a complete, correct set of MSVC-ABI RTTI type descriptors (`??_R0` TypeDescriptor through `??_R4` CompleteObjectLocator) for every class - what nxdk's toolchain genuinely lacks (confirmed via `llvm-nm --defined-only` against every one of its prebuilt C++ runtime libraries, the same check divergence #37 already made for this exact symbol) is only the RUNTIME WALKER that reads this already-correct, already-emitted metadata - a categorically smaller, more tractable problem than "implement RTTI from scratch." Researched, not guessed: Lukasz Lipski's "RTTI Internals in MSVC" (lukaszlipski.dev) and Quarkslab's "Visual C++ RTTI Inspection" (blog.quarkslab.com) independently corroborate the same `TypeDescriptor`/`PMD`/`RTTIBaseClassDescriptor`/`RTTIClassHierarchyDescriptor`/`RTTICompleteObjectLocator` 32-bit struct layout; the public Sun RTTI specification (via the `itanium-cxx-abi/cxx-abi` project) gives the general `dynamic_cast` algorithm shape (linear search for a target base, ambiguity on multiple distinct-offset matches); Microsoft's own real `rtti.cpp` (a GitHub mirror of MSVC's build tools) confirmed the exact top-level `__RTDynamicCast` dispatch shape and parameter semantics. None of this is transcribed - this project has no license to reproduce Microsoft's proprietary CRT source - the file is a genuine clean-room reimplementation written from understanding the documented behaviour, the same methodology freevgui's own README states for its own VGUI1 reimplementation. Deliberately, honestly scoped: non-virtual inheritance (single AND multiple) is fully, correctly handled by one unified linear-scan algorithm - simpler than Microsoft's own split SI/MI/VI functions, valid because this codebase's real call sites never need Microsoft's more elaborate cross-cast disambiguation - verified to cover every real `dynamic_cast<>` site in `3rdparty/freevgui` (`frame.cpp`, `table.cpp`, `treefolder.cpp`, `layout.cpp`) by direct grep, including real multiple-inheritance SOURCE objects (`TextEntry : Panel, InputSignal`) that can genuinely flow through them. Virtual inheritance is deliberately left unimplemented (returns `nullptr`) - verified unreachable by anything this codebase calls today (grep found exactly one virtual-inheritance class, `DataInputStream`, nowhere near `Panel`/`LayoutInfo`'s hierarchy), and the exact `vdisp`/vbtable semantics for that case could not be confirmed against an authoritative primary source - an honest, blocking `nullptr` was chosen over a guessed, silently-wrong offset, the same "honest failure over silent wrong success" standard divergence #42 already applied to this identical symbol. Reference-cast-throws-`bad_cast` is likewise deliberately unimplemented (`nullptr` instead) - verified no real caller in this codebase compiles a reference `dynamic_cast` today. **Verification is the important, unusual part here, and was not "it links."** A real ground-truth test harness was built and run: the exact same implementation file, compiled with real Microsoft Visual C++ (Visual Studio 18, `cl.exe`, 32-bit x86 - the actual real compiler whose ABI this file reimplements, already installed on this dev machine), alongside a small test hierarchy matching freevgui's real class shapes - a single-inheritance chain `MessageBox : Frame : Panel`; real multiple inheritance `TextEntry : Panel, InputSignal`; a `Combined : Frame, InputSignal` case exercising a genuine, nonzero-offset multiple-inheritance cross-cast. The test program called both the real compiler's own built-in `dynamic_cast` and this file's own `__RTDynamicCast` (aliased under a different linker name to avoid colliding with MSVC's own predefined declaration of the same symbol) on the SAME real, constructed C++ objects, and compared the resulting pointers directly. All 6 real cases - a positive single-inheritance downcast, a negative multiple-inheritance-source non-match, the hard nonzero-offset cross-cast, an unrelated-type negative match, the `BorderLayoutInfo`/`LayoutInfo` shape, and an exact-type-match fast path - produced byte-identical pointer results to real MSVC's own `dynamic_cast`. Only after that did the real combined link get re-measured: `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh hl --include-client --include-freevgui --expect-undefined 0` now reports **0 duplicate, 0 undefined** and produces a real linked output for the first time ever with server, client, and freevgui all real together - X7.4 step 2's own real link-only exit gate, met. Found in the same investigation, not a regression: `compat/xbox/freevgui_app.cpp` (divergence #42) does not compile under the generic engine sweep's own plain flags (`tools/xbox-sweep.sh engine filesystem public common compat 3rdparty/library_suffix/src` calls `tools/xbox-cc.sh` directly, with no `-I3rdparty/freevgui`) - a real, expected gap matching `engine/common/net_chan.c`'s own already-established "compiled specially elsewhere, not the plain sweep, by design" precedent (that file needs `bzlib.h` on its include path the same way `freevgui_app.cpp` needs freevgui's own headers), never actually measured when divergence #42 was written. Those objects' own compiled virtual calls are baked-in indirect calls through specific vtable BYTE OFFSETS, determined once, at cl_dll's own compile time, by Valve's header's declared member order - offsets freevgui's implementation must match exactly for those existing calls to reach the RIGHT function. Adding one new virtual to `Panel` or `LayoutInfo` would insert a new vtable slot and shift every slot after it - `CHECK_STRUCT_SIZE` would NOT catch this (it asserts `sizeof(Panel)`, the per-INSTANCE size, which does not change when a class gains a virtual method - only the shared, per-class vtable does), so the mistake would compile and link cleanly while silently sending some already-correct virtual call from `cl_dll`'s real, working code to the WRONG function at runtime - a memory-corruption-class bug, categorically worse than the honest, blocking, easy-to-see undefined-symbol error it would replace. Round 1: an `.mpak`'s whole reason to exist is superseding the loose `maps/<name>.bsp` + WAD-resolved textures it was baked from, but the first version tried to get that from the new entry's *position* within `g_archives[]` — wrong, since `FS_AddGameDirectory` mounts every entry in `g_archives[]` as one group, entirely BEFORE it adds the plain directory, and array order only ever decides precedence among archive types against each other, never against the plain directory (`FS_AddArchive_Fullpath` prepends, so whatever mounts LAST is searched first). Reverted `mount_after_plain_dir` and the two-pass split entirely: `.mpak` is an ordinary `g_archives[]` entry now, mounted in the main group before the plain directory like every other archive type, so a loose `.bsp` wins if both are present and baking a map's `.mpak` cannot break that map's existing load path. Fixed by clamping exponent accumulation at 1000 — comfortably past double's real ±308/-324 dynamic range, so every legitimate value is unaffected and every value beyond it correctly saturates to inf/0 exactly as an unclamped computation would, just without the unbounded loop or the overflow. Verified natively (compiled and run the same algorithm on the host, not just re-read): `1e2147483647`/`-1e2147483647`/a 19-digit-mantissa 10-digit-exponent string all resolve to the correct inf/-inf/inf in under a microsecond, alongside all the ordinary cases (`42`, `-3.14`, `1.5e2`, `0.001`, `3.14abc` → `3.14`) still parsing correctly.** | New file in `compat/xbox`, no new declaration needed anywhere (pdclib's own `<stdlib.h>` already declares it correctly) — zero merge cost | Low — ours, generic C, no platform API dependency at all | None while pdclib leaves this unimplemented. Fixed by using this project's own `_fseeki64`/`_ftelli64` instead — already real, already used by `3rdparty/vorbis` under the identical `_WIN32` condition — rather than making any new assumption about pdclib's `fpos_t` layout.** The calls this file keeps — `_fileno`/`_filelengthi64` for `op_fseek`'s `SEEK_END` path, `_fseeki64`/`_ftelli64` for the rest — have a real nxdk equivalent (`GetFileSizeEx`, and this project's own existing 64-bit seek/tell pair) and are genuine functions, not stubs: `_fileno` wraps this file's own `fileno()` (already the `FILE*`-to-descriptor bridge divergence #12/#7c's own history required), `_get_osfhandle` looks up the real `HANDLE` `fileno()` registered in `r3d_fds`, `_filelengthi64` calls `GetFileSizeEx` on it. Verified by linking: opusfile's own 4 files (`info.c`, `internal.c`, `opusfile.c`, `stream.c`) now compile and link clean, and the 10 `op_*`/`opus_tags_query` symbols `engine/client/soundlib/snd_ogg_opus.c` already referenced (previously undefined — part of X1's 74-undefined measurement) are now resolved, dropping the count to 64. **Found in review, 2026-09-08 (second pass): making `op_get_file_callbacks` always report seekable meant `op_fseek`'s `SEEK_END` call to `fileno()` now runs on every seekable open (opusfile itself seeks to end during open, `opusfile.c:1399`), which registers a borrowed slot in `r3d_fds` — a slot only ever released by this file's own `close(fd)`, never by plain `fclose()`, which is what `opusfile`'s default close callback was. Every seekable opusfile open would have permanently consumed one of `R3D_MAX_FDS - 3 = 61` slots for the life of the process, latent rather than live (nothing calls opusfile from the engine yet) but a real leak regardless of when it fires. Fixed with `_r3d_release_stream_fd` (new, releases a borrowed slot for a given `FILE*` without touching the OS handle) and an NXDK-specific `op_fclose_nxdk` close callback that calls it before `fclose`.** | The `stream.c` patch is a **patch file** (`patches/opusfile-xbox-stream.patch`), auto-applied idempotently by `tools/xbox-cc.sh` — same mechanism, same reasoning, as divergence #5's `library_suffix` patch (not committed to the submodule, which would require forking `xiph/opusfile`; invisible to a fresh clone without the auto-apply). `_fileno`/`_get_osfhandle`/`_filelengthi64` are new functions in files this project owns | Low for the `compat/xbox` additions (ours). **Entry #98, specialist review findings, all fixed.** `reference-renderer-specialist` caught a real citation error (an earlier version of the code comment and this entry named a nonexistent `GL_SetupGL` - the real function is `R_SetupGL(true)`, called from `R_RenderScene`) and one disclosed-but-real simplification (the reference's actual cull-test input is `tr.modelorg`/`RI.cullorigin`, not raw `vieworigin` - equal to it for this fork's real single-player path, since no mirror/portal pass exists here to diverge them, but an equivalence worth re-checking if one is ever added). `nv2a-xbox-specialist` found two real things: an evaluation-order inefficiency (the backface test - the only one of the three cull tests with a pointer chase into a separate, deduplicated plane pool - ran unconditionally on every face, including ones the cheaper frustum/PVS tests had already rejected; **fixed** by moving it after those two, so it only runs on their survivors) and, more importantly, that **the NV2A already has its own, always-on hardware backface cull** (`deps/nxdk/lib/pbkit/pbkit.c`: `CULL_FACE_ENABLE=1`, clockwise front-face winding, cull the back), unconditionally active since X9-A and never touched by anything in this module. This CPU test is therefore a SECOND, independently-computed cull decision layered on top of an already-active one - its real value is skipping vertex-transform/pushbuffer cost for faces the GPU would have discarded anyway, not correctness the GPU test lacked - but the two tests (this one's plane/`SURF_PLANEBACK` math, the GPU's screen-space winding) must actually AGREE, or this test can silently punch real holes in walls the GPU alone would have drawn correctly (a one-directional failure mode: too permissive is merely redundant, too aggressive is a visible defect). By construction they should agree - the baker's own fan triangulation preserves each face's real edge-loop winding with no reversal, the identical reconstruction `ref/gl`/`ref/soft` themselves use - and real maps have rendered correctly under the GPU's own always-on cull since X9-B, which is circumstantial support, but nobody has directly cross-checked the two tests against each other. **Concrete, cheap, not-yet-done verification step, named explicitly rather than left implicit**: an `nv2a_r_nocullback 0` vs `1` A/B walk of a real level, looking for any face that only appears with this test disabled - the same kind of live check X9-F's own `nv2a_r_nocull` A/B eventually got (divergences.md #96), not yet performed here because live-boot access is exactly what this entry's own verification gap (below) is missing. A new `nv2a_cull_faces_backface_only` counter (mirroring the existing `nv2a_cull_faces_*` telemetry, `tools/xemu-debug.py mem`-readable) now measures this test's own marginal contribution - faces backface-culled that frustum/PVS did NOT already reject - once live access exists to read it, turning "this should be a net win" from an inspection-based judgment into a measurable one.

**Entry #98, Codex review, two real findings, both fixed.** High confidence (0.99): this test's own correctness depends on agreeing with the NV2A's separate, always-on hardware backface cull, and that agreement genuinely has not been live-verified this session (the same open gap this entry already disclosed). Shipping a correctness-risking cull test ENABLED by default while the one check that would confirm it safe is explicitly not yet done does not match this project's own "compile and boot before calling anything done" discipline. **Fixed** by defaulting `nv2a_r_nocullback` to `"1"` (disabled) rather than `"0"` - the test exists in the tree, ready to enable and verify the moment live-boot access exists, but no longer silently risks real geometry on every boot until that verification happens. Flip the default back once a real `nv2a_r_nocullback 0` vs `1` A/B walk on live hardware or xemu confirms no face disappears with it on - the same way X9-F's own `nv2a_r_nocull` A/B was later confirmed live (#96). Medium confidence (0.99), a second real finding: the new `nv2a_cull_faces_backface_only` counter's own comment overclaimed it measured "the actual marginal win... in dwords" - it does not. The run-merging logic snaps each face's index range outward to a 6-index boundary and merges contiguous runs, so a backface-culled face immediately adjacent (in bake order) to a surviving one can still have its own indices fall inside that neighbour's submitted run - the counter increments with no matching reduction in `nv2a_cull_dwords` for that specific face. **Fixed** by correcting the comment to describe what the counter actually measures - a logical face-classification count, real and useful on its own terms, just not a literal bandwidth-saved number - rather than renaming or removing it (a true dword-delta measurement would need comparing `nv2a_cull_dwords` with the test on vs. off, disproportionate to what this bring-up counter needs to prove).

**Entry #98 (X9-H, backface culling) - same open verification gap #97 recorded, not re-attempted at length a second time.** `tools/xbox-cc.sh ref/nv2a/r_context.c` compiled clean (one pre-existing, unrelated path-case warning) after this slice's own edit. `tools/xbox-link-check.sh` was tried once more this session - it reached its first real checkpoint ("every engine object is vouched for by the sweep manifest") then produced no further output for several minutes with no `clang.exe` process observed running under it, the same practical outcome as #97's own stalls (a real compile step reached, then no forward progress), just at an earlier checkpoint this time. Per the same "stop and escalate with data, not a fresh hypothesis" reasoning #97 already applied, this was not retried a second or third time - the environment gap #97 already opened stays open, and this entry adds no new data point to it beyond confirming it is still present one slice later. What this entry's own reference-fidelity claims rest on: three parallel specialist reviews (`reference-renderer-specialist`, `nv2a-xbox-specialist`, `upstream-fidelity-specialist`), each independently verifying the reference claims and the diff. **Concrete next step, unchanged from #97**: get `tools/xbox-link-check.sh`/`tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh` running clean in a healthy environment first, then complete the real link/sweep/boot chain both #97 and #98 are missing - at this point two consecutive slices have shipped without it, which is worth someone's attention on its own.

**RESOLVED, same session, later - see #97's own matching resolution note for the full root-cause.** Not an environment problem: a real, deterministic bug in `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh`'s own leak-check (`grep`'s `\?` silently literal on this host's `PATH`-resolved `grep.exe`), fixed with `grep -E`. With the fix, `tools/xbox-link-check.sh` runs clean end to end for the tree as it stands after X9-H (both `ref_soft` and `ref_nv2a` isolate 0-leaked; the real link is **0 duplicate, 0 undefined**), closing the build/link gap this entry opened. `nv2a_r_nocullback`'s own default stays disabled - that default was about a live camera-agreement risk (CPU plane test vs. the NV2A's hardware winding cull), which a clean link cannot confirm or refute; it still needs the real A/B walk on hardware or xemu.

**Entry #97 (X9-G, PVS culling) - verification, honestly incomplete, a real gap, not swept under the row above.** `tools/xbox-cc.sh ref/nv2a/r_context.c` compiled clean (one pre-existing, unrelated path-case warning) after every edit in this slice, including after each specialist-review fix below - re-verified four separate times, not once at the start and trusted afterward. The full real-Xbox verification chain (`tools/xbox-sweep.sh`, `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh nv2a`, `tools/xbox-link-check.sh`, `tools/xbox-game-link-check.sh hl --include-client --include-freevgui`) that every prior X9 slice completed and quoted real duplicate/undefined counts from could **not** be completed this session: three separate attempts at `tools/xbox-link-check.sh` (one contaminated by a stray concurrent invocation of the same script, killed; two clean, isolated attempts afterward) each stalled with near-zero CPU growth for several minutes inside the script's own third-party-codec-compile or `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh soft` steps - files this change never touches, and the identical stall shape occurred on a `git stash`-verified **unmodified** tree too, ruling out this diff as the cause. Per this project's own "after two failed attempts, stop and escalate with data, not a fresh hypothesis" rule: this is recorded as an open environment/tooling gap for the next session with a healthy build environment to pick up, not chased into a fourth or fifth retry. The standalone `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh nv2a` self-check ("N objects, 0 leaked") every prior X9 entry also quoted could not be reproduced clean either, on this same tree, for the same reason, on **unmodified pre-X9-G main** too (confirmed via `git stash`) - so this is not a regression this slice introduced, but a real, currently-open gap in what this session could verify about the tree at all, X9-G included. What entry #97's own reference-fidelity claims rest on instead: four parallel specialist reviews (`nv2a-xbox-specialist`, `render-architecture-specialist`, `reference-renderer-specialist`, `upstream-fidelity-specialist`), each independently reading the real reference source and the diff, which found and the entry's own code now fixes three real defects (below); and `tools/codex-review.sh --base main`, run per `AGENTS.md`'s own "Review on every PR" policy once `tools/xbox-link-check.sh` itself stopped being the blocker (its first two invocations this same session returned empty, untrusted results - a Codex usage-limit message the first time, an incomplete-lifecycle turn the second - not a real review either time; the third attempt, after the quota's own stated reset time, returned a genuine `needs-attention` verdict), which found and this entry's code now also fixes three FURTHER real defects, including a correctness bug (a face-count-mismatch fail-open path that logged a warning and then kept computing visibility unsafely anyway) neither this session's own reading nor any of the four specialists caught. Both review passes are a substitute for build verification, not a replacement for it. **Concrete next step for whoever picks this up next**: get `tools/xbox-link-check.sh`/`tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh` running clean in a healthy environment first (it is a precondition for trusting ANY future X9 slice's own claimed numbers, not just this one), then complete the real link/sweep/boot chain entry #97's own numbers are missing.

**RESOLVED, same session, later.** The "stalls" and "leaked" reports above were never an unhealthy environment - they were a real, deterministic bug in `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh` itself (see the divergence ledger row for the `xbox-ref-isolate.sh` fix, and that script's own updated comment), root-caused by isolating it with a byte-level test rather than accepting "flaky" as an explanation: `grep`'s `\?` BRE extension is silently a literal backslash-question-mark on `/c/msys64/usr/bin/grep.exe` specifically (the grep this project's own `PATH` exports resolve to), so every prefix self-check this script ran always reported every symbol as "leaked," regardless of whether the rename actually worked - it always had. Fixed by switching to `grep -E` with a plain `?`. With the fix in place, the full chain this entry could not complete now runs clean end to end: `tools/xbox-link-check.sh`, including both `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh soft` (24 objects, 452 symbols, 0 leaked) and `tools/xbox-ref-isolate.sh nv2a` (5 objects, 99 symbols, 0 leaked), reports **0 duplicate, 0 undefined** - a real, valid link, `.sweep/link-check.exe`, PE stack reserve checked and ok. This closes the "not yet fully link-verified" gap this entry opened for X9-G. Live-boot/hardware confirmation (a real `nv2a_r_nocullback` A/B walk, the frame-time exit criterion, photometric comparison) remains genuinely open and is a separate, later step - this resolution is about the build/link chain specifically, not about hardware behaviour.

**Entry #97, three real findings from specialist review, all fixed before the entry above was written, none left as "noted, not fixed."**
1. **Wrong PVS radius constant (nv2a-xbox-specialist and reference-renderer-specialist, independently, both rounds).** The first version used `mod_local.h`'s `FATPVS_RADIUS` (8.0f) - a real constant, but the **server's** own entity-relevancy radius (`sv_game.c`'s `Mod_FatPVS` call), not the client renderer's. The renderer's own real value is the shared `r_pvs_radius` cvar (`engine/client/dll_int/ref_common.c`, default 0.1 - 80x smaller, `FCVAR_ARCHIVE`, exposed to ref modules via `ENGINE_SHARED_CVAR_LIST`/`DECLARE_ENGINE_SHARED_CVAR_LIST()`, already in scope via `ref_common.h`), the exact value `ref/gl` and `ref/soft` both actually pass. At 8x the correct magnitude, `Mod_FatPVS`'s own split-plane recursion would OR together both children's PVS rows for every splitting plane within 8 world units of the camera - not just the boundary-straddling case the crossing-test already handles - silently inflating the visible set in ordinary GoldSrc-density geometry (doorways, vents, stairwells) and defeating a real fraction of this slice's own cull ratio with no visual symptom to catch it. **Fixed** by using the real, shared `r_pvs_radius` directly - confirmed, not assumed, that `RETRIEVE_ENGINE_SHARED_CVAR_LIST()` (`ref/common/ref_context.c`'s own `GetRefAPI`, which `ref/nv2a` links and whose renamed copy is this module's own real entry point) populates it before `R_Init` ever runs.
2. **Citation error on the marksurface-marking loop, corrected THREE times before it was right.** First attribution: to `R_MarkLeaves` itself - wrong (upstream-fidelity-specialist; `R_MarkLeaves` only stamps `node->visframe` on leaf ancestors, it never touches a marksurface). Second attempt: credited the disused, overview-mode-only `R_DrawTopViewLeaf` instead, and claimed the normal single-player path "does not derive visibility from leaf marksurfaces at all" - also wrong (Codex review, reading `R_RecursiveWorldNode` and `R_CullSurface` in full): `R_RecursiveWorldNode` (`gl_rsurf.c:3457-3496`) IS the normal path, and its own leaf-handling branch (`:3481-3489`) stamps every one of a PVS-visible leaf's own marksurfaces with `visframe = tr.framecount` - the identical step this port's loop performs directly - which its own later node-based `firstsurface`/`numsurfaces` walk then filters by via `R_CullSurface` (`gl_cull.c:70`). Third, corrected version: this function performs the SAME concrete visibility-deciding step the reference's real, active, single-player path performs, just without the node-based iteration on top of it that this module has no use for (it already has a flat per-face table from X9-F, not a node tree to iterate). All three attempts are recorded in the function's own comment and this entry, in order - a second wrong citation is exactly the failure `lessons.md`'s own "'Checked individually' is a claim about the search, not a guarantee about what remains" entry warns about, and this is a live instance of it, not a hypothetical one.
3. **Two missing reference dev-aids, added.** `r_lockpvs` (freeze the PVS table for inspection) had no port equivalent at all (reference-renderer-specialist) - added as `nv2a_r_lockpvs`, same position in the function the reference uses. `RF_DRAW_OVERVIEW`'s omission from the `novis` OR-chain was real but undocumented at this specific call site (it relies on the same "unreachable in this fork's real, networking-disabled, single-player-only configuration" fact `GL_RenderFrame`'s own frustum code already argues elsewhere in this file) - now cited at this call site too, not just assumed obvious from the other one.

**Entry #97, one deliberate, disclosed departure from the reference, not a bug - and a real Codex-caught bug in the FIRST attempt at it.** The reference's own `FCVAR_CHANGED` handling nulls `RI.viewleaf`, which (because `R_MarkLeaves` doesn't recompute `RI.viewleaf` itself - a separate function does, earlier the same frame) forces exactly one fully-unculled frame on ANY `r_novis` toggle, in either direction, then leaves the crossing-test block unreached that frame (`RI.viewleaf` is `NULL`). `nv2a_mark_leaves()` recomputes its own viewleaf unconditionally every call, so nulling it would be immediately overwritten and achieve nothing - the first fix instead reused the SAME `force` flag the genuine crossing-test sets, to force a recompute on a convar toggle. **Real bug, Codex review, medium/0.97:** because `force` also gates the SECOND, merged `R_FatPVS(test, ...)` call, reusing it meant a convar toggle could trigger that merge even though the crossing test never actually ran that call's own `test` point through a genuine "is this really a different, non-solid leaf" check - and if `test` happened to land somewhere with no valid PVS cluster (e.g. solid space), `Mod_FatPVS` fills the buffer with all bits set, silently defeating culling until the next real leaf crossing, exactly when a developer is trying to A/B it. **Fixed** by splitting into two flags: `recompute` (set on `FCVAR_CHANGED`, only ever overrides the same-leaf skip-check) and `force` (set only by the real crossing test, the only thing that gates the merged `R_FatPVS` call - matching the reference's own real behaviour, where the convar-changed path never reaches that call at all). A toggle from on to off now draws a correctly-culled frame immediately, with no stray unculled frame and no risk of a phantom all-visible merge - a real, deliberate, disclosed improvement over the reference's own incidental one-frame quirk, not a reason to reproduce it byte-for-byte.

**Entry #97, two smaller consistency fixes, both cheap.** The new per-face visibility table's own allocation (1 byte/face) is now folded into `nv2a_load_map_geometry()`'s existing `NV2A_MAP_BUDGET_BYTES` ledger (nv2a-xbox-specialist: it was being allocated without ever passing through the one check this file's own comment says exists specifically to catch real memory like this). A one-time-per-map `numsurfaces` parity check (render-architecture-specialist) logs the cross-subsystem face-count invariant the whole index-bridging scheme depends on - but see the next paragraph for a real bug in what the FIRST version of that check did once it logged.

**Entry #97, one more real Codex-caught bug: the parity check logged and then kept going unsafely.** High severity, confidence 0.94. The first version of the `numsurfaces` parity check above printed a warning on mismatch and then fell straight through into computing visibility from marksurface offsets ANYWAY - under the exact condition it had just detected as false (`nv2a_map_faces[i] == world->surfaces[i]`), silently zeroing `nv2a_map_face_visible` entries for faces that might be genuinely visible, the opposite of every other failure mode in this file, which all fail OPEN (draw more than necessary) rather than closed (draw less than necessary, i.e. missing geometry with no visible symptom). **Fixed** by disabling PVS culling for the map entirely and permanently the moment a mismatch is detected: free `nv2a_map_face_visible`, set it `NULL`, and return before any indices are trusted - every subsequent call for that map then hits the function's own top-of-function "no table" guard, and `nv2a_draw_map_geometry()` already treats that exactly like `nv2a_map_faces == NULL`: frustum-only, never a black screen, and now never silently-missing geometry either.

**Entry #97, Codex review ROUND 2, one more real bug, high confidence (0.99): the parity check above only ever ran for the FIRST map.** `nv2a_pvs_parity_checked_for` was reset nowhere - `nv2a_free_map_resources()` (called on every unload, before every new load) cleared `nv2a_viewleaf`/`nv2a_oldviewleaf` but not this. Not hypothetical: the engine keeps the world model in fixed `mod_known[0]` storage across level transitions (`engine/common/model.c`), so a genuinely different SECOND map can hand back the exact same `model_t*` pointer value the first map used - `nv2a_pvs_parity_checked_for != world` would then read "already checked" for every map after the first, silently skipping the one check that exists specifically to catch a baker/loader face-count mismatch, for the rest of that boot session. **Fixed** by resetting `nv2a_pvs_parity_checked_for = NULL` in `nv2a_free_map_resources()`, alongside the two leaf pointers it already resets there. Per `AGENTS.md`'s own "capped at two rounds" review cadence, this is the second and final round for this branch - fixed and not re-reviewed a third time.

**Shape as of X0. Corrected 2026-09-07, three times in the same day — the
first two corrections each introduced a new error while fixing the last
one, found across three separate review rounds.** This time, two facts are
kept deliberately separate rather than added together, because conflating
them is exactly what went wrong twice before.

**Fact one: which files carry `XASH_XBOX` guards, headers included.** Seven
— `engine/client/identification.c`, `engine/common/system.c`,
`engine/common/sys_con.c`, `engine/common/host.c`,
`engine/platform/sdl2/vid_sdl2.c`, `filesystem/sys.c`, and
`engine/platform/platform.h` — 40 lines carrying the macro between them
(`grep -c`; 41 raw token occurrences, because `host.c:1243` names it twice
on a single line).

Corrected 2026-09-07, found in review: 40/41 is every line that mentions
the macro, and that is not the same number as the guards themselves — five
of those forty lines are comment prose that discusses `XASH_XBOX` rather
than testing it (`host.c:1241,1243`; `platform.h:164,261,394`, each a
comment explaining why a nearby `#if` is shaped the way it is). Recounted
by grepping each file for an actual `#if`/`#elif`/`#ifdef`/`#ifndef`
directive line containing the macro: **35** guard directives, not 40. Line
count is still the more useful number for "how much of these files
mentions Xbox at all" — it is what the per-file tallies elsewhere in this
document already use — so both numbers stay, kept apart rather than one
standing in for the other: 40 lines mention the macro, 35 of them are the
guards that actually widen a platform test. Every one of those 35 is a
widened platform test, not a rewrite. `platform.h` is the one that grew
across rounds: the declaration block, then `Platform_Init`/
`Platform_Shutdown`, then `Platform_Input`, then `Platform_NanoSleep`, then
`Sys_DebuggerPresent`'s dispatch of `Platform_DebuggerPresent`, then
`Sys_SetupCrashHandler`/`Sys_RestoreCrashHandler` — the last two rounds
each claimed to have checked exhaustively and each still missed one, which
is its own lesson about how much confidence "checked individually" deserves
after the fact rather than the moment it is written.

**Fact two: the `.c`-file compile count, which `platform.h` is not part of,
because it is not a `.c` file.** `find engine filesystem public common
compat -name '*.c' | wc -l` gives 220, and 186 of those 220 compile. Of the
186: six are the `.c` files from fact one that carry guards; two are new,
`engine/platform/xbox/con_xbox.c` and `net_xbox_log.c` (divergences #12,
#13), the cheap form on the ladder and not edits to anything upstream; the
remaining 178 are upstream `.c` files that needed no edit at all
(178 + 6 + 2 = 186). Of the 34 that do not compile, 26 are files the build
never selects, 6 are the parked networking group, and 2 are the
`platform/posix/` pair that a one-word `engine/wscript:181` change excludes.
None of them is waiting on an `#ifdef` in engine code.

That 178-of-186 ratio is the whole bet behind treating `XASH_XBOX` as a
sub-platform of `XASH_WIN32`, and it is the number to re-measure whenever it
looks like it is slipping. The two prior corrections to this paragraph both
failed by mixing fact one's count (files, including a header, that carry
guards) into fact two's arithmetic (a `.c`-file-only compile count) as if
they were the same denominator. They are not, and this version does not
add them together.

**Divergence #7c: the `Wcon_*` stub question, considered and rejected.**
Moved out of the table row above, 2026-09-07, found in review: the
paragraph below used to sit inside #7c's "Why" cell, after a blank line,
which broke the row out of valid Markdown table syntax — its Form/Merge
risk/Exit cells were still present in the text, but glued onto the end of
a free paragraph rather than parsed as table cells, leaving the row
effectively incomplete to any reader or tool that parses the table
structurally rather than eyeballing the raw text.

Considered and rejected, 2026-09-07, found in review: routing the six
`Wcon_*` sites (`host.c` ×2, `sys_con.c` ×1, `system.c` ×3) through new
stub functions in `con_xbox.c` instead, the way `Xbox_ConPrint`/
`Xbox_Init`/`Xbox_NanoSleep` were. The ladder in `AGENTS.md` ranks a new
file above `#ifdef`, and `con_xbox.c` already exists, so the question is
fair. It does not reduce to the same move, though: `Xbox_ConPrint` etc.
replace a call to a *different-named* function (`Wcon_WinPrint` →
`Xbox_ConPrint`), which upstream's own dispatch pattern
(`Platform_Init`/`Platform_Shutdown`) already made room for — no upstream
line needed editing beyond what an `#elif XASH_XBOX` arm already does
elsewhere in `platform.h`. These six sites call `Wcon_*` by name directly,
with no such dispatch point above them; giving
`Wcon_InitConsoleCommands`/`Wcon_ShowConsole`/`Wcon_DisableInput`/
`Wcon_DestroyConsole` real, empty definitions in `con_xbox.c` under those
exact names would need `con_win.c` excluded from the Xbox file set at the
build-system level, or the two definitions collide at link time — and no
`wscript` Xbox target exists yet to exclude it from (that is explicitly
X1's job, `tools/xbox-sweep.sh`'s own header comment). Building that
exclusion now, for four functions alone, ahead of the rest of X1's build
integration, is a larger and riskier change than the six lines of
`#ifdef` it would replace. Revisit when X1 wires up the real Xbox
`wscript` target — routing these four through `con_xbox.c` then costs
nothing extra, because the exclusion has to exist for the whole file set
at that point regardless.

## Anticipated divergences

What the plan already knows will diverge, so it comes as no surprise. They
move to the table above once written.

| Area | Expected form | Risk | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mod_bmodel.c` / `model.c`: MPAK entry | **None.** | — | **Revised 2026-09-09, twice, after `docs/r3d/mpak-loading-design.md`.** This row used to say "Rewrite of 6 functions under `#ifdef`" (`Mod_LoadVertexes/Edges/SurfEdges/Surfaces/Textures/Lighting`) with the 9 structural loaders untouched. Both halves were wrong: the structural loaders index into `Mod_LoadSurfaces`'s output (`:2552`, `:2601`, `:3532`), so nothing loads without the render-side lumps, and every consumer before X9 — `ref/soft`'s per-frame edge rasterizer, the server's and both renderers' per-style/per-dlight lightmap rebuilds, texture-name lookups in `pm_trace`/`sv_world` — needs exactly the classic lumps the v6 bake dropped. The design's first draft then proposed one `case` in `Mod_LoadModel` plus a `dheader_t` synthesised in memory inside `Mod_LoadBrushModel`; review found that `SV_ValidateMap` → `Mod_TestBmodelLumps` (`sv_game.c:823-853`, `mod_bmodel.c:4323-4398`), `Mod_CheckLump`/`ReadLump`/`SaveLump` and `con_utils.c:93` all read the header **from disk** before any loader runs, so `+map` would have refused the archive first. Final shape: MPAK v7 puts a real `dheader_t` at offset 0 (descriptor at 128), making the archive a BSP30 file that every existing path — validators, `Mod_LoadModel`'s version switch, the unmodified `Mod_LoadBmodelLumps`, and `Mod_ProcessRenderData`'s raw-buffer hand-off to renderers — accepts as-is. The work is entirely in `tools/asset-studio` and `filesystem/` (#24, #25) |
| `ref/nv2a/` | New backend | None | It fits where `ref/soft` fits. `ref_interface_s` is not touched |
| Memory ceiling over `zone.c` | `#ifdef` + new functions | Medium | `zone.c` already has named pools with `file:line` attribution; the cap is what's missing |
| Polymorphic audio | Refactor of `engine/client/sound/` | **High** | The only one of the big pieces upstream did not abstract. Candidate to send upstream |
| Xbox trims (demos, HTTP, voice, networking) | `#ifdef` / build flags | Low | Prefer existing `wscript` flags over new `#ifdef`s |
| `3rdparty/mainui` screens (`fork-plan.md` XM, drafted 2026-09-15) | Owned fork of FWGS `mainui_cpp` on a `resonance3d` branch, the submodule repinned to it (`.gitmodules` `branch = resonance3d`); in-place rewrites of `menus/Main.cpp`, `VideoModes.cpp`, `Gamepad.cpp`/`Controls.cpp`, `Audio.cpp` behind `#if XASH_XBOX`, PC-only screens hidden the same way; one new art file (a captionless pad diagram, TGA under `gfx/shell/`); whatever `tools/xbox-cc.sh` currently auto-patches inside the mainui checkout folds into the branch | Medium - the submodule tracks FWGS `continuous` closely, so upstream `mainui` merges become ours to do; bounded by the guard and by touching only `menus/*.cpp`, never the framework | Precedent: `deps/hlsdk`'s owned `resonance3d` branch (#44) and `sultim-t/xash-rt-menu`'s in-place graphics screen. Framework, registration and pad navigation stay stock (see the narrowed "menu framework" bullet below). Moves to the table above per screen as each is written |

## Things that are NOT divergences (and must not be touched)

Verified in the tree. Recorded here so nobody "fixes" what already works:

- **The renderer is already polymorphic.** `engine/ref_api.h`,
  `REF_API_VERSION 17`, `ref_interface_s` with ~59 pointers, and three
  shipping backends: `ref/gl`, `ref/soft` (17,422 lines — the proof that the
  interface tolerates a radically different backend), `ref/null`.
- **Static linking is already a first-class build mode.** `XASH_STATIC_LIBS`
  in `common/defaults.h:102-105` turns on `XASH_LIB LIB_STATIC` and
  `XASH_INTERNAL_GAMELIBS`, which is already handled in `ref_common.c:620`,
  `filesystem_engine.c:218`, `cl_gameui.c:1013`, `host.c:905` and
  `lib_common.c:133,153`. The option itself is defined in
  `scripts/waifulib/xshlib.py:20` and reaches the engine via
  `engine/wscript:155`. There is also `--single-binary` at
  `engine/wscript:53`.
- **The platform layer already exists**, with 16 backends, among them
  `psvita`, `nswitch` and `dos`. An `engine/platform/xbox/` is just one more.
- **The menu *framework* does not need rewriting** - narrowed 2026-09-15;
  it used to say "the menu". `3rdparty/mainui`'s framework (`ADD_MENU`
  registration, `CMenuFramework`, the cvar-linked widgets, `Utils.h`'s
  pad-to-key navigation, `menus/Gamepad.cpp`, `menus/Gyro.cpp`) is what the
  console ports use, reproduces the classic WON menu, and X6 confirmed it
  navigates with a pad untouched. What IS planned to change, on an owned
  fork, is a handful of the *screens* behind it - PC-shaped Video/Controls/
  Main pages rewritten in place or hidden under `XASH_XBOX` for the console
  (`fork-plan.md` XM); that is the row in the anticipated table above, not
  a contradiction of this one. Reuse the framework; do not rewrite it.
- **The memory pools already exist.** `zone.c` (726 lines):
  `Mem_AllocPool`/`Mem_FreePool`/`Mem_PrintStats`/`Mem_Check`, with
  `__FILE__:__LINE__` attribution and separate pools (`mempool`, `imagepool`,
  `soundpool`).
